# Deliberate divergences from GNU Emacs

## Observable by a package: owned local editor commands

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-OWNED-EDITOR | `execute-extended-command`, `dired`, `find-file` | GNU supplies the complete command/completion, Dired and file policy libraries. | The default workspace uses separate Romacs-owned Lisp orchestration: interactive command-name completion/history and a read-only local directory browser with GNU-style long metadata columns, semantic filename faces and n/p/RET/^/g/q. The C-locale ordinary-file listing matches the GNU reference; locale collation, ACL/security indicators and special-device size columns are not implemented. Control-character filenames remain explicitly escaped on one row. Local UTF-8 files and directories share native visits; remote handlers, listing switches, Dired mutation commands and the full GNU coding/mode lifecycle remain unsupported (file coding: VM2-WORKSPACE-CODING). The pristine GNU Lisp tree is unchanged; GNU-only startup does not install these owned modules, and a `--loadup` session keeps GNU's dired.el. | A useful slim editor path independent of GNU's full diagnostic/startup dependencies. Maintained GUI command tests and private native-keyboard journeys cover the supported slice, not full GNU API parity. | <!-- symbols: execute-extended-command, dired, dired-noselect, find-file, find-file-noselect -->
| VM2-READONLY-ERASE | `erase-buffer` rejection in a narrowed read-only buffer | The pinned GNU implementation widens before reporting `buffer-read-only`, changing the accessible region despite rejection. | The native buffer-level guard runs before widening, preserving text, point, restriction and modification ticks on rejection. Any non-nil `inhibit-read-only` bypasses the buffer-level guard; text-property protection is a separate boundary. | Deliberate failure-state preservation for editor-owned read-only views. Live pinned GNU probes distinguish the widening side effect; maintained text/byte-buffer tests assert the safer contract and typed condition/data identity. | <!-- symbols: erase-buffer, buffer-read-only, inhibit-read-only -->

## Observable by a package: scoped GUI command bootstrap

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-GUI-SIMPLE-SCOPE | GUI GNU loadup | GNU's dumped image normally supplies all of simple.el. | When simple.el stops at an unrelated unsupported declaration, the GUI separately evaluates the exact source forms for the line-motion command dependency group. Existing definitions are never replaced; the original partial-file result and each supplemental source line remain reported. | Decouples C-a/C-e from generic-function bootstrap without native command stand-ins or edits to GNU Lisp; scoped source-loading and original command-loop tests cover it. |

## Observable by a package: columns and minibuffer identity

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-CURRENT-COLUMN | `current-column`, `current-indentation`, `move-to-column`, `indent-to` | GNU accounts for display tables, invisibility and composition, and `current-column` caches its last answer by point and modification count (so a `tab-width` change alone can leave it stale). | Native Unicode text width, tab stops and caret controls are counted without moving point; display-property overrides are not projected, and non-UTF-8 byte text refuses. | Plain-text command-motion slice, not a complete display-table implementation. |
| VM2-MINIBUFFER-PRED | minibufferp | GNU retains every initialized minibuffer and recognizes active enclosing recursive levels. | The predicate recognizes native minibuffer-window buffer identities and current activation. Hidden buffers from enclosing recursive levels are not tracked by this predicate. | Uses the current native window model rather than buffer-name heuristics. |


## Observable by a package: save batch (WIP, not accepted)

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-SAVE-PATH | Save-side path expansion, coding and handlers | GNU dispatches expansion/directory helpers and supports filename coding. | Expansion and its helpers (`directory-file-name`, `file-name-nondirectory` in expand_cp_target) dispatch their handlers in GNU's order, through Lisp where one is selected, then the operation's own handler runs. Non-UTF-8 filename coding and nonrepresentable byte paths remain refused. | The full filename codec is not implemented. `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/filesave/README.md`, `evidence/handler-local/`. |
| VM2-SAVE-RENAME | Rename confirmation and cross-device fallback | GNU may prompt on integer overwrite arguments and implements cross-device copying. | Collision with integer confirmation and EXDEV explicitly refuse without performing copy/delete. Linux atomic no-replace syscall failures propagate; no racy fallback. | Local atomic rename subset. `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/filesave/gnu.json`. |
| VM2-SAVE-LINKS | `copy-file`, `add-name-to-file`, `make-symbolic-link`, `set-file-times`, `file-name-all-completions` | An integer OK-IF-ALREADY-EXISTS that meets an existing name asks `yes-or-no-p` (batch reads stdin). PRESERVE-PERMISSIONS copies extended (POSIX or NFSv4) ACLs; an SELinux context only in a libselinux build, which the pin is not. `file-name-all-completions` decodes entries with `file-name-coding-system`. | An integer proceeds while the name is free and refuses with `VM2 <operation> interactive overwrite confirmation` on a collision, before writing. PRESERVE-PERMISSIONS refuses before creating NEWNAME when the source carries an extended ACL. Listing refuses non-UTF-8 file-name coding and a non-UTF-8 entry that passes the prefix test, and gives `./` and `../` first, then `read_dir` order (both readdir). Handlers dispatch as GNU's; expansion handlers refuse (VM2-SAVE-PATH). | No prompt from a leaf; the Hermes and gnosis callers pass nil or t. Oracle test `file_names_links_and_copies_match_gnu` (`crates/romacs-vm/tests/real_ert_load.rs`) compares values, errors and the resulting files with pinned GNU. | <!-- symbols: copy-file, add-name-to-file, make-symbolic-link, set-file-times, file-name-all-completions -->
| VM2-SAVE-TEMP | Temporary-name entropy | GNU appends six randomized alphanumeric characters without creating a file. | Same shape/noncreation; random sequence is not GNU PRNG sequence equality. | Names are not reservations. `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/filesave/README.md`. |
| VM2-SAVE-LOCK | Native locking protocol and lifecycle | GNU uses symlink interlocks, local PID/boot checks and automatic edit/save lifecycle locking. | `lock-file` and `unlock-file` dispatch their handlers and implement symlink interlocks, stale removal and conflict callbacks; a buffer's first modification locks its truename (`rlisp/vm/insdel.el`), `restore-buffer-modified-p` and `set-buffer-modified-p` lock or unlock it as the state flips, `kill-buffer` unlocks a modified buffer's file, `kill-emacs` unlocks every modified buffer's (unlock_all_files), `write-region` locks LOCKNAME before writing and unlocks it after (but for an auto-save), and a visit unlocks the truename and the name, as GNU does. Where GNU's C calls `Flock_file` or `Funlock_file` directly, Romacs calls `lock-file` or `unlock-file` as Lisp functions, so advice on them applies. Before files.el defines `make-lock-file-name` (the bare regime) a file has no lock name. Some nested callback errors propagate instead of redirecting to unlock error handling. Nonregular lock files refuse, link-less filesystem fallback and cross-host boot databases are absent. The boot field is omitted (GNU-supported legacy identity) because `/proc/stat` differs from GNU gnulib boot time on this host; live local PID locks are conservatively retained regardless of the supplied boot field. Reboot/PID-reuse stale detection is therefore unimplemented and may report a false conflict instead of deleting a live owner. Current login environment supplies identity; subsequent changes may affect it. | Explicit native locking slice, not full lifecycle parity. `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/filelock-save/REPORT.md`. |
| VM2-ACL1 | `file-acl`, `set-file-acl`, `file-selinux-context`, `set-file-selinux-context` | Pinned GNU is built with libacl and without libselinux: `file-acl` is `acl_to_text` of `acl_get_file`'s access ACL (named entries by name, `\t#effective:` where a mask withholds rights), nil for a missing file or an error `acl_errno_valid` rejects; `set-file-acl` is `acl_from_text` then `acl_set_file`, t on success, nil for invalid text or a non-string; a context is `(nil nil nil nil)` and setting one returns nil, each after its handler. | Read and written as the `system.posix_acl_access` attribute (the mode bits when it is absent), transcribing libacl's text forms; contexts as pinned GNU. `acl_from_text` is transcribed for access entries (long and one-letter tags, names or numbers, comma or newline separators, `#` comments); `default:` entries are refused as invalid text, so nil. | `tests/real_ert_load.rs` `fileio_defuns_match_gnu`, against pinned GNU with an ACL made by `setfacl`. | <!-- symbols: file-acl, set-file-acl, file-selinux-context, set-file-selinux-context -->
| VM2-AUTOSAVE1 | `do-auto-save` | `fileio.c:6315` runs `auto-save-hook` safely, writes `auto-save-list-file-name` with stdio, auto-saves each modified buffer (those without a `write-region` handler first) with `auto_saving` set (the visited file's mode, `utf-8-emacs` unless into the visited file, no lock, no fsync), reports an error through `display-warning`, disables a buffer that shrank a lot, and is also run by the command loop after `auto-save-interval` events or `auto-save-timeout` idle seconds (`record_auto_save` resets that count). | The subr's loop is a Lisp transcription (`rlisp/vm/auto-save.el`) over native `romacs--auto-save-1` (auto_save_1), `romacs--auto-save-done`, `romacs--auto-save-failure-time` and `romacs--write-auto-save-list`; `write-region` honours the auto-saving state as GNU's does. The message stack is kept as the current message. Where GNU's C calls `write-region`, `file-modes` or `restore-buffer-modified-p` directly, Romacs calls them as Lisp functions, so advice on them applies. | `tests/real_ert_load.rs` `fileio_defuns_match_gnu` compares an auto-save, its list file and modes with pinned GNU. | <!-- symbols: do-auto-save, set-buffer-auto-saved, clear-buffer-auto-save-failure -->
| VM2-SAVE-CASE | Actual filesystem case sensitivity | `fileio.c:2647` answers from the platform: nil on GNU/Linux, where no `_PC_CASE_*` pathconf exists, without asking the filesystem; t on MS-Windows and Cygwin; macOS asks `pathconf (_PC_CASE_SENSITIVE)`, walking up while it cannot tell. Handlers run first. | Handlers run first. On Linux Romacs measures where GNU assumes nil: from the name's directory (the name itself when it is a directory, else the directory it is in, walking up to the nearest that exists), one `statfs` reads the filesystem type. vfat and msdos (`MSDOS_SUPER_MAGIC` 0x4d44), exFAT (0x2011bab0), the old ntfs driver (0x5346544e, case-insensitive unless mounted `case_sensitive=yes`), HFS (0x4244) and HFS+ (0x482b) answer t; an HFSX volume, which may be case-sensitive, answers t as well. On ext2/3/4 (0xef53), f2fs (0xf2f52010) and tmpfs (0x01021994), one `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS` on the directory, opened `O_DIRECTORY` and `O_NOFOLLOW`, answers t when `FS_CASEFOLD_FL` is set. Every other filesystem answers nil, among them ntfs3 (case-sensitive unless mounted `nocase`, which statfs cannot show), CIFS and SMB2 (the server and mount options decide) and FUSE. Any error answers nil, as GNU does when it cannot tell; nothing signals for an unknown answer, and no directory is listed, so a directory's size never matters. A world build records the walk's entry types (not their times), the filesystem type and the flags as keyed probes, and restore asks them again; its confined thread reads flags through an unconfined thread that makes only that query. A unibyte non-ASCII name (ENCODE_FILE's output, which `set-auto-coding` passes on) names the file its UTF-8 bytes spell; other bytes are refused. Off Linux: t on MS-Windows, else nil (no macOS pathconf). | Deliberate Romacs choice: the answer is measured on the filesystem, not a platform constant. The earlier directory-entry measurement signalled for a directory over 4096 entries and broke startup from a large working, home or temporary directory. Qualified for real only on a casefold tmpfs made in a private user and mount namespace (`filename_case_measures_a_real_casefold_directory`, `crates/romacs-vm/tests/savejourney.rs`); no real vfat, exFAT, NTFS, HFS, ext4 or f2fs casefold mount has been qualified, and those answers rest on the magic numbers and the classifier's unit tests (`savejourney.rs` `case_tests`). Beside a case-sensitive filesystem, and a directory of 4100 entries, answers match pinned GNU (`filename_case_matches_gnu_beside_a_huge_directory`). | <!-- symbols: file-name-case-insensitive-p -->
| VM2-SAVE-DECODE | String coding conversion | GNU performs full codec conversion. | `encode-coding-string` and `decode-coding-string` support nil/no-conversion, UTF-8 and raw-text coding systems, aliases, EOL conversion, simple BOM handling, NOCOPY and last-coding-system-used. GNU ASCII fast paths are retained. Other conversion codecs, coding hooks, BOM-pair detection and buffer destinations explicitly refuse. | This is a bounded string-conversion domain, checked against pinned GNU in `tests/coding_strings.rs`; it does not establish general codec or file-coding parity. | <!-- symbols: encode-coding-string, decode-coding-string -->
| VM2-SAVE-ATTRS | File metadata and symlinks | GNU supports full filename/ID coding and attribute platform semantics. | Numeric and named-ID file-attributes and local symlink reads are implemented; unresolved user/group lookups fall back to numeric IDs. Non-UTF-8 symlink targets refuse; stat error and concurrent-change domains are not fully qualified. | Scoped native blockers; details in `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/savejourney/native-followup.md`. |
| VM2-SAVE-KEYDESC | key-description | GNU formats the complete event/sequence and string-property domain. | Character/string/vector/list formatting, prefix/ESC folding and symbolic event formatting implemented. Compound events explicitly refuse; rich properties and mixed unibyte formatting remain partial. | Needed by real GNU help.el prerequisite, not claimed full parity. `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/savejourney/continue-report.md`. |

## Observable by a package: executable image immutability

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-IMAGE-FREEZE | Object-granularity executable-image rehydration cost; opt-in raw-image freeze | GNU's writable dumped world permits mutation without copying these roots. Live pinned GNU preserves mixed code/data/plist identity, and byte-compiled literals remain mutable. | Executable restoration copies every reachable supported Lisp object (cons/string), including code literals, plists, condition properties and captures, with one identity memo. Cold IR remains mapped and undecoded: generated uninterned symbol slots root the private literal objects for later decoding and moving GC. The yank-menu fixture costs exactly **4 private Lisp objects / 195 bytes**; this excludes native metadata allocations. Bare `Vm::new` and raw data-image attachment remain **0 / 0** independently. The mixed fixture preserves `eq` and makes mutation through the variable visible to its function, while another strand remains unchanged. Executable Lisp objects are not frozen. Explicit raw `ImageBuilder` graphs remain immutable and their mutation is refused. | The retained raw-image freeze is an explicit embedding boundary, not GNU's writable dump semantics. Per-strand heaps share immutable encoded bytes, never mutable Lisp objects or page-level copy-on-write. Copy-on-write semantics are realized eagerly at executable restoration, at the measured **4 objects / 195 bytes** fixture cost rather than GNU's zero-copy writable-dump access. Eager object rehydration and existing symbol roots preserve cross-root and cold-code aliasing without a new GC root class or image-page writes. This is an allocation-cost divergence, not a mutation/identity divergence. Tests: `tests/code_image.rs`, `code_image::image_alias_tests`; oracle: `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/image-freeze-oracle.json`. | <!-- symbols: setcar, setcdr, nreverse, defvar, symbol-plist -->

## Observable by a package: isolates and frozen values

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-ISOLATE | `isolate-spawn`, `isolate-create`, `isolate-send`, `isolate-receive`, `isolate-await`, `isolate-on-done`, `isolate-future-cancel`, `isolate-interrupt`, `isolate-kill`, `freeze`, `frozen-p`, `thaw`, `buffer-snapshot`, `with-snapshot-buffer`, `buffer-snapshot-current-p`, `isolate-retire` | Not in GNU. GNU's concurrency is `make-thread` (cooperative, one global lock); an uncaught error in a thread is stored in `thread-last-error` and `thread-join` returns normally. | Worker VMs are child processes that adopt the spawner's world (`--isolate`), run `(apply FUNCTION ARGS)` on frozen arguments and answer with a frozen result or the error, delivered at wait points (the command loop's idle, `isolate-await`). `isolate-await` re-signals the worker's error with its condition and transportable data, defining an unknown condition with the worker's `error-conditions` ancestry; unfreezable data elements arrive as `(isolate-unfreezable PRINTED)`. A worker's death resolves its futures with `isolate-died`, a kill with `isolate-killed`. The worker's backtrace is not transported (`isolate-error-backtrace` stays nil). `isolate-interrupt` quits CPU-bound Lisp at the engine's safepoints, honouring `inhibit-quit`; a job blocked in a primitive (a `call-process` wait) is not interrupted until it returns, and `isolate-kill` follows after `isolate-interrupt-timeout`. GNU's `make-thread` family is not implemented (void). `buffer-snapshot` freezes a buffer's accessible text with its start, ticks, point, file name, directory, mode, generation and chosen locals; `with-snapshot-buffer` gives a worker a buffer of its own over that text in that mode, with writable properties and read-only text (a change made under `inhibit-read-only` signals when the body returns). `isolate-retire` ends an idle worker with its spawner's bye, without waiting and without a death report. | A package observes these only by calling them. Re-signalling is deliberate: a background job's failure must reach the code that asked for it. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/tests/isolates.rs`, `tests/isolate_interrupt.rs`; design: vm-concurrency note §4. | <!-- symbols: buffer-snapshot, with-snapshot-buffer, isolate-retire, isolate-spawn, isolate-create, isolate-send, isolate-receive, isolate-await, isolate-on-done, isolate-future-cancel, isolate-interrupt, isolate-kill, freeze, frozen-p, thaw, make-thread, thread-join -->
| VM2-FROZEN | Writes to frozen and executable-image objects | GNU raises `(error "Attempt to modify read-only object" OBJ)` for pure-space and constant strings (`CHECK_IMPURE`); it has no user-creatable read-only conses, vectors, records, bool-vectors, char-tables or hash tables. | `freeze` copies a value graph (conses, strings with their text properties, vectors, records, floats, bignums, bool-vectors, char-tables, non-weak hash tables, symbols by name) into an immutable region; every mutator of such an object (`setcar`, `setcdr`, `nreverse`, `nconc`, `sort`, `delq` past the head, `aset`, `fillarray`, `clear-string`, the text-property writers, `puthash`, `remhash`, `clrhash`, `set-char-table-range`, `set-char-table-parent`, `set-char-table-extra-slot`, `modify-category-entry`) signals GNU's read-only error with the object, as do writes to executable-image strings and conses (which signalled a VM2 text before). `copy-sequence` and `thaw` give private copies. Markers, overlays, buffers, processes, windows, frames, closures, subrs, weak tables, obarrays, finalizers, sqlite handles and syntax tables refuse to freeze with `(error "Cannot freeze" OBJ PATH)`. | One refusal for every frozen write, GNU's own error. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/src/frozen_tests.rs` (`every_mutator_refuses_with_gnus_read_only_error`). | <!-- symbols: setcar, setcdr, aset, fillarray, puthash, remhash, clrhash, nreverse, set-char-table-range -->
| VM2-ISOLATE-CLOSURES | Functions sent to a worker | GNU has no boundary: any function object is callable anywhere. | `isolate-spawn` accepts a symbol (the worker calls its own definition, autoloading as needed) or a lambda form, a list the worker evaluates, with free variables supplied by `:bindings`; a closure object signals `(error "Cannot send closure" FN)`. | Closure export (literal identity, symbol relocation, macro and compiler dependencies, captures) is unresolved; design note §3.3, M6. | <!-- symbols: isolate-spawn -->
| VM2-ISOLATE-GLOBALS | What a worker sees of the spawner | n/a (one heap). | A worker starts from the world's globals plus `isolate-inherited-variables` (thawed into worker-owned copies) and a job's `:bindings`; it never sees the spawner's later `setq`s, loaded packages or user init. A pooled worker keeps its own symbol values between jobs of the same `:require` set; a job that changed `features` retires its worker from the pool. | Racket's and Python's contract for isolated interpreters; design §3.5, §7. | <!-- symbols: isolate-inherited-variables -->
| VM2-ISOLATE-DISPLAY | Frame and window queries in a worker | n/a. | A worker has the batch session's initial frame and terminal: `display-graphic-p` is nil, `frame-width` and friends answer as in `emacs --batch`. | A worker owns no display. | <!-- symbols: display-graphic-p -->
| VM2-PROJECT-ISOLATE | `project-find-file`, `project-find-regexp` | Enumerate (`project-files`) and search (`xref-matches-in-files`) synchronously; the session blocks until the prompt or the xref buffer appears. | When a worker of the session's own world stands prepared and idle, the enumeration and the grep run in it; the command returns to the command loop at once and continues, with the identical candidate list or xref matches, when the result arrives: the prompt or xref buffer appears then, and keys typed meanwhile run their commands. Without such a worker the GNU definitions run unchanged and a worker is warmed for the next command once the session idles; `project-find-regexp` warms its worker while the regexp is typed, so its first run normally finds it ready; a worker that will not take the job after all (queue full, process gone) is passed over the same way. The commands never wait for a worker's boot. Without a world (a source boot, batch) nothing is warmed. | The candidate list and the matches are the worker's `project-files` and `xref-matches-in-files` over the same project; only the timing of the prompt differs. The continuation ends as GNU's command does: `xref-show-xrefs` leaves its window selected and `*xref*` at the head of the buffer list; the progress note it shows meanwhile is echoed, not logged. Live journey: `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/isolates_live.py`. | <!-- symbols: project-find-file, project-find-regexp -->
| VM2-FONTIFY-ISOLATE | Font Lock of large buffers (`font-lock-fontify-region` under jit-lock) | Redisplay fontifies each chunk it shows in the session, blocking it while Font Lock runs. | A visited buffer of `romacs-fontify-isolate-size` characters or more in a mode of `romacs-fontify-isolate-modes` (Emacs Lisp, C, C++) is snapshotted (`buffer-snapshot`) once the session idles and fontified in a pooled worker of the session's world, which runs the mode on its own copy and walks it with `jit-lock-function` as redisplay would, from the window's start; the worker streams the resulting text properties block by block. While the snapshot is current (`buffer-snapshot-current-p`: text, mode, generation) and the worker's Font Lock setup has the session's fingerprint (keywords as written, region, extension and syntactic-face functions, managed properties, chunk size, the syntax of characters 0-255), checked again at each use, `font-lock-fontify-region` puts the worker's properties on a covered region instead of fontifying it; anywhere else, and for runs whose values are not plain data (keymaps, functions, nested lists), it fontifies as GNU's. An edit drops the results and snapshots again after `romacs-fontify-isolate-delay` idle seconds; a fingerprint mismatch (a user's hook added keywords or changed the syntax table) leaves the buffer to the session. The worker is retired (`isolate-retire`) once its job is done. The worker sees `romacs-fontify-isolate-variables`, not the user's init (VM2-ISOLATE-GLOBALS). `romacs-fontify-isolate` nil disables it. | The properties are those the same jit-lock walk from the same start gives in the session, apart from cc-mode's whitespace-scan caches (`c-in-sws`, `c-is-sws`), which record what a walk happened to scan (test `a_workers_fontification_is_the_sessions`; live harness `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/fontify_isolate_live.py`); only where the work runs differs, so a key that scrolls into unfontified text costs the properties' application instead of the fontification. cc-mode's order-dependent type discovery is redone in the worker's later passes, as redisplay would. Design: vm-concurrency note §3.6, M3. | <!-- symbols: font-lock-fontify-region, buffer-snapshot -->
| VM2-SESSION-IMAGE | Carrying a session into a new process (`romacs-restart`, `--restore IMAGE`) | desktop.el saves buffers that visit files or have a handler, by file name and position, and savehist the histories; unsaved text is lost, and a restart is two separate steps. | `warm-restart-write` writes a session image: one printed plist (buffers by `warm-restart-buffer-id` with point, mark, mark ring, narrowing, modes, `desktop-locals-to-save`, `hard` newlines and the undo list as data; the window state of each frame; the kill ring, registers, histories and `warm-restart-globals`) beside the raw internal text of every unsaved or non-file buffer, through `romacs--session-image-write` and `-read` (owner-only, synced, renamed into place, each part with a digest that a reader checks before anything is applied). `romacs-restart` writes one, runs `kill-emacs-hook`, starts the next process (this build, or `romacs-restart-command`) with `--restore IMAGE` and exits; the new process restores it after its init, puts the buffer list back in its order in one change (`romacs--lead-buffer-list`), and lays the windows out after the startup hooks. Buffers whose mode has a `desktop-buffer-mode-handlers` entry come back through it, under `warm-restart-passive`, and attach from `warm-restart-activate-functions`. A buffer `warm-restart-blocker-functions` names refuses the image; processes, terminals and network connections end, and are listed with every other thing not carried in the image's `:lost`. | Unsaved text travels and every loss is named (design note: live handoff, stage S1). A restore that cannot bring a carried text back as it was keeps the image and puts the text in a plain buffer. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm-gui/tests/warm_restart.rs`; live journey `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/restart_live.py`. | <!-- symbols: romacs-restart, warm-restart-write, warm-restart-restore-image -->

## Font selection order

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-FONT-SORT | `internal-set-font-selection-order` candidate scoring | GNU `font.c:2144-2166` scores candidate size including fixed-size fonts, DPI and average-width mismatch, with factor-of-two rejection; `xfaces.c:5850-5907` validates, stores and invalidates realized faces. | Exact validation accepts duplicates and returns nil, without changing `face-font-selection-order`. Order is stored in VM display state, published to layout, consumed by resolver ranking, and changed orders invalidate previous face publications and resolver primary/character caches. The `:height` versus other-attribute trade-off is **NOT REPRODUCED**: order is honoured only to the extent current StyleScore expresses it (width/weight/slant; Height has zero distance). | Fontconfig catalog discards non-scalable candidates and retains no size/DPI fields (`romacs-font/src/fontconfig_catalog.rs:279`); VM2 snapshot drops width/weight/slant (`romacs-vm/src/display.rs:631-636` in lane base); layout Face cannot carry them (`romacs-layout-input/src/lib.rs:23-28` in lane base); StyleScore has fixed default priority and no size term (`romacs-font/src/style.rs:148-179` in lane base). Observable font selection can therefore differ. Widening this coordinated DISPLAY substrate is deliberately deferred, not impossible. Live pinned validation witnesses: `crates/romacs-vm/tools/primh/font-selection-specs/internal-set-font-selection-order.json`; cache/ranking assertions: `romacs-font/src/resolver/tests/selection_order_test.rs`. Configured family alternatives are now copied/interned into a traced VM root, published as owned state, and successful setters always invalidate previous face publications and resolver primary/character answers. The scalable resolver still uses its existing FontsetPolicy: consuming the new GNU alternative rows is deferred, not approximated with Rust lowercase (GNU uses case-table uppercase comparison, including final-sigma equivalence and Lisp symbol identity). | <!-- symbols: internal-set-font-selection-order, internal-set-alternative-font-family-alist -->


| VM2-FONT-ALTERNATIVES | Alternative-font setters and inherited intern/casing substrate | GNU family setter copies both list-spine levels, interns strings in the current obarray, stores/returns the converted alist and unconditionally frees realized faces. Registry setter instead calls full current-buffer `downcase`. | Family setter implements shape/error/copy/storage/invalidation semantics; inherited initial-obarray interning loses symbol-name properties and rejects non-UTF8 names. Registry setter remains absent, rather than introducing default-only lowercase. | Live family corpus `font-alternative-specs/internal-set-alternative-font-family-alist.json`: `/0045` and `/0046` VALUE pins (symbol-name properties); `/0047` SIGNAL-VS-VALUE pin (unibyte name). Excluded decimal-bignum probe fails in existing reader before invocation (VM2-READ4); excluded custom-case-table probe requires absent current-case-table/set-case-table substrate. Registry requires GNU casetab.c validation/derived-table regeneration and four buffer case-table roots, plus casefiddle.c special lowercase/string semantics; bounded port stopped before a partial implementation. | <!-- symbols: internal-set-alternative-font-family-alist, internal-set-alternative-font-registry-alist -->

## Native leaf batch boundaries

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-LEAF-SORT | `sort` default comparison, list identity and callback effects | Default comparator is native `value<`; stable timsort writes sorted elements back into the original list cons cells and exposes its callback/mutation order. | Default `value<` compares the runtime's numbers, strings, symbols, lists, vectors, bool-vectors, records, markers and buffers with GNU ordering, mismatch data and depth limits; process and symbol-with-position objects remain unavailable. Explicit comparators and keyword parsing work, with stable small-input natural-run/binary insertion. Inputs of 64 or more use stable insertion sort, with differing callback count/order and complexity; intermediate vector mutation/error effects are not matched. | Partial implementation, not complete GNU sort parity. Maintained value-order tests cover default sorts, mixed numeric precision, raw-byte names, circular lists and depth-limit recovery. Live corpus `tools/primh/evidence/sort.json` and the small-sort probes retain the separate sort limitations. | <!-- symbols: sort, value< -->
| VM2-LEAF-MAPCONCAT | `mapconcat` property preservation and absent bool-vector constructor | Concatenation preserves properties from mapped strings and separators; bool-vectors are valid sequences. | Inherited native concat drops properties (`mapconcat/0021`, GNU bold versus nil). Bool-vector witness stops at missing make-bool-vector (`/0016`). | Shared concatenation/property and representation boundaries; live corpus and exact witnesses in `evidence/leaf-sequences/divergences.json`. Pinned GNU itself aborts when a mapping callback truncates its active list; excluded crashing witness retained in pin-probes.json, not synthesized as a condition. | <!-- symbols: mapconcat, concat, make-bool-vector -->
| VM2-LEAF-DEBUG | The debugger on the signal path, and the command loop's error report | eval.c `signal_or_quit` considers the debugger for an error with `debug-on-signal`, when no handler catches it, or when the catching clause lists `debug` or is C's `error` (the command loop's and top level's); `maybe_call_debugger` then asks `inhibit-debugger`, `debug-on-error` (or `debug-on-quit`), `debug-ignored-errors` and once per input event, and `call_debugger` applies `debugger` to `(error ERROR)`. keyboard.c `cmd_error` passes the error, its macro context and `Vsignaling_function` (the frame below the top one at the signal, past `error`) to `command-error-function`, whose default rings the bell and prints the message to the echo area, *Messages* logging the caller before it | The same rule in `threaded_signal.rs` `debugger_wanted` for every signal, those primitives raise included, with the rest in `romacs--call-debugger-p` (rlisp/vm/prelude.el) and the call in the signal path's own form, so the debugger's frame sits on the signalling one; the command loop's and batch top level's catch-all is `(debug t)` (rlisp/vm/keyboard.el, `crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/batch.rs`), and its report is `command-error-function` with the caller a `handler-bind` reads from the frames (`romacs--note-signaling-function`). Different: (1) a quit is never continued: a debugger returning from one lets it unwind, where GNU resumes code that `maybe_quit` stopped (C-g is no asynchronous quit, VM2-HOSTIN1); (2) batch top level prints no backtrace for each error (top_level_2's `debug-early--handler`, VM2-ERRTEXT1); (3) the caller *Messages* names comes from Romacs's frames, which record every primitive call: where pinned GNU's byte code inlines a primitive (`insert`, `forward-char`, `goto-char` in compiled code) or its native code calls one, GNU names the caller one level further out; a `signal` frame under `error` or `user-error` is skipped, as pinned GNU's native-compiled subr.el records none | **Visible to the user** in *Messages* for (3). `crates/romacs-vm/tests/debugger_entry.rs` (32 cases against pinned GNU batch); `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/command_errors_live.py` against pinned GNU pgtk: command errors' echo, *Messages* and bells, M-: (car 1) and a `debug-on-error` command entering *Backtrace*, q and c | <!-- symbols: debug-on-error, debug-on-signal, debug-on-quit, debug-ignored-errors, debugger, inhibit-debugger, internal-when-entered-debugger, signal-hook-function, command-error-function, command-error-default-function -->
| VM2-USERSIG1 | User signals (SIGUSR1, SIGUSR2) | keyboard.c `handle_user_signal` turns each into a `sigusr1` or `sigusr2` event (kbd_buffer_get_event), which read_char runs through `special-event-map` or returns as input; when the signal's name is `debug-on-event` (`sigusr2` by default) it enters the debugger at the next quit check instead | The GUI and batch binary and the GNU test runner count both signals (`host::catch_user_signals`) and read_char's Lisp half (rlisp/vm/keyboard.el) reads a pending one as its event, before the host's, a special one running its binding. Different: `debug-on-event` is not consulted, so SIGUSR2 is an event like SIGUSR1, and nothing wakes a wait for a signal: a batch read takes one at once, but the GUI, whose own loop waits for host input, takes it when its command loop next reads a key, not while idle. An isolate worker keeps SIGUSR1 as its interrupt (isolate.rs) | **Visible** to a user who sends SIGUSR2 to a hung session expecting the debugger, and to a GUI session sent a user signal while idle, which handles it before its next key. Test: `gnu-tests/runner/tests/real_harness.rs` `real_mode_reads_a_user_signal_as_an_event`, against pinned GNU's `(nil (usr1))` | <!-- symbols: special-event-map, debug-on-event, signal-process -->
| VM2-LEAF-VARDATA | `symbol-value` wrong-type payload in variable probe | `(symbol-value 2)` signals `(wrong-type-argument symbolp 2)`. | Signals `(wrong-type-argument listp 2)`. | Existing variable-access condition-data gap exposed by internal-make-var-non-special/0018; retained VALUE class pin, no invented oracle answer. | <!-- symbols: symbol-value -->

## fns.c sequence access boundary

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-ELT1 | `elt` absent object constructors | `Felt` (fns.c:1853) accepts bool-vectors and rejects records/byte-code objects with `wrong-type-argument sequencep`; `Faref` accepting the latter does not make them arrays for Felt. | Lists, strings, vectors, bool-vectors and existing char-tables work. The two bool-vector missing pins were removed after live GNU replay. Remaining record/byte-compile construction witnesses retain their existing limitations. These are not fake vectors or successful nil results, and the construction errors are NOT claimed to equal GNU's elt errors. | Remaining construction boundaries are recorded in `tools/primh/evidence/elt.json`. Closure rejection itself agrees with GNU when only condition and predicate are inspected. | <!-- symbols: elt -->
| VM2-ELT3 | `elt` circular input printer control | Printer serializes the circular input `(1 2 1 2 . #2)` in the corpus control. | Refuses circular list printing without `print-circle`; actual elt circular access is tested successfully at maximum fixnum. | Existing printer gap, 1 PRINTER witness in `tools/primh/evidence/elt.json`; not an elt semantic alternative. | <!-- symbols: elt -->
| VM2-ELT4 | `elt` on the separate native syntax-table kind | `(elt (standard-syntax-table) 97)` returns a syntax descriptor cons, because GNU syntax tables are char-tables. | Refuses with `(wrong-type-argument sequencep TABLE)` rather than inventing a descriptor. This is NOT GNU-equivalent for that valid input. Ordinary CharTable objects are supported, but VM2's separate regexp-host SyntaxTable is not. | Inherited VM2-SY1 representation boundary; explicit refusal witness in elt corpus. Repair requires the out-of-scope syntax/char-table substrate, not an elt approximation. | <!-- symbols: elt -->

**This file is the ledger. A divergence that is not listed here does not
exist — it is a bug.** Detail lives in the per-crate files linked below; this
page is the one place to look to find out what romacs does differently and
why.

Every entry answers four things, in this order:

1. **What GNU does**, from the implementation in `src/*.c` — not the
   docstrings, which have been wrong here more than once.
2. **What romacs does.**
3. **Can a package observe it?** This is the test that decides whether a
   divergence is acceptable at all.
4. **Why**, in one line.

A divergence that a package can observe is not forbidden; an unrecorded one
is. Unsupported operations should fail loudly unless their row explicitly
records a non-signalling alternative. Existing exceptions include ignored face
`:font`, integer default `match-data` in binary buffers (VM2-MD2), ignored syntax
descriptor flags (VM2-SY1), overlay enumeration/tie order and weak-hash slot reuse
order (H4), and the silent DOCSTRING difference below (VM2-ALIAS2). Other rows explicitly describe changed return values,
selection/precedence, metadata, allocation, or ignored options without a runtime
diagnostic; their disclosure is not a claim that the operation signals. Every
observable difference must be recorded here, whether or not it fails loudly.

---

## Residual interpreted macro calls — KNOWN, PARKED and UNFIXED

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-RESIDUAL-MACRO | Execution-time residual macro recognition | Interpreted execution recognises residual macro calls on every execution, without caching expansions: expansion counts at definition/first/second call are `(0 1 2)` non-eager and `(1 1 1)` eager. See `src/eval.c:3449-3473`, `433-448`, `2763-2787`; original body/call/argument cons identities remain unchanged; weak-reference probes reclaim expansion-created strings and subsequent executions produce distinct closures. | `force_lazy` compiles the whole body at first call, permanently freezing unresolved calls including untaken branches: `(0 1 1)` non-eager. Under eager loading, or after a previously skipped branch, GNU returns 42 where Romacs signals `invalid-function`. Observable, KNOWN, PARKED and UNFIXED. | Reached-call-thunk prototype preserved on `vm2/lazy-macroexpand`, evidence `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/lazy-macroexpand/` on that branch. Rejected on MEASURED cost, not principle: STANDARD WARM 165/6847 → 123/3768 (42 lost files, 3079 lost forms), 165 regressed prefixes, zero gains, 35 real breakages versus one frozen-model expectation, and earlier real-ERT failure. Apparent mule-util 18/18 gain was an artefact of an earlier subr.el form 284 line 3536 preload failure causing non-eager target loading. No fix claimed. | <!-- symbols: load macroexpand -->
| VM2-EVAL-EXPAND1 | When the macro calls `eval` runs expand | GNU's `eval` interprets: each macro call expands when evaluation reaches it, again at each reach (`src/eval.c` eval_sub), inside the handlers around it; `load` expands each top-level form first (`internal-macroexpand-for-load`) | Romacs compiles what `eval` runs. A macro call expands as the form compiles, once, and its expansion is kept. It expands alone: no handler or catch around the compilation sees what it signals or throws. One whose expansion signals or throws is compiled to expand at its reach instead, so the form's own handlers see what the macro raises, and the calls inside a deferred call's expansion defer in turn, and so is a call whose function is not yet defined, which a form run before it may define; a `lambda` with nothing to capture compiles at its first call. Different: a successful expansion runs before the form runs and only once; a failing one runs while compiling and again at its reach, so its side effects happen twice, the first before the form's own (pinned GNU `(handled (body cleanup (caught (error "uw"))))`, Romacs `(handled (cleanup body cleanup (caught (error "uw"))))`); C-g during the compile-time expansion raises what left the expansion (the quit, or what a cleanup replaced it with) at the form's reach, without running the macro again. Whether a quit is still unwinding is one mark per VM, not carried on each escape: a quit expansion compiled into a branch never taken, or a cleanup that catches while a quit unwinds, can make an expansion's escape raise as it left where GNU would expand again, or expand again (asking for another C-g) where GNU would raise it | **Observable** only through a macro's own side effects or the dynamic state it reads while expanding; what an expansion signals, throws or quits reaches GNU's handlers. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/tests/macro_escape.rs` (against pinned GNU), `crates/romacs-vm/tests/quit_async.rs` | <!-- symbols: eval, defmacro, macroexpand -->
| VM2-LAMBDA-LIST-EXPAND | When a `(lambda ...)` list called as a function expands its macros | GNU's `funcall_lambda` interprets the list (`src/eval.c`): its body's macro calls expand at each call, so an expander that reads dynamic state could expand differently from call to call; a closure's body is expanded once, when the closure is made (`cconv`), and compiled code at compile time | Romacs compiles the list once (`Vm::lambda_list_closure`, `crates/romacs-vm/src/source_cache.rs`) and reuses that code at the next call while the list still has the text it was compiled from (conses, strings and vectors compared by contents, other objects by identity) and no function cell has changed since (the world epoch every `defun`, `defmacro`, `defalias` and `fset` advances): a `setcar` into the list, an `aset` into a literal, a redefined macro or a function that became a macro takes effect at the next call. The body's macros, its inner lambdas' too, expand once per definition, as a closure's do; in GNU's dynamic dialect every binding is dynamic, so a special declaration changes nothing the list compiled to. A list larger than 4096 items, or circular, is compiled at each call. Rust ts mode's `rust-ts-mode--fontify-pattern` (a `defalias` of a backquoted list) costs 240 µs per call compiled each time | A macro whose expansion depends on dynamic state, inside a lambda list called repeatedly, expands with the state of its first call. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/tests/lambda_list_call.rs` (against pinned GNU) | <!-- symbols: funcall, apply, run-hooks, defmacro -->

## Ordered ERT measurement

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-LOAD-SURFACE | Remaining evaluator/byte-compiler surface | GNU readevalloop is also used by eval-buffer and eval-region; byte compilation uses its own top-level environment, not runtime sibling evaluation. | Source load now shallow-expands and sequentially evaluates resulting progn children, resolving former VM2-RECORD-EAGER. The public eval_source compiler API still compiles each complete read form; eval-buffer, eval-region and .elc input remain unavailable. The loader uses a 128-expansion/native-stack guard. | Loader repair is not a claim to implement GNU byte compilation or the missing buffer evaluators. GNU source-load and compiled witnesses deliberately differ; see TOPLEVEL-PROGN.md. | <!-- symbols: load eval-buffer eval-region -->
| VM2-MULE-LATE-MACRO | Early compiled push expander | GNU interpreted push can expand macroexp-let2 and gv-letplace once their definitions exist. | Ordered bootstrap compiles GNU subr.el push before those two macros exist; mule-util.el form 6 line 168 later signals invalid-function. Reloading only the unchanged real push definition after the prefix makes all 18 mule-util forms load in a separate diagnostic. | Same early-compilation ordering class as easy-mmode, not cured by the top-level progn boundary; no native Lisp stand-in added. | <!-- symbols: push macroexp-let2 gv-letplace -->
| VM2-PROGN-DIAGNOSTIC | Additional failed-prefix diagnostics | GNU aborts a failed dependency load. | Independently measured ewoc after failed cl-preloaded and help-mode after failed faces retain those extra failed prefixes solely for attribution. A push-definition/subr reload is another independent diagnostic. | None is an accepted bootstrap, STANDARD WARM gain or real-ERT execution; strict adapter retains only VM2-ERT-ORDER. See TOPLEVEL-PROGN.md. |
| VM2-RECORD-SCOPE | Existing unavailable generic predicates | GNU arrayp and sequencep both return nil on records; read reads their printed spelling. | Native arrayp now returns nil on records and make-list supports the record boundary probes. Bare VM2 still has no sequencep or read primitive; the unchanged record corpus honestly retains six MISSING results for those two symbols. | These existing missing APIs are not represented as passing record parity. | <!-- symbols: recordp -->
| VM2-ERT-ORDER | MEASUREMENT REGIME: retained failed bindings prefix | GNU aborts a failed loadup dependency. | Explicit `--bootstrap ordered` retains only bindings.el failure at form 129, line 1225, `(void-function map-keymap)`, then loads easy-mmode after the macro definitions in its prefix. Other failures remain fatal. | Diagnostic progress measurement only, not an accepted bootstrap or STANDARD WARM figure; avoid compiling easy-mmode before bound-and-true-p exists. |

## Filesystem predicate boundary

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-FILE-ACCESS | `file-accessible-directory-p` path domain | Expands buffer-relative names, dispatches matching file handlers and handles native filename bytes. | Checks the actual kernel `PATH/./` accessibility (including directory/search permission and symlinks), but dispatches expansion and its own handler as GNU does; non-Unicode names are refused. `setq default-directory` works in this expander. | Reuses the existing filesystem subset rather than inventing successful answers for unsupported names/handlers; live pinned matrix and mutation proof are in `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/file-accessible-directory.md`. | <!-- symbols: file-accessible-directory-p -->

## VM2 explicit garbage collection

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-GC-STATS | Missing interval pool | `alloc.c:6075–6098` returns nine rows, including `(intervals SIZE USED FREE)`. | `garbage-collect` runs the existing moving collector at its traced engine boundary and returns the other eight rows, in GNU order. No `intervals` row is returned. | `(assq 'intervals (garbage-collect))` is nil: properties use Rust containers, not an interval pool; inventing interval sizes/counts would be false. Pinned GNU shape is recorded in `crates/romacs-vm/tools/primh/evidence/garbage-collect.json`. | <!-- symbols: garbage-collect -->
| VM2-GC-ACCOUNTING | Native heap accounting | GNU counts its own typed allocation pools and vectorlike objects. | Counts describe Romacs storage: cons arena entries/reserve; symbol slots excluding private binding slots and freed slots; string headers and actual string bytes; ordinary vector arena entries and element slots/reserve; float arena entries; both buffer variants. Mixed object-arena reserve is not attributed to strings or floats (their typed free pools are empty). SIZE is the corresponding fixed Romacs storage, excluding variable backing allocations; buffers use the shared object-arena slot size. Non-vector Rust objects are not claimed as GNU pseudovectors. | Numeric results and pseudovector population differ; this is heap accounting, not a GNU heap-size emulator. No startup objects are allocated. | <!-- symbols: garbage-collect -->
| VM2-GC-PACING | Collection pacing units and scale | `consing_threshold` (`alloc.c:5740-5809`) collects once the bytes allocated since the last GC exceed max(`gc-cons-threshold`, 80000) or, for a float `gc-cons-percentage` (default 0.1), that fraction of the live heap's bytes plus half the bytes allocated since; a percentage of 2 or more never collects. | The same rule, read live from both variables, with GNU's defaults (800000, 0.1), in Romacs units: allocation counts heap objects at 16 bytes each (a GNU cons), and the live heap is what a collection traverses, the objects the last collection retained plus the compiled code units (IR ops and lowered instructions) it traced. The percentage is scaled by 17 (`PERCENTAGE_SCALE`), so GNU's 0.1 collects after about 11.3 traced heaps, and the scaled value is clamped to 1.8 (`MAX_SCALED_PERCENTAGE`): any setting from about 0.106 up, gcmh-style 0.2 to 1.0 included, defers to 18 traced heaps, and none reaches GNU's never-collect point. Negative and NaN settings leave the threshold alone. An embedder's `Vm::set_collection_floor` replaces both variables (as GNU's memory-full state replaces them) with the floor rule tests and `--gc-floor` use. | A Romacs collection moves objects and walks compiled code, so it costs more per traced unit than GNU's: unscaled, 0.1 did 65 collections on the census file, +30% instructions over the uninhibited base. Scaled, a config written for GNU gets proportional behaviour: restoring 0.1 after startup is the default, and raising either variable defers collection. The scale is the smallest measured that is not slower than the 60ebf00b base, which never collects inside a load. Frame-pointer gate builds on the development machine, each loading its own worktree's Lisp, on a long nested load (15M allocating iterations in a `require`d file); for each scale: instructions against the base's 121.00G, collections and peak RSS (base 2.09 GB): 15, +0.8%, 14, 0.76 GB; 16, +0.2%, 10, 0.79 GB; 17, −0.5%, 8, 0.85 GB; 18 (and 20, 30 or 45, which the 1.8 clamp makes the same), −0.5%, 7, 1.16 GB; 20 with a 3.0 clamp (a scaled 2 or more never collects proportionally), −0.3%, 3–4, 2.22 GB. Over 12 rotating triples of the base, 15 and 17 (load 2.9–4.0), the means of wall, user and sys time, instructions, collections and peak RSS: base 11.26, 9.95 and 1.20 s, 121.00G, 0, 2.10 GB; 15 11.17, 10.23 and 0.85 s, 121.97G, 14, 0.78 GB; 17 10.83, 9.73 and 1.02 s, 120.37G, 8, 0.85 GB. Paired against the base, 15 is −0.10 s in wall time (standard error 0.10 s) and 17 is −0.43 s (0.11 s), faster in 10 of 12 pairs. At 17 against the base, the `--loadup` bootstrap: −1.7% instructions (64.45G vs 65.57G, 30 collections, as at 15), peak RSS 236–241 MB vs 273–278 MB. The census file: −1.3% (59.29G vs 60.10G, 5 collections), 796–806 MB vs 801–812 MB. GUI per key (`perkey_live`, `--loadup`, 200 lines): self-insert 87.8M vs 90.5M instructions, C-n 297.6M vs 297.0M, C-f 126.4M vs 129.2M. In the long load's collections, sweep and compaction take about 55% of the time, marking about 29% and the compiled-code walk about 9%. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/tests/garbage_collect.rs`, `gc::tests`. | <!-- symbols: gc-cons-threshold gc-cons-percentage garbage-collect -->
| VM2-GC-INHIBITED | Collection inhibition | `alloc.c:6044` returns nil without collecting when collection is inhibited; otherwise GNU may collect inside any Lisp call, its conservative stack scan keeping C locals alive. | Collection runs only where no native frame keeps heap Values in untraced Rust storage (`Vm::may_collect`): every running engine other than the collecting one is suspended (state, code and registers traced), no compiler builder is open, and no watcher notification comes from a native writer that has not rooted its frame. Native frames keep later-used Values in traced storage (a root frame, a binding, a `#:` slot); `crates/romacs-vm/tests/fixtures/reentry-sites.txt` classifies every reentry call site. So `eval`, `load`, `require`, `macroexpand`, autoloads, lazy bodies, `signal-hook-function`, handler-bind callbacks, reader callbacks, `(lambda ...)` list calls and watchers of `setq`, `let` and its unwinding in compiled code collect. Inhibited, and `garbage-collect` returns nil: Lisp a leaf, T2 continuation or legacy primitive reaches without suspending its engine (watchers of `set`, `set-default`, `defvaralias`, `makunbound` and other writes made inside primitives, a `defalias-fset-function`, a face's function `:height`); watchers of variables the loader and other native code bind or set; and macro expansion while a compiler builder is open. | A Rust frame cannot be scanned conservatively: each inhibited region holds Values in locals that have not been audited for a moving collection. The heap grows only within one such call, never across a load, prompt or `eval`. No collection or fake statistics are claimed in those regions. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/tests/gc_reentry.rs` (forced moving collections, `ROMACS_GC_STRESS`), `tests/reentry_sites.rs`. | <!-- symbols: garbage-collect -->
| VM2-GC-FINALIZER | When finalizers run | `make-finalizer` (`alloc.c:3978`) makes an object whose function runs once after the collection that finds it unreachable: `garbage_collect` dooms the unmarked finalizers after the roots and before weak tables (`queue_doomed_finalizers`), keeps them and their functions, and calls each before it returns, with `inhibit-quit` t and a signal logged to *Messages* as `finalizer failed: ...` (`run_finalizers`, `alloc.c:3960`), automatic collections included. Its conservative stack scan may keep a finalizer a C frame still mentions. | The collector dooms finalizers at GNU's point in marking and keeps them, with their functions, until they run (`crates/romacs-vm/src/gc.rs`). `romacs--run-finalizers` (`rlisp/vm/prelude.el`) calls them as `run_finalizers` does, from the engine just after an explicit `garbage-collect` returns its statistics, and before each wait for a command key (`romacs--timer-check-idle`): an automatic collection runs inside an allocation, where no Lisp may run, so its doomed finalizers wait for one of those. A finalizer a register of the running top-level evaluation still holds survives until that register is reused. | Lisp cannot run inside an allocation. Test: `crates/romacs-vm/tests/garbage_collect.rs` (GNU-compared), `finalizer-object-type` in GNU's alloc-tests.el. | <!-- symbols: make-finalizer, garbage-collect -->
| VM2-GC-IDLE | Collection while the command loop is idle | `read_char` (`keyboard.c:2984`) calls `maybe_gc` only after its idle auto-save wait (`auto-save-timeout`, 30 s or more, and only after input since the last auto-save) with no input pending, and that collects only when the automatic trigger is already due. `garbage-collect-maybe` (`alloc.c:6110`) collects once more than 1/FACTOR of the trigger's allocation happened. | The GUI's command loop, on an idle pass where no process output, timer or auto-save ran and no message waits to be shown, asks `romacs--idle-collect` (`rlisp/vm/keyboard.el`): once idle `romacs-gc-idle-delay` seconds (2; nil turns it off) with no input pending and more than 1/`romacs-gc-idle-factor` (4) of the way to an automatic collection (`romacs--collection-share`: the larger of the objects allocated over their trigger, VM2-GC-PACING, and the request code compiled over the request boundary's threshold), the host collects after the idle pass returns. Below that share nothing is scheduled, so a quiet session never wakes for it. `gcs-done` then grows while no command runs. A prompt's key wait runs in Lisp (`romacs--read-char`) and does not collect. Batch sessions have no idle loop and are unchanged. `garbage-collect-maybe` uses the object trigger alone: request code retires only at a request boundary, which Lisp never is. | A Romacs collection traces and moves the whole heap, compiled code included, so each one stalls the session for tens to hundreds of milliseconds, and in a session most are due to request code (a quarter of the installed functions compiled since the last collection), not to `gc-cons-threshold`. Collecting at idle moves those stalls off commands. The host makes the collection outside any request, where request code retires; a collection from Lisp inside the idle pass would keep it. On the general-usage bench replay (94 compared steps, 2.5 s after each key step, development machine under load), collections during commands went from 9 (3.28 s in all, the longest 1.27 s) to 6 (1.47 s, the longest 0.63 s), with 18 more at idle (4.77 s, the longest 0.52 s); median RSS from 994 MB to 784 MB; no step's verdict changed. Tests: `timers::tests::idle_collects_after_its_delay_once_enough_was_allocated` (`crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/timers.rs`), `collection_counters_and_garbage_collect_maybe` (`crates/romacs-vm/tests/garbage_collect.rs`, GNU-compared). | <!-- symbols: garbage-collect-maybe, gcs-done, gc-elapsed -->

## Observable by a package: case-table error routing

| ID | Surface | GNU behavior | VM2 behavior | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-CASETAB-ERROR-ROUTE | Malformed non-nil standard-case-table extra slots during implicit scratch or legacy `with-temp-buffer` creation | Buffer initialization repairs the standard table through `set-standard-case-table`; invalid case-table structure signals `wrong-type-argument case-table-p`. | Normal `get-buffer-create` preserves the typed condition, but legacy implicit/temporary-buffer creation can erase it into a generic error. No successful substitute table is returned. | Existing creation error-routing boundary exposed by new case-table validation; not full casing support. | <!-- symbols: set-standard-case-table current-case-table -->

## VM2 standard case-table startup stage

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-CASETAB1 | Standard table initialization stage | `casetab.c:init_casetab_once` constructs four ASCII-only tables, subsequently enriched by GNU Lisp before the dumped oracle starts. The pinned dumped oracle maps character 192 to 224. | Lazily constructs and independently roots all four ASCII startup tables and the canonical-to-equivalence link. Before the GNU enrichment runs, `(aref (standard-case-table) 192)` is nil, not the dumped oracle's 224. | Preserve the actual pre-Lisp initialization contract rather than copying post-load Unicode data; table printer representation remains VM2-CT1. | <!-- symbols: standard-case-table -->

## VM2 GNU bytecode representation

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-BYTECODE1 | GNU bytecode object predicate substrate | `src/data.c:531` accepts GNU closure objects with string bytecode; `#[0 "\\300\\207" [42] 1]` is one. | `byte-code-function-p` is callable but returns nil for VM2's representable objects; the positive reader witness signals `invalid-read-syntax`. VM2 register-code closures are not GNU bytecode. | GNU bytecode object representation, reader and producers are absent; this is a bounded negative-domain implementation, not bytecode execution support. | <!-- symbols: byte-code-function-p -->
| VM2-BYTECOMP1 | `byte-compile` of a function value | `byte-compile` (`lisp/emacs-lisp/bytecomp.el:2959`) recovers an interpreted function's source through `byte-compile--reify-function` (`(aref fun 0..2)`), compiles it to byte-code and, for a symbol, `fset`s the result; a compiled function is returned with the message `Function %s is already compiled`. bytecomp.el itself byte-compiles seven of its functions when it is loaded from source | Under the loadup bootstrap (`rlisp/romacs-bytecomp.el`, `:around` advice added before bytecomp.el loads) a VM2 closure, or a symbol whose definition is one, is returned unchanged and silently: VM2 compiled it when it was defined, and its closures are opaque (VM2-CLOSEQ1). A `(lambda ...)` list first goes through bytecomp's own front end (`byte-compile-preprocess`'s steps: `macroexpand-all`, then closure conversion as a check, inside GNU's `displaying-byte-compile-warnings`); when that signals, as for `(let 1)` or `(cond 1)`, the result is GNU's diagnostic string and nothing is `fset`. Otherwise its expansion, so each macro expands once, is evaluated in the dialect GNU would compile it in (a symbol's list definition dynamically, an argument in the caller's `lexical-binding`) and a symbol is `fset` to the closure. Other arguments and file compilation (`byte-compile-file`) reach GNU's code unchanged (`tests/bytecomp_function_value.rs`) | **Package-observable:** the value is a VM2 closure, not byte-code: `byte-code-function-p` stays nil (VM2-BYTECODE1), so code that byte-compiles only when that predicate is nil (cc-defs.el:2517, cc-mode.el:490) calls `byte-compile` each time it is evaluated. An error GNU raises only in code generation, past the front end, is not reported for a list, and a VM2 closure is returned even when GNU's recompilation of its source would fail. `(advice-member-p #'romacs-bytecomp--function-value 'byte-compile)` is non-nil | <!-- symbols: byte-compile, byte-code-function-p, interpreted-function-p -->
| VM2-UNIDATA1 | Unicode property table functions | `admin/unidata/unidata-gen.el` byte-compiles its runtime functions into eight generated tables (`international/uni-name.el`, `uni-old-name.el`, `uni-comment.el`, `uni-decomposition.el`, `uni-category.el`, `uni-combining.el`, `uni-bidi.el`, `uni-brackets.el`): extra slots 1-3 of each `char-code-property-table` hold byte-code objects. | Those slots hold the symbols of the same functions in source, `rlisp/romacs-unidata.el`, and each of the eight files first requires that library. `tools/romacs_unidata.py` derives the files from the pinned GNU build's output, replacing only the `#[...]` spans. Every other generated `uni-*.el` file is the pinned build's bytes. Verified on all 1,114,112 code points in pinned GNU (`get-char-code-property` and `char-code-property-description` against stock tables), and on Romacs for 16 properties over U+0000-U+2FFFF. | VM2 runs Lisp from source (VM2-EV5, VM2-BYTECODE1); compiled code inside data files cannot be read. **Package-observable:** `(char-table-extra-slot (unicode-property-table-internal 'name) 1)` is the symbol `romacs-unidata-get-name`, not a byte-code object, and the `romacs-unidata-*` functions are fbound. | <!-- symbols: get-char-code-property, char-code-property-description, unicode-property-table-internal -->

## VM2 buffer-local substrate subset

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-BUFLOCAL1 | Native local-variable inventory | Enumerates every native buffer slot in native order. | Every per-buffer slot plus ordinary locals, in symbol-slot order; details below. | Romacs keeps locals by symbol, not in a native buffer structure. | <!-- symbols: buffer-local-variables, buffer-local-value, local-variable-p -->
| VM2-BUFLOCAL2 | Major-mode reset | Resets all native mode state and invokes variable watchers. | Resets represented fields and preserves modeled visit state; absent subsystems and watchers are not simulated, details below. | Missing case/abbrev/redisplay/watcher substrates are not supplied by this lane. | <!-- symbols: kill-all-local-variables, kill-local-variable -->

- **VM2-BUFLOCAL1 — native local-variable inventories are incomplete.** GNU
  `src/buffer.c:1485` enumerates ordinary locals plus every native buffer slot,
  in buffer-alist/native-slot order. VM2 enumerates ordinary locals and every
  `DEFVAR_PER_BUFFER` slot local in the buffer, in symbol-slot order; a
  package can observe the ordering. `buffer-local-value`/`local-variable-p` for unrepresented native
  buffer fields retain their existing ordinary-variable behavior. Why: this
  lane adds clear-one storage and modeled slots, not the entire GNU buffer
  structure. The live-GNU corpus compares explicit supported projections,
  never equates that corpus with complete inventory parity.
  <!-- symbols: buffer-local-variables, buffer-local-value, local-variable-p -->

- **VM2-BUFLOCAL2 — major-mode reset covers represented state only.** GNU
  `src/buffer.c:1137,3031` resets case tables, abbrev tables, invisibility state,
  redisplay flags and (for `reset`) the entire native buffer state; it also
  notifies variable watchers except for the documented suppressing arguments.
  VM2 runs `change-major-mode-hook`, removes eligible ordinary/forwarded
  locals, preserves permanent hooks and modeled visit metadata, and resets
  major-mode/mode-name, local keymap and syntax table. `reset` additionally
  clears modeled file name/read-only/format and visit timestamp/identity/
  truename metadata. Missing case/abbrev/redisplay state and variable watchers
  are not reset/notified. Packages can observe this. Why: the missing native subsystems are outside
  the buffer-local storage slice, rather than silently claimed as implemented.
  <!-- symbols: kill-all-local-variables, kill-local-variable -->

## VM2 source-position and alias diagnostics

- **VM2-SYMPOS1 — positioned symbols are not represented.** GNU
  `src/data.c:362,840,854,876` provides `symbol-with-pos-p`, `bare-symbol`,
  `symbol-with-pos-pos` and `position-symbol` over `PVEC_SYMBOL_WITH_POS`.
  VM2 lacks that object type and these callable primitives. Packages observe
  `(void-function symbol-with-pos-p)` while expanding GNU `define-inline`
  through macroexp.el. A predicate returning nil unconditionally would conceal
  this missing substrate. Why: full positioned-symbol identity, reader,
  evaluator and GC integration is deferred rather than supplied as a stub.
  <!-- symbols: symbol-with-pos-p, bare-symbol, symbol-with-pos-pos, position-symbol -->

- **VM2-ALIAS-DIAG1 — investigated, no GNU divergence.** A two-hop alias whose
  terminal target is void reports the ORIGINAL alias in GNU and VM2, not the
  missing terminal. Pinned GNU 45b0f7699df returns `(void-function alias-a)`
  for direct, funcall and apply calls through `alias-a -> alias-b ->
  alias-missing`. This can obscure the dependency in diagnostics, but changing
  the Lisp condition would introduce a divergence. Evidence and mutation-tested
  regression: `INLINE-CHAIN.md`, `tests/alias_void_chain.rs` in romacs-vm.
  <!-- symbols: funcall, apply -->
## VM2 replace-match

| # | Mechanism | GNU | VM2 | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-REPLACE1 | `replace-match` buffer/case operations | `search.c:2344` replaces buffer matches through `replace_range` and applies current-buffer case/syntax tables when FIXEDCASE is nil. | String and buffer replacement both infer all-caps/initial casing from the match. A buffer replacement is `romacs--replace-range` (change hooks, undo, markers, match-data adjustment), then `upcase-region` or `upcase-initials-region` over the new text, then point after it. Conversion of initial letters shares VM2-CASE1 pinned titlecase limitations; a casing that expands a character follows VM2-CASE3. VM2 has no intangible text, so point always ends after the replacement. | Buffer and string cases are compared with pinned GNU in `tests/replace_match.rs`. |
| VM2-REPLACE2 | `replace-match` mixed encodings | GNU concat converts unibyte non-ASCII pieces into a multibyte result when required. | Mixed-encoding replacement explicitly refuses copied unibyte non-ASCII pieces when the result is multibyte. | Avoid silently corrupting raw-byte characters or text-property coordinates until conversion is implemented. |
| VM2-REPLACE3 | `replace-match` arity payload | GNU direct-call arity errors name the function as a symbol. | VM2's shared native arity dispatcher names the subr object; condition kind and argument count agree. | Shared dispatch representation remains observable. |
| VM2-REPLACE4 | `replace-match` register capacity | GNU retains allocated unused match registers, including startup search history, and reports nonexistent subexpressions inside that capacity; a buffer replacement's `\N` past that capacity signals invalid replacement text and one inside it inserts nothing. | VM2 uses its retained match-group vector length, so an unused SUBEXP can signal args-out-of-range earlier and its maximum differs, and a buffer replacement's `\N` past the match's groups signals where GNU's capacity may insert nothing. | Match-register allocation history is not modeled; successful groups and explicit nil groups retain their normal semantics. |

GNU pin: `45b0f7699df36b3849edc3bdef37d4cb9e75b9ea`, `src/search.c:2344`.


## VM2 host-name bootstrap subset

- **VM2-SYSNAME1 — `system-name` does not refresh GNU's identity cache.** GNU
  `src/editfns.c:1283` refreshes the kernel host name when `Vsystem_name` is EQ
  to its cached object (`src/sysdep.c:1608`), repairing in-place string edits
  and noticing kernel host-name changes. VM2 materialises its forwarded initial
  value on first access and returns its current binding verbatim, without refresh.
  Packages can observe this with `(let ((before (copy-sequence system-name)))
  (aset system-name 0 88) (equal (system-name) before))`: the pinned live GNU
  returns `t`, this implementation returns `nil` on the measured host.
  Evidence: `crates/romacs-vm/tools/primh/evidence/system-name.json`, case
  `system-name/0010` (VALUE). Why: this lane unblocks the startup call and
  preserves explicit string/non-string/nil overrides; cache state is deferred.

- **VM2-SYSNAME2 — startup host acquisition is Linux/UTF-8-only.** GNU
  `src/sysdep.c:1608` obtains raw kernel bytes via `gethostname` (or `uname`),
  normalizes spaces/tabs, and uses `build_string`'s multibyte detection. VM2
  reads `/proc/sys/kernel/hostname` as UTF-8, normalizes spaces/tabs and exposes
  a unibyte string; absent procfs, read errors or invalid UTF-8 yield the
  explicitly non-signalling fallback `"unknown"`. Non-ASCII hosts can differ
  in string multibyteness; non-Linux or restricted-procfs packages can see the
  fallback rather than their host name. Why: the proven target is the measured
  Linux ASCII host; no new unsafe syscall boundary or platform dependency is
  introduced. Those other host configurations have not been live-oracle tested.

- **VM2-SYSNAME3 — host acquisition is deferred; build-details policy is absent.**
  GNU `init_editfns` initializes host state at startup, including after dump
  reload, and `init_system_name` returns nil when build details are disabled.
  VM2 acquires the host name on first access, not at `Vm::new`; a host-name
  change before that access can therefore affect the initial value. Code images
  do not save forwarded storage, so their fresh VM also acquires the current
  host on first access. No `--no-build-details` host-name policy is implemented;
  packages can see a non-nil name in a reproducible-build regime. Why: preserve
  VM2's allocation-free constructor; build-details CLI policy remains deferred.

- **VM2-SYSNAME4 — buffer-local host-name bindings are unsupported.** GNU permits
  `make-local-variable` / `make-variable-buffer-local` on `system-name`. As a
  forwarded built-in, VM2 instead signals `VM2 cannot make built-in variable
  buffer-local: system-name` (VM2-CVAR2). Global assignment and dynamic
  nil/string/non-string overrides remain supported. Why: localized forwarding
  is not yet implemented; the host-name variable uses the same storage contract
  as its peer built-ins.

## Buffer lifecycle remaining boundaries

- **VM2-BUFLIFE3 — RESOLVED, superseded by VM2-EXCUR1.** `save-excursion` is
  implemented natively (`src/save_excursion.rs`, GNU `src/editfns.c:818`) and
  `src/buffer-tests.el::test-remove-overlays` passes again; the file's floor
  rose 108 to 113 in the same recording. Its unibyte boundary, VM2-EXCUR1,
  is withdrawn.

- **VM2-IFC-HELD1 — the overlays of a buffer `insert-file-contents` is
  deciding are held aside.** GNU makes the empty buffer unibyte while
  `set-auto-coding-function` reads the file's bytes, and its markers stay at
  BEG. VM2's unibyte buffer takes the markers, at BEG (`insert_from_gap_1`
  moves none), and holds the empty multibyte buffer's overlays aside until
  the unwind restores it (`fileio_set_multibyte_flag`). While it is held,
  overlay queries on that buffer signal, and `set-buffer-multibyte t`
  signals `cannot decode buffer while insert-file-contents decides its
  coding`, where GNU succeeds. GNU's
  `set-auto-coding` only reads the buffer, so only a custom
  `set-auto-coding-function` can observe this; the buffer and its markers
  are intact afterwards (`insert_file_contents_refuses_unsupported_decoding_intact`).
  <!-- symbols: insert-file-contents, set-auto-coding-function -->


- **VM2-INDIRECT1 — indirect buffers share only multibyte text.** GNU makes
  an indirect buffer of any buffer and lets `set-buffer-multibyte` convert a
  base buffer's text for all its indirect buffers. VM2 shares a multibyte
  (UTF-8) buffer's text only: `make-indirect-buffer` of a unibyte buffer
  signals `VM2 has no indirect buffer of a unibyte buffer`, and making a base
  buffer unibyte while it has indirect buffers (by `set-buffer-multibyte` or
  `insert-file-contents`'s coding toggle) signals `VM2 cannot make a buffer
  sharing its text unibyte`, before anything changes. In an indirect buffer
  `set-buffer-multibyte` signals GNU's own error. No caller is known to make
  an indirect buffer of unibyte text; comint, org and markdown-ts use
  multibyte ones (`tests/indirect_buffer.rs`).
  <!-- symbols: make-indirect-buffer, set-buffer-multibyte -->

- **VM2-BUFLIFE1 — missing process subsystem.** Pinned GNU
  `45b0f7699df36b3849edc3bdef37d4cb9e75b9ea` runs the default
  `process-kill-buffer-query-function` and returns t when killing a normal
  process-free buffer. VM2 runs that function too, but it signals
  `(void-function get-buffer-process)` after completed `subr.el` loads: VM2 has
  no process object or buffer-process association. Custom query functions and
  kill hooks genuinely run; nil queries veto. Inhibited buffers skip all three
  lifecycle hooks for their lifetime. **Package-observable unresolved
  subsystem**, not an ignored optional argument or a disabled GNU query hook.
- **VM2-BUFLIFE2 — replacement selection.** As GNU's Fkill_buffer, the
  Lisp half (`rlisp/vm/buffer_lifecycle.el`) calls `replace-buffer-in-windows`
  once window.el defines it, then makes `other-buffer`'s choice current in
  place of a killed current buffer, and keeps the buffer when it is its own
  choice (a sole `*scratch*`) or the minibuffer's only fallback. Where those leave the buffer shown or current
  (the bare VM, or `other-buffer` finding none), the native kill takes
  other_buffer_safely's: the first live Unicode buffer not beginning with a
  space and not the minibuffer's, otherwise a new `*scratch*`. VM2 keeps no
  frame buffer list (VM2-FRAME1), so `other-buffer` scans the buffer list
  alone and `buffer-list` of a live frame is the buffer list. **Package-observable** only where GNU's frame buffer list would order
  candidates differently.

## Observable by a package

| ID | Area | GNU behavior | Romacs behavior |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| VM2-SEALED1 | Calls of GNU's byte-op primitives are sealed: `car`, `cdr`, `cons`, `eq`, `memq`, `assq`, `nth`, `not`, `null`, `consp`, `listp`, `symbolp`, `stringp`, `length`, `aref`, `aset`, `setcar`, `setcdr`, `1+`, `1-`, `+`, `-`, `*`, `/`, `%`, `=`, `<`, `>`, `<=`, `>=`, `max`, `min`, `list`, `concat`, `insert`, `nconc`, `nreverse`, `get`, `set`, `fset`, `symbol-value`, `symbol-function`, `point`, `goto-char`, `char-after`, `set-buffer`, `current-buffer`, `equal`, `member`, `elt`, `nthcdr`, `substring`, `upcase`, `downcase`, `string=`, `string<`, the buffer and match primitives of `lisp/emacs-lisp/bytecomp.el:3970-4275` (`byte-defop-compiler` rows with an opcode), at the argument counts those handlers emit the opcode for (`crates/romacs-vm/src/sealed.rs` lists them; `sealed::tests` pins the list to the file). | The byte compiler emits a byte code for such a call (`src/bytecode.c:658` `Bcar`, `:1083` `Bnot`, `:1331` `Bplus`, `:1675` `Bnconc`); byte code never reads the function cell, so *compiled* code ignores `fset`, `defalias`, `advice-add` and `cl-letf` of these names, while the *interpreter* (`eval_sub`) reads the cell and sees them. Cell readers (`funcall` of a variable holding the symbol, `apply`, `mapcar`, `symbol-function`) see them in both; byte-opt folds `(funcall 'car x)` and `(apply #'car (list x))` into the byte code. Pinned GNU 32.0.50 after `(advice-add 'car :around ADV)`: byte-compiled `(car '(1 2))` => `1`, `(funcall f '(1 2))` with `f` = `car` and `(mapcar #'car l)` => advised; interpreted `(car '(1 2))` => `(advised . 1)`. After `(fset '+ (lambda (a b) 'plus-redefined))`: compiled `(+ 8 3)` => `11`, interpreted => `plus-redefined`; `(defalias 'null ...)` leaves compiled `(null nil)` and `(not nil)` at `t`. | Every call of these names compiles to the primitive itself (`Op::Sealed`), with no function-cell guard; `funcall`, `apply`, `mapcar` and `symbol-function` read the cell as in GNU, and `advice-add`/`advice-remove` on a sealed name work for them.
