Romacs has no interpreter: `eval`, `eval-expression`, `ielm` and `load` of source compile, so where GNU's interpreter would see a redefinition of `car`, Romacs behaves as GNU's compiled code. `(funcall 'car x)` is not folded and reads the cell (GNU compiled: the byte code). `(+ x y z)`, and the same three-argument `-`, `*`, `min` and `max`, with no number among the arguments, is `(+ (+ x y) z)`: two byte codes, as `byte-optimize-plus` and its siblings rewrite it (`lisp/emacs-lisp/byte-opt.el:917`). Any other call with an argument count GNU does not compile to a byte code (`(car a b)`, `(+ a b c d)`, `(max)`) is an ordinary call and reads the cell. Rationale: a small set of primitives whose semantics cannot be changed is accepted Elisp practice (`bytecomp.el`'s own open-coding), and the per-call guard it replaces was 9% of `ert-tests.el`'s instructions and 2% of the GUI journey's; measurements in the commit. No `gnu-lisp/`, `rlisp/` or test code redefines a byte-op name except fixtures that probe the redefinition itself (`evidence/text-known-answers.json`, re-recorded from compiled GNU). Tests: `sealed::tests`, `tests/sealed_primitives.rs` (advice, against pinned compiled GNU), `tests/traversal.rs`, `tests/core.rs`. | <!-- symbols: car, cdr, cons, eq, memq, assq, nth, not, null, consp, listp, symbolp, stringp, length, aref, aset, setcar, setcdr, 1+, 1-, +, -, *, /, %, =, <, >, <=, >=, max, min, list, concat, insert, nconc, nreverse, get, set, fset, symbol-value, symbol-function, point, point-min, point-max, buffer-size, goto-char, char-after, set-buffer, current-buffer, equal, member, elt, nthcdr, substring, upcase, downcase, string=, string<, string-equal, string-lessp, match-beginning, match-end, forward-char, forward-line, forward-word, end-of-line, bolp, eolp, bobp, eobp, widen, narrow-to-region, delete-region, buffer-substring, current-column, indent-to, indent-to-column, skip-chars-forward, skip-chars-backward, char-syntax, set-marker, move-marker, rplaca, rplacd, following-char, preceding-char, numberp, integerp, car-safe, cdr-safe, advice-add, advice-remove, cl-letf -->
| VM2-PROFILER1 | profiler.c's CPU and memory profilers: where samples are taken and what they weigh | `SIGPROF` on a `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID` timer interrupts the main thread every SAMPLING-INTERVAL of its CPU time, and the handler records the specpdl backtrace (`get_backtrace`) at that instant, including GNU's `redisplay_internal (C function)` frame during redisplay; a sample inside GC counts in `[Automatic\ GC nil]`. The memory profiler probes `xmalloc`/`lisp_malloc` with the bytes asked for, so a small object counts only when its block is allocated, and after each GC it probes the bytes the collection freed (`alloc.c:6019`) under the caller's backtrace. | A thread (`host::cpu_sampler`) wakes every SAMPLING-INTERVAL of wall time and counts a sample only when the Lisp thread is running then (its `/proc` state is `R`, which also holds while it is runnable but preempted), so no sample falls in a wait; the evaluator charges the count at its next call boundary, the entry or exit of a traced call (`Vm::trace_enter`, `Vm::trace_leave`), to the stack that boundary sees. The backtrace changes only there, so the stack charged is the one that ran. A collection (`Vm::collect`) first charges what came before it, and the ticks it takes are `[Automatic\ GC nil]`. The memory profiler weighs, at the same boundaries, the bytes the heap's arenas grew by (conses, cells, closures, objects with their string and vector payloads), so every allocation counts, in Romacs sizes; reclaimed bytes are not probed. Frames VM2 does not trace are not in any sample: sealed byte-op calls (VM2-SEALED1, as GNU's byte-ops), inlined bodies, and redisplay, which has no pseudo-frame, so its CPU time falls under the Lisp frame that started it or the empty backtrace. Log format, `profiler-max-stack-depth`, `profiler-log-size`, eviction of the lower half into `[Discarded\ Samples nil]` and `function-equal` are GNU's. | A CPU-time timer is not used: under a zero `RLIMIT_RTTIME` hard limit (agent scopes have one) Linux kills a thread whose CPU timer fires, as it kills pinned GNU there. Same report shape, different counts: a call-dense hot path is charged where it runs; work inside a long primitive is charged to the primitive at its exit. Not running costs one flag test per call boundary; running at 10 ms adds a relaxed load per boundary and a thread wake per interval. Tests: `crates/romacs-vm/src/profiler_tests.rs`, GNU's `test/src/profiler-tests.el`. | <!-- symbols: profiler-cpu-start, profiler-cpu-stop, profiler-cpu-running-p, profiler-cpu-log, profiler-memory-start, profiler-memory-stop, profiler-memory-running-p, profiler-memory-log, function-equal, profiler-max-stack-depth, profiler-log-size, profiler-start, profiler-report -->
| VM2-BCALL1 | Every named call compiled by Romacs (all Lisp is compiled: `eval`, `load`, `ielm`) evaluates its arguments, then reads the function cell. | GNU's byte code does the same (`src/bytecode.c:758-829` `Bcall`: `call_args` are on the stack before `call_fun = XBARE_SYMBOL (call_fun)->u.s.function`); GNU's interpreter (`eval_sub`) reads the cell first. Pinned GNU 32.0.50, byte-compiled `(f (progn (fset 'f g) 1) 2)` calls `g`; `(undefined-fn (progn (setq zz 1) 2))` signals `void-function` with `zz` = 1; interpreted, `f` and no assignment. | A redefinition, `advice-add` or `cl-letf` made while an argument runs is seen by that very call, and `void-function` for an unbound head is signalled after the arguments ran. A redefinition made before the call is always seen at the call (the site cache compares the cell's version on every call: `threaded.rs` `direct`, `direct_kernel`, `direct_generic`). One loader-order case is kept: a *top-level* form, or a form deferred to its reach (R215's non-eager regime), whose head has no function when it is compiled (typically a macro from a file not yet loaded) checks the head before its arguments, so a load stopping at `(define-inline …)` reports `(void-function define-inline)` as GNU's loader does, not the void variable its first argument would be; the check runs once per such form and never inside an eagerly compiled function body (there the void variable is reported first, as compiled GNU does). | The compiled order is the one GNU packages are byte-compiled against; it costs one load-compare per call where the interpreter order needed a separate resolution instruction and a traced snapshot register per call (`named-call-1` micro and ERT/census measurements in the commit). Tests: `tests/call_heads.rs` `named_call_reads_the_cell_after_its_arguments`, `tests/oclosure.rs`. | <!-- symbols: fset, defalias, advice-add, cl-letf, void-function, funcall -->
| VM2-FH1 | Cyclic file-name-handler-alist | GNU fileio.c:370 walks cons tails until completion or user quit; a circular outer alist does not terminate by itself. | `find-file-name-handler` matches acyclic alists, operation metadata, inhibition and match-data preservation, but refuses a detected circular outer alist with `(error "VM2 cyclic file-name-handler-alist is unsupported")`. Live GNU observations and regression tests are in `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/file-name-handler/`. |

These change what a package sees. Each is deliberate.

| # | mechanism | GNU | romacs | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-SUBRP1 | `subrp` and callable object representation | GNU `data.c:510` tests `PVEC_SUBR` (`lisp.h:2216`), including unevalled special-form subrs and native-compiled Lisp (`NATIVE_COMP_FUNCTIONP`, `lisp.h:5494`). Live pinned GNU reports `(subrp (symbol-function 'if))` and `(subrp (symbol-function 'get-upcase-table))` as t; the latter also satisfies `native-comp-function-p` and is defined in `lisp/case-table.el:84`, not C. | VM2 returns t exactly for its `Kind::Subr` objects, nil for symbols and compiled Lisp closures. Each special form's function cell holds its subr, with arity `(MIN . unevalled)`, so `(subrp (symbol-function 'if))` is t as in GNU. There is no native-comp object kind or native library loader: the `get-upcase-table` witness returns nil and is pinned VALUE, and `native-comp-available-p` is nil where pinned GNU, built with native compilation, answers t. `byte-compile` is GNU Lisp not loaded in the bare replay, pinned MISSING; compiled-function object introspection remains outside this implementation. | **Package-observable representation gaps**, not a claim that all GNU callable kinds are representable. | VM2-KBD1 | Command loop and keyboard macros: `execute-kbd-macro`, `recursive-edit`, `read-key-sequence`, `read-event`, `read-char`, `read-char-exclusive` | GNU's `command_loop_1`, `read_key_sequence` and `read_char` (`src/keyboard.c`) and `Fexecute_kbd_macro` (`src/macros.c:276`) are C. Different: (1) with no unread event, no macro input, no host keyboard and no live file notification watch, a read signals `(end-of-file "Error reading from stdin")`, thrown out of the innermost recursive edit or minibuffer when there is one, instead of blocking; with SECONDS, or without them while a watch is live, it waits as batch GNU built with file notification waits in kbd_buffer_get_event (`romacs--read-batch-wait`): timers, process output and file notification events run until SECONDS pass, input is requeued or no watch is left, and the terminal is never read (VM2-INOTIFY); (2) no echo, `help-form`, menus or down/drag rewriting; GNU's key translation maps (a pending prefix in any of them keeps the sequence open), unbound upper-case/shift fallback, mouse-area prefix keys, default bindings and remapping are kept, and `input-decode-map` and `local-function-key-map` are global rather than per terminal; (3) `recent-keys` records characters, symbol events and symbol commands only; (4) `read_char` takes `unread-post-input-method-events`, `unread-command-events` and `unread-input-method-events` in GNU's order, but no input method runs: `input-method-function` is never called; (6) no point adjustment after a command, `delayed-warnings-hook` or PRIMARY selection update; macros are defined as macros.c and record_char define them (`start-kbd-macro`, `end-kbd-macro`, `call-last-kbd-macro`, `store-kbd-macro-event`, `cancel-kbd-macro-events`, `discard-input` ending the definition), each command's keys joining the macro once it ran without leaving a prefix argument, so kmacro.el's C-x (, C-x ), C-x e, F3 and F4 work (`harness/command_errors_live.py` against pinned GNU pgtk); (7) a wait for the host keyboard runs one timer_check pass per round and checks requeued input and SECONDS before it blocks, so a timer that reschedules itself at once delays neither a key nor SECONDS; | VM2-READ5 | `read-from-string` error payloads | GNU reading `(a . b c)` signals `(invalid-read-syntax "expected )")`. | VM2 signals `(invalid-read-syntax "dotted list requires one tail at byte 8")`; the same condition symbol does not imply matching condition data. `read-from-string/0170` is explicitly pinned `DATA`. Other reader errors may likewise expose internal messages/byte positions rather than GNU payloads. | **Package-observable staged diagnostic contract:** preserve complete actual fault data in evidence rather than hide payload differences as printer behavior. | <!-- symbols: read-from-string -->
| VM2-MD3 | `set-match-data` negative end extraction | GNU `search.c` retains signed match offsets; after `(set-match-data '(2 -1))`, `(match-string 0 "abcd")` returns `"c"` through substring's negative end normalization. | VM2 stores offsets as unsigned internally; `match-data t` round-trips `(2 -1)`, but extraction rejects the wrapped end and signals `(args-out-of-range "abcd" 2 -1)`. `set-match-data/0507` is pinned `SIGNAL-VS-VALUE`, not a printer gap. | **Package-observable bounded regexp state:** signed match state across every consumer needs separate substrate work; this slice documents safe range rejection rather than claim extraction parity. | <!-- symbols: set-match-data, match-string -->
| VM2-CALLINT1 | `call-interactively`, `funcall-interactively`, `interactive-form`: the supported spec subset and what VM2 lacks around them | GNU `Fcall_interactively` (`callint.c:256-801`) asks `interactive-form` through its symbol (`callint.c:305`), evaluates a non-string spec with `Feval` in the closure's lexical environment (`callint.c:316-319`), records on `command-history` through `add-to-history` (`callint.c:325-330`, `780-783`), handles the prefix characters `+ * - @ ^` (`callint.c:364-405`) and every code letter `a b B c C d D e f F G i k K m M n N p P r R s S U v x X z Z` (`callint.c:449-762`), formatting each prompt with `format-message` (`callint.c:457`). `Finteractive_form` (`data.c:1139-1210`) reads the `interactive-form` property along the alias chain, a subr's DEFUN intspec, `CLOSURE_INTERACTIVE`, autoloads (via `autoload-do-load`) and `(lambda ...)` lists. `funcall-interactively` (`callint.c:239-254`) is `Ffuncall` in a frame that `called-interactively-p` (subr.el) finds by walking the backtrace. | Recorded live against GNU 32.0.50 through primh (`tools/primh/callint-specs/*.json`, `evidence/{call-interactively,funcall-interactively,interactive-form,prefix-numeric-value}.json`, inputs in `tools/primh/logs/callint/make_spec.py`). **Supported and matching GNU row-for-row:** prefix characters `* - ^ @` (`^` calls `handle-shift-selection` when it is fbound; `@` with a window event signals unsupported), `+` error, codes `d e i m p P r R U Z`(no prefix) `N`(with prefix), the reading codes `D f F G` (`read-file-name` through its symbol with callint.c's arguments) and `s` (the `read-string` subr, not its symbol), their prompts through `format-message` (`tests/callint_reading.rs`), invalid-letter error text, `this-command`/`this-original-command`/`real-this-command`/`last-command` save and restore, `current-prefix-arg` (never `prefix-arg`), KEYS vector and `inhibit-mouse-event-check`, RECORD-FLAG history items including `interactive-args`, `history-length` (variable and property), the list-spec path in the closure's own lexical environment, the `interactive-form` property chain, and the GNU intspecs of the VM2 subrs that GNU defines as commands. **Divergent:** (1) the other minibuffer/keyboard-reading codes (`a b B c C k K M n S v x X z`, `N` without and `Z` with a prefix) signals `(error "call-interactively: the input-reading code `X' is not supported in VM2 yet")` where GNU reads input (`call-interactively/0466`, `/0471` pinned `CONDITION`; GNU batch gives `end-of-file`); (2) a `%` in a prompt signals the same kind of unsupported error where GNU formats it (`/0497`, GNU `Not enough arguments for format string`); (3) VM2 has no mark, so `m`, `r` and an active-region `R` always take GNU's no-mark error, and `set-mark` is void (`/0475`); (4) `buffer-read-only`, `inhibit-read-only` are ordinary global specials, not GNU's per-buffer slot, and buffer edits do not enforce them — only `*` reads them; (5) `add-to-history` (subr.el) is void in a bare VM2, so the KEEP-ALL body is run natively, and a non-nil `history-delete-duplicates` signals unsupported; (6) `called-interactively-p`, `command-execute`, `advice-add`, `identity` are void (subr.el/simple.el/nadvice.el not loadable), so those rows are pinned `MISSING`; (7) a closure's spec lives in a private key-weak `eq` table keyed by the closure (VM2 closures have no `CLOSURE_INTERACTIVE` slot), so `(interactive SPEC)` registered by `function`/`lambda`/`defun` is found, but a closure GNU would build from `make-interpreted-closure` with an explicit IFORM is not; `(interactive SPEC . MODES)` drops MODES as `interactive-form` does; (8) quoted `(lambda ...)` lists are read, stored and called as GNU does (`/0439`, `funcall-interactively/0507`, `interactive-form/0110`; VM2-ALIAS1); (9) `funcall-interactively` leaves no frame, so `called-interactively-p` cannot be implemented on it. (10) GNU binds `command-debug-status` to nil around a string-spec command (`callint.c:797-800`); the primitive step protocol (`Step::Done`/`Step::Call`, `src/prim.rs`) has no scoped, unwind-safe binding (the engine's specbind machinery is not exposed to primitives, and a set-and-restore would leak on a non-local exit), so the caller's value shows through (`call-interactively/0540`: GNU nil, VM2 9). Engine-owned substrate work, not infeasible. (11) GNU evaluates ANY non-string spec in the target closure's lexical environment (`callint.c:318-323`); a compiled VM2 closure keeps positional captures with unused bindings pruned, so there is no named environment to evaluate an arbitrary form in; only the closure's own registered spec runs in it (through its thunk) and any other spec (an `interactive-form` property, a redefined `interactive-form`, advice) is evaluated as `(eval SPEC nil)` (`/0541`: GNU 8, VM2 `void-variable x`). Every pinned row of these five evidence files is also locked to its exact reviewed VM2 outcome (`tests/primh_cmdloop_pins.rs`), so a pinned answer that changes within its class fails. Printer-only rows (circular lists, closures, `'X`, killed buffers) are pinned `PRINTER`/`DATA`/`VALUE` under VM2-EV2. | **Package-observable**. A command whose spec reads from the minibuffer, prompts with `%`, uses the mark, or is quoted-lambda old-style signals a VM2-specific error naming the code instead of reading input — loud, never a silent default. Read-only buffers are not enforced by edits. The reading codes need the minibuffer and the mark needs the marker lane; both are separate surfaces | <!-- symbols: call-interactively, funcall-interactively, interactive-form, prefix-numeric-value, command-history, this-command, real-this-command, this-original-command, last-command, current-prefix-arg, inhibit-mouse-event-check -->

| VM2-LP1 | GUI `load-path` | `load-path` grows past `lisp/` at STARTUP, not at dump: `normal-top-level` (`lisp/startup.el:612-638`, reached from loadup.el's last form `(eval top-level t)`) loads `subdirs.el` from every `load-path` entry, `default-directory` bound to it, visiting entries earlier files added. The Lisp function `normal-top-level-add-to-load-path` (`startup.el:472-489`) splices the expanded names right AFTER the file's own directory (after the LAST element if absent). Measured on GNU 32.0.50: `emacs -Q` has 25 entries (`lisp vc use-package url ... calendar calc obsolete`; site-lisp omitted); GNU's own loop and function run on loadup.el's 7-entry `temacs -l loadup` path (`lisp/loadup.el:57-99` splices `emacs-lisp progmodes language international textmodes vc` when `dump-mode` is nil) give 31, the 24 after `lisp` and the 6 again after them; `(locate-library "xterm")` nil, `gnus-dbus` from `lisp/obsolete/` | The workspace launch (`startup::initialize`) sets no `load-path` at all (`startup::runtime_load_path` is used only for its bundled half). Under `--gnu-lisp`/`--loadup` and `--loadup-report`, VM2 runs `loadup.el` (`crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/loadup.rs` `drive`), which can define `normal-top-level` before startup completes; when the ordered subdirs result is absent and startup left the input path unchanged, `loadup::apply_subdirs` does normal-top-level's subdirs loop IN RUST: `update_subdirs` ports the script for a GNU build whose `update-subdirs` ran in the C locale (`build-aux/update-subdirs:24-37` at pin `45b0f7699df`: shell `for file in *`, then prepend except `obsolete`). Recomputation ALWAYS uses C (byte) order, regardless of the original builder's locale; test `update_subdirs_uses_c_locale_byte_order` pins mixed-case/punctuation names (`Zed`, `abc`, `a-c`, `ab`) in reverse byte order. The port uses its own string building (applied only where `lisp/Makefile.in` applies it; a symlinked directory is decided by its target, as GNU's `find -type d` set implies; under that C-locale contract each directory's file is applied as GNU applies it or NOT AT ALL: if a candidate subdirectory name or the directory's path under `lisp/` falls outside ASCII letters, digits and `-_.+,@=~` (or starts with `~`), none of that file is applied and the entry is named in the drive's status line, where GNU adds `/root` for `~root`, `<lisp>/az` for `a\z`, nothing for a file broken by a `"` in a name, the raw bytes of a non-UTF-8 name, and writes no file under a path with whitespace (its Makefile word-splits); the pin has no such name) and `add_to_load_path` ports the splice, including the absent-parent case and the walk over added entries. Result on this tree: 31 entries in GNU's `temacs -l loadup` order (test `loadup_load_path_reaches_gnu_subdirectories_in_gnu_order`; script text byte-compared to GNU's on the pin's directories, `update_subdirs_writes_what_gnus_script_writes`; splice compared to GNU's own run on a fixture, `apply_subdirs_splices_as_gnus_startup_loop_does`). `lisp-bundled/` and its immediate subdirectories (8 entries) follow, 39 in all. Since `default-directory` became per-buffer (VM2-PATH2), GNU's own `normal-top-level` loop runs during the drive and loads the retained `gnu-lisp/subdirs.el` (GNU's script output for the pin's directories, LISP-GENERATED.json), so `apply_subdirs` finds the ordered path established and is not applied (measured in `loadup_load_path_reaches_gnu_subdirectories_in_gnu_order`: drive status "ordered subdirs path is already established", `subdirs.el` in `load-history`, `normal-top-level-add-to-load-path` bound). **NOT GNU's mechanism**: the Rust port stays the fallback that supplies GNU's generated-file application when startup has not established its ordered result. Deeper bundled trees (`lisp-bundled/vendor/{fsm,markdown-mode,websocket}/`) are not on the path. The installed tree is found through `ROMACS_RUNTIME_ROOT` | **Package-observable:** a non-C-locale GNU build can order mixed-case/punctuation directories differently, changing `load-path` precedence without any skipped entry here; the pinned tree's names agree in C and en_US.UTF-8. Through the fallback only, `load-path` is a fresh list, not GNU's `setcdr`-spliced one, and no `subdirs.el` is loaded; the path has 31 GNU entries (the 6 loadup ones twice) like `temacs -l loadup`, not dumped GNU's 25; site-lisp and any `subdirs.el` outside `lisp/` are not consulted. What a package FINDS is GNU's: `(locate-library "org")` is `lisp/org/org.el` and `calendar` resolves (VM2-EV5: source only); `xterm`, bare `analyze` (cedet/semantic/) and `latin-pre` (leim/quail/) are nil as in GNU. Bundled Lisp follows every GNU entry, and test `loadup_load_path_finds_every_gnu_file_before_bundled_lisp` checks every bundled `.el` whose name a GNU path directory also has: `gnus-dbus` is GNU's `lisp/obsolete/gnus-dbus.el` (before vm2/loadpath it was `lisp-bundled/gnus/gnus-dbus.el`, a different file, because `obsolete/` was off the path and the comment claiming a bundled file "can never shadow a GNU file" was false for this path). **`jabber.el` still cannot load by name:** `(locate-library "fsm")` is nil and jabber modules `(require 'fsm)` at top level; `goto-addr` (`lisp/net/`) is now findable. Org still does not LOAD: this makes it findable only. **Why Rust, not solely GNU's function:** it is Lisp in `startup.el`, now partially reached, but generated `subdirs.el` files remain absent | <!-- symbols: load-path, locate-library, normal-top-level-add-to-load-path -->
| VM2-DEPTH1 | Lisp evaluation depth accounting and native stack exhaustion | GNU eval.c:2604-2609,3196-3201 checks the dynamically bound integer limit (default 1600, floor 100 on overflow), signals `(excessive-lisp-nesting DEPTH)`, and restores depth on unwind; bytecode.c:783-788 signals plain `error` at compiled call overflow. loadup.el:106 raises the limit to 4200 and :156 doubles it. eval.c:255-277 restores borrowed reserve from CURRENT limit/reserve values; signal_or_quit borrows 20 (eval.c:1969). Per level at limit 100, measured at the pin: interpreted code reaches 30 `eval`-reentry and 45 call levels, byte-compiled 48 and 96. GNU has no recoverable native-stack condition (a C stack overflow is caught and Emacs returns to top level, `keyboard.c:166-169,13426` "Re-entering top level after C stack overflow") | VM2 consults the forwarded limit at every Lisp activation and shares one budget across eval/load/macro engines; register storage grows and rebases; `eval`/load/lazy-compile/autoload reentry runs on heap stack segments grown on demand (`stacker`), so reentry depth is bounded by the Lisp counter, not by the thread stack. Separately (R193), every activation and every compiler `expr` step refuses when fewer than 256 KiB of native stack remain, with a recoverable `(error "native stack exhausted during Lisp evaluation")` or `... while compiling Lisp` (compiler recursion is refused, not grown). Accounting is GNU bytecode's: 50 and 99 levels at limit 100 (`tests/eval_depth.rs` `reentry_and_call_levels_follow_gnu_bytecode_accounting`). Measured sustainable depth (exactly 4200 and 8400 reached, then `excessive-lisp-nesting`): on the GUI evaluator's explicit 64 MiB stack for plain calls, `apply` and `run-hooks` recursion and `eval` reentry in debug, gate (optimized, no LTO) and release (LTO) builds; on a 2 MiB thread in debug and release for every shape. `signal-hook-function` borrows twenty levels from `lisp-eval-depth-reserve` (default 200), restoring on normal/error/throw exits | **Package-observable:** (1) VM2 always signals `excessive-lisp-nesting`, also where GNU bytecode signals plain `error`; recursion reaches more levels than GNU's interpreter (50 vs 30, 99 vs 45). (2) as GNU's, the debugger borrows 200 levels (VM2-LEAF-DEBUG), and returning `handler-bind` callbacks borrow 200 levels and restore the current limit/reserve after normal or nonlocal exit. (3) In the gap between the native guard and the Lisp limit -- reached only on small threads in optimized non-LTO builds (2 MiB: `apply` recursion refused before depth 4200, `run-hooks` before 8400; 8 MiB: 8400 reached) or with a raised limit (50000 levels on 2 MiB) -- code sees a plain `error` with that message instead of `excessive-lisp-nesting`, and a handler for `excessive-lisp-nesting` alone does not catch it; it is never a process abort. GNU's 4200/8400 loadup raises are effective at the GUI's 64 MiB. See `src/eval_depth.rs` and `tests/eval_depth.rs`. | <!-- symbols: max-lisp-eval-depth, lisp-eval-depth-reserve, excessive-lisp-nesting, signal-hook-function, handler-bind -->

| D002 | `format` of raw high octets | mixes raw bytes into text | explicit handling | no silent mojibake |
| [VM2-TIME1](crates/romacs-vm/GNU-DIVERGENCES.md#vm2-time1--timestamp-bignums-require-the-missing-integer-substrate) | `current-time` and timestamp bignum output | `timefns.c:1817,820,720` returns exact realtime nanoseconds as `(TICKS . 1000000000)` with nil `current-time-list`, promoting TICKS to a bignum as needed; non-nil selects `(HIGH LOW USEC PSEC)` | VM2 `current-time` preserves every native nanosecond in either representation, but nil format signals `(error "VM2 current-time requiring bignum ticks is unsupported")` exactly when TICKS is outside `[-2305843009213693952, 2305843009213693951]`. Non-nil format is exact without bignum ticks; a HIGH outside the same fixnum interval explicitly signals `VM2 current-time requiring bignum HIGH is unsupported` (not reachable with this host's native clock range). Existing `visited-file-modtime` has the analogous tick refusal, and timestamp consumers still do not accept reader bignums | Package-observable numeric-substrate limitation, never float rounding, clamping, frequency reduction or an automatic list fallback. Bind `current-time-list` non-nil to request GNU's lossless list representation. `current-time` is a realtime clock, not a monotonic-clock promise | <!-- symbols: current-time, current-time-list, visited-file-modtime, set-visited-file-modtime -->
| VM2-FMT1 | `format` conversion language and numeric arguments | GNU parses positional `N$`, flags, width and precision and accepts floating and integer/character conversions (`src/editfns.c:3611-3653,3729-3730,3829-3845`). | The shared formatter supports those fields and `%s`, `%S`, `%c`, `%d`, `%i`, `%b`, `%B`, `%o`, `%x`, `%X`, `%e`, `%f`, `%g` and `%%`, including exact bignum integer output and float coercion/rounding. Width, precision and field-number components above 2^20 refuse; GNU string-identity behavior remains unsupported. Text properties follow styled_format: the format string's move to where its characters land, and each `%s` string argument's start at its text (after left padding) with runs ending at its end extended over the field; `%S` of a propertized string prints it as `prin1` does. | Live GNU comparisons are in `tests/format_numeric.rs` and `tests/format_properties.rs`. Locale/quoting capability limits remain VM2-FORMAT-MESSAGE1; this is not full GNU formatting parity. | <!-- symbols: format -->
| VM2-PATH2 | `default-directory` and `expand-file-name` | `default-directory` is a per-buffer C variable, always local and permanent (`src/buffer.c:5426`, index -1): a new buffer copies the current buffer's (`reset_buffer`, `buffer.c:1093`), the first buffer gets the process directory with a trailing slash (`init_buffer`, `buffer.c:4969-5017`), `kill-all-local-variables` keeps it and a `let` of it binds the current buffer's value. `expand-file-name` (`src/fileio.c:994-1765`) reads it when DEFAULT-DIRECTORY is nil, expands a relative or `~` one against `invocation-directory`, falls back to `/`, calls the `expand-file-name` handler of NAME, of the directory and of the result, and keeps a trailing slash, a leading `//`, `..` above the root and an unknown `~user`; every file primitive expands its names through it, and `openp` (`src/lread.c:1756`) expands each PATH element and then, for a still-relative result, against the buffer's directory | VM2 now models the variable as GNU's (`crates/romacs-vm/src/forwarded.rs`, `buffer.rs` `create_named_buffer`) and ports `Fexpand_file_name` as a T2 state machine (`crates/romacs-vm/src/prims/expand_file_name.rs`): each handler call and each recursive expansion is a Lisp call, and the file primitives run the same machine natively, continuing through Lisp `expand-file-name` only where GNU would call a handler (`file-exists-p`, `file-readable-p`, `file-executable-p`, `file-writable-p`, `file-regular-p`, `file-directory-p`, `file-modes`, `set-file-modes`, `set-file-times`, `delete-file`, `delete-file-internal`, `delete-directory-internal`, `make-directory-internal` and `locate-file-internal`; `file-directory-p`, `file-regular-p`, `file-modes` and `delete-directory-internal` also call a `directory-file-name` handler), so a `/:`-quoted directory reaches `file-name-non-special` as in GNU (`executable-find` binds one). Every other file primitive that expands a name (`insert-file-contents`, `write-region`, `copy-file`, `rename-file`, `add-name-to-file`, `make-symbolic-link`, `directory-files`, `directory-files-and-attributes`, `file-attributes`, `file-symlink-p`, `file-accessible-directory-p`, `file-name-all-completions`, `file-name-completion`, `file-name-case-insensitive-p`, `file-system-info`, `file-newer-than-file-p`, `access-file`, `get-file-buffer`, the visited-modtime and lock primitives, `sqlite-open`) does the same, and then calls its own operation's handler as GNU does (`tests/real_ert_load.rs` `file_name_handlers_match_gnu` compares each one's handler calls with pinned GNU's, tramp's completion handler for names such as `/home` among them). Differences that remain: `load` resolves a nil or relative `load-path` element against the process directory, not `default-directory` (measured: `(let ((default-directory D) (load-path '(nil))) (load "f" nil t))` finds `D/f.el` in GNU and signals `file-missing` in VM2); `init_buffer`'s `/:` quoting of a magic process directory never applies because no handler exists when the first buffer is made; a non-ASCII `$HOME` is taken as its bytes, not decoded with the file name coding system; when NAME and the directory are both ASCII but differ in multibyteness the result keeps NAME's, where GNU converts NAME (VM2 reads every string literal as multibyte, so converting would turn ordinary names into unibyte copies no longer EQ to the argument); the first buffer's directory is created decoded (unibyte ASCII, else multibyte UTF-8) because VM2 runs no startup.el to decode `init_buffer`'s unibyte bytes. In the gnu-ert bootstrap regime loaddefs.el is not loaded, so `tramp-mode` is void and `file-name-non-special` signals there; the loadup regime matches GNU (`crates/romacs-vm/tests/real_ert_load.rs` `default_directory_and_expansion_match_gnu`) | **Package-observable, loud:** a handled name (e.g. a `/:` or remote name) given to one of the listed primitives signals instead of being handled; `load` through a nil `load-path` element ignores a bound `default-directory`. Plain expansion, the per-buffer variable and `/:` quoting through the staged primitives match GNU | <!-- symbols: expand-file-name, default-directory, locate-file-internal, file-directory-p -->
| VM2-EXECDIR1 | `exec-directory`, `configure-info-directory` | `configure-info-directory` is the build's `PATH_INFO` (`src/callproc.c:2132`; pinned GNU: `share/info`). `init_callproc_1` (`src/callproc.c:1960`) sets `exec-directory` to the first `EMACSPATH` element or the build's `PATH_EXEC` (pinned GNU: `libexec/emacs/32.0.50/x86_64-pc-linux-gnu/`), where GNU installs its helper programs (`movemail`, `hexl`, `rcs2log`), and appends it to `exec-path` | `exec-directory` is `invocation-directory`, the directory of the running VM2 executable (`crates/romacs-vm/src/forwarded.rs`): Romacs installs no libexec helpers, so their programs are found through `exec-path` alone, which VM2 does not extend. `configure-info-directory` is `info` under the runtime root (`ROMACS_RUNTIME_ROOT`, else the checkout), as `data-directory` is its `etc/` | **Package-observable:** a caller that only probes `exec-directory` (rmail's `movemail` check, vc-rcs's `rcs2log`) finds no file there and falls back to `exec-path`, as GNU does when a helper is not installed; one that runs the helper by that absolute name (`hexlify-buffer` and `dehexlify-buffer` pass `(expand-file-name hexl-program exec-directory)` to `call-process-region`) fails, since Romacs ships no libexec helpers | <!-- symbols: exec-directory, exec-path, configure-info-directory -->
| VM2-TLS1 | TLS client sessions | The pinned GNU links GnuTLS: `gnutls-boot` (`src/gnutls.c:1915`) negotiates on a network process's socket, verifies the peer (gnutls_verify_boot, `:1681`: the chain's status bits, then the host name) and refuses it only as `:verify-error` asks; `gnutls-peer-status` (`:1385`) reports the warnings, each certificate (`emacs_gnutls_certificate_details`) and the key exchange, protocol, cipher and MAC; a `:nowait` process's `:tls-parameters` handshake runs in the wait (`process.c:3794`, `:5458`) before `finish_after_tls_connection`'s NSM check and "open"; `gnutls-available-p` lists GnuTLS's extensions and crypto capabilities and `libgnutls-version` is 30813 | Sessions are rustls's (ring provider) behind the same primitives (`crates/romacs-vm/src/tls.rs`, `prim/leaf/gnutls.rs`, `prims/gnutls.rs`, `rlisp/vm/subprocess.el`): one verifier records webpki's chain verdict as GnuTLS's status bits and the host match, never failing the handshake, so `gnutls-boot`'s errors, initstages (including a refused blocking client keeping stage 9 with its socket plain, as GNU's no-op deinit leaves it), warnings and certificate details (DER-order DN, serial, dates, key ids, PEM) match pinned GNU (`tests/gnutls.rs`); the system bundle is always trusted with `:trustfiles`. Only TLS 1.2 and 1.3 with AEAD suites exist, so a priority string selects versions only (`-VERS-TLS1.x`), `:diffie-hellman-prime-bits` never appears and `:key-exchange` is ECDHE-RSA under TLS 1.3 as GnuTLS names it; `gnutls-anon`, CRL files (accepted, unused), `:min-prime-bits` and `gnutls-format-certificate` are absent; a `:nowait` connection whose peer its `:verify-error` refuses fails, closed and its sentinel told, where GNU's wait discards gnutls_verify_boot's verdict (`process.c:5466`) and opens the socket unencrypted; `gnutls-available-p` answers `(gnutls gnutls3)`, claiming no `gnutls-hash-*`/`gnutls-symmetric-*` crypto, and `libgnutls-version` stays -1 (no libgnutls) | **Package-observable:** network-stream's STARTTLS and `:type tls`, smtpmail, url-http's https, nsm and jabber run on native TLS; package.el now prefers `https://` archives, as GNU with GnuTLS does | <!-- symbols: gnutls-available-p, libgnutls-version, gnutls-boot, gnutls-peer-status, gnutls-get-initstage, gnutls-deinit, gnutls-bye, gnutls-errorp, gnutls-error-fatalp, gnutls-error-string, gnutls-peer-status-warning-describe, gnutls-log-level -->
| VM2-LATE-SPECIAL1 | A `let` compiled before its variable is special | GNU's interpreter decides at each `let` whether it binds dynamically (`Flet`, `src/eval.c:1109`: `declared_special` now), so a function that let-binds a package's variable binds it dynamically once the package's `defvar` has run, however early the function was defined | Romacs compiles a top-level form whole and decides then.
