| VM2-SITELISP1 | Site Lisp directories | GNU adds its configure-time `PATH_SITELOADSEARCH` (`<datadir>/emacs/VERSION/site-lisp` and `<datadir>/emacs/site-lisp`, `src/epaths.in`) to `load-path` unless `--no-site-lisp`/`-Q`; only an Emacs installed under prefix `/usr` sees the distribution's `/usr/share/emacs/site-lisp` (the pinned GNU under its own prefix does not), and GNU loads a package's `.elc` from there | Romacs's runtime has no site-lisp of its own and adds none, as a GNU under another prefix; an init that wants a distribution package (notmuch from the system package) adds `/usr/share/emacs/site-lisp` itself, and Romacs then loads its `.el` sources (VM2-EV5), not the `.elc` another Emacs compiled | A system package's Lisp is one `add-to-list 'load-path` away; its `site-start.el`/`subdirs.el` are not run | <!-- symbols: load-path -->
| VM2-WINCHANGE1 | Window change functions | `redisplay_internal` runs `run_window_change_functions` (`src/window.c:4111`): per frame, what changed since the last run (a window's buffer or sizes, the selected window or frame, a deleted window, `set-frame-window-state-change`) runs `window-buffer-change-functions`, `window-size-change-functions`, `window-selection-change-functions` and `window-state-change-functions` buffer-locally and by default, then `window-configuration-change-hook` (`run_window_configuration_change_hook`, `:3790`) and `window-state-change-hook`, and records the state `window-old-buffer` and its siblings report | Each redisplay's frame snapshot (`Vm::frame_snapshot`) runs `romacs--run-window-change-functions` (`rlisp/vm/window-change.el`, with the command loop's Lisp half) unless redisplay is inhibited and only when a Rust record of the frames' windows (buffers, pixel boxes, parameters, mode and header line presence, the faces' revision and the frame's line and column, the selection, and the collection count) changed since the last run: the same hooks in GNU's order from a per-frame record of each window's buffer and pixel sizes compared with the last run, and Lisp `window-old-buffer`, `window-old-pixel-width`/`-height`, `window-old-body-pixel-width`/`-height`, `old-selected-window`, `old-selected-frame` and `frame-old-selected-window`; `run-window-configuration-change-hook` matches pinned GNU (`tests/window_change.rs`). A frame's change is its windows differing from the record, not GNU's change flag, so a change undone before the redisplay runs nothing, and `set-frame-window-state-change` is absent | notmuch-hello's auto-refresh and other window-change consumers run as in GNU (notmuch_parity_live.py) | <!-- symbols: window-buffer-change-functions, window-size-change-functions, window-selection-change-functions, window-state-change-functions, window-state-change-hook, window-configuration-change-hook, run-window-configuration-change-hook, window-old-buffer -->
| VM2-MAIL1 | Mail | GNU ships message.el, smtpmail and Gnus; notmuch's Emacs package comes with notmuch | Romacs ships no mail interface of its own (`romacs-notmuch.el` and `romacs-mail.el` are gone): the real notmuch package, loaded from the user's init, runs on GNU's message.el and smtpmail with native TLS (VM2-TLS1) and HTML parts through shr (VM2-LIBXML1); `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/notmuch_parity_live.py` drives it side by side with pinned GNU on a synthetic store | A workspace launch (`--no-gnu-lisp`) has no mail commands | <!-- symbols: notmuch, notmuch-hello -->
| VM2-FILLARRAY1 | `fillarray` of a char-table | `Ffillarray` (`src/fns.c:3308`) stores ITEM in the 64 top-level slots and the default, but not in the table's ASCII cache, which `CHAR_TABLE_REF_ASCII` reads for characters 0-127: pinned GNU answers `(1 2)` for `(let ((ct (make-char-table 'foo))) (aset ct ?a 1) (fillarray ct 2) (list (aref ct ?a) (aref ct ?b)))`, and `(make-char-table 'foo 7)` filled with nil still reads 7 for ASCII | VM2 writes the t and nil ranges as `set-char-table-range` does (`crates/romacs-vm/src/prims/str.rs`), so every character, ASCII included, reads ITEM: `(2 2)`; other characters, the default and the parent match GNU (`tests/fillarray_char_table.rs`) | **Package-observable, only through GNU's stale cache:** code that set ASCII entries before filling the table sees the fill, not the old entries | <!-- symbols: fillarray, char-table -->
| VM2-FILEIO1 | `insert-file-contents` and `write-region` subset | `fileio.c:4057` `insert-file-contents` consults file name handlers (`Ffind_file_name_handler`, `file-name-handler-alist`; `-Q` ships jka-compr, epa and tramp entries), decodes through `coding-system-for-read` or detection (`detect_coding`, `set-auto-coding-function`, `file-coding-system-alist`: CRLF/CR EOLs, BOM, latin-1 and every other coding system), supports REPLACE (`t`/`if-regular`), unibyte buffers, devices and FIFOs, runs `before-`/`after-change-functions` for a non-visiting insertion, calls `format-decode` and `after-insert-file-set-coding`, and on VISIT also records modtime, `SAVE_MODIFF`, the file's truename and `buffer-file-coding-system`, and unlocks. `fileio.c:5461` `write-region` likewise consults handlers, runs `write-region-annotate-functions`/`buffer-file-format` annotations and `write-region-post-annotation-function`, chooses and ENCODES through `coding-system-for-write`/`buffer-file-coding-system`/`select-safe-coding-system-function` (EOL conversion included), asks before overwriting when MUSTBENEW is non-nil and not `excl`, locks LOCKNAME, and on VISIT records modtime and `SAVE_MODIFF`. It returns nil; an interactive `Wrote`/`Added to`/`Updated` message is selected by APPEND and suppressed when VISIT is non-nil but neither t nor a string; batch suppresses the message (`fileio.c:5859`) | VM2 (`crates/romacs-vm/src/prims/fileio.rs`, `insert_coding.rs`) reads through GNU's decision and decoding (`insert_coding.rs`, a T2 state machine): `coding-system-for-read` (including `auto-save-coding`), else `set-auto-coding-function` (given the name ENCODE_FILE makes through `file-name-coding-system` or `default-file-name-coding-system`) on the read bytes made the empty buffer's unibyte text with undo off and restored on every exit (decide_coding_unwind), or on the head and tail sample in the ` *code-converting-work*` buffer when the buffer has text, then `file-coding-system-alist` through `find-operation-coding-system` including function entries, CHECK_CODING_SYSTEM with lazy define forms, and raw-text for unibyte buffers; a visited missing file stores `coding-system-for-read` in `buffer-file-coding-system`, and visiting by a for-unibyte coding makes the buffer unibyte. Decoding transcribes coding.c `detect_coding` for undecided types (NUL, 8-bit and EOL scan, `prefer-utf-8`, the inhibit flags, the priority walk with GNU's ISO-2022, UTF-8, UTF-16, charset, Shift-JIS, Big5 and emacs-mule detectors (`latin-extra-code-table` and the ISO categories' safe charsets included; `tests/coding_detection.rs`), `no-conversion` for NUL-only text) and the utf-8-auto category, then the native string decoder (UTF-8, UTF-16, charset and raw-text types, and decoding of Shift-JIS and Big5 as decode_coding_sjis and decode_coding_big5 do) with decode_coding_gap's EOL naming; inserted text keeps the decoder's `charset` properties. After the visit bookkeeping it calls `after-insert-file-set-coding`, then `format-decode` and `after-insert-file-functions` with `inhibit-point-motion-hooks` and `inhibit-modification-hooks` bound and undo off, range-checking each count as `inserted-chars`, and restores or adjusts the undo list as GNU does; `buffer-substring` (refusing a unibyte range that carries text properties) and `save-excursion` work in unibyte buffers for that Lisp. Bootstrap regime: the bare registry sets up no coding category, so detection finds nothing in 8-bit text there and the raw decoder's raw bytes are refused; the GUI workspace defines GNU's UTF-8 and Latin-1 systems (VM2-WORKSPACE-CODING); GNU's UTF-8 reading is recorded in the loadup regime (`real_ert_load.rs`); writing through GNU's `choose_write_coding_system` (`prims/write_coding.rs`, a T2 state machine: `coding-system-for-write` with `coding-system-require-warning`, a local `buffer-file-coding-system`, `file-coding-system-alist` through `find-operation-coding-system` including function entries, the buffer's value, raw-text for unibyte buffers and raw-text-type codings, `select-safe-coding-system-function`, EOL inheritance from the default value, lazy define forms, all inside one save-restriction extent that a nil START widens) and encoding the region or string with the native string encoder (UTF-8, UTF-16, charset, raw-text and undecided types, EOL conversion), with `last-coding-system-used` set to the choice; `buffer-file-coding-system` is GNU's permanent per-buffer C variable. Bootstrap regime: before mule-conf.el defines `raw-text` (the bare VM), syms_of_coding's `no-conversion` stands for it (it encodes byte-identically to raw-text-unix), so a nil choice reports `no-conversion` and naming a system the bare registry lacks, such as `utf-8`, signals `coding-system-error`. The loadup regime does not run `set-locale-environment`, so the default `buffer-file-coding-system` stays nil where GNU batch has the locale's `utf-8-unix`; the write choices recorded against pinned GNU coincide; BEG/END offsets (integers and integral floats), APPEND `t` and numeric offsets, VISIT `t`/string (sets `buffer-file-name`, EQ to an already-expanded argument), `excl`, the `file-missing`/`file-already-exists`/`permission-denied`/`file-error` conditions and data, `buffer-read-only`/`inhibit-read-only` (including the point's inhibit text property, sticky read-only insertion boundaries, selective inhibition lists and category properties), `deactivate-mark`, and visiting a missing file (visit completes, then `file-missing`). Output devices and FIFOs with a reader are supported; `/dev/null` succeeds and `/dev/full` signals `file-error` / `Write error`. `write-region-inhibit-fsync` is a forwarded boolean defaulting to t; nil requests fsync with EINTR retry and EINVAL ignored. A FIFO with no reader fails closed at nonblocking open with ENXIO instead of waiting as GNU does; output remains nonblocking and write/poll fails with ETIMEDOUT after a one-second deadline instead of GNU's C-g-interruptible indefinite write. A failure can leave a partially written destination, but never completes VISIT metadata or reports success; explicit close errors also signal `Write error`. `write-region` returns nil for every supported VISIT mode. REPLACE of a non-empty accessible region follows fileio.c's decoding path (4684-4880): the whole range decoded, only the text between the longest common head and tail (on character boundaries) deleted and replaced as two ordinary changes with their hooks, the insertion's after-change seen with point after the new text, point kept as a marker and spread over the new text when it was inside the replaced text (restore_window_points, bug#19161; window points other than point are not adjusted, and point is restored after a read-only refusal of the insertion but not on a non-local exit from a change hook), read-only text checked only in the ranges it changes, an undo entry recorded for the deletion and for the insertion, a middle holding raw-byte characters refused before anything changes, and a file that is the buffer's head truncating it (`tests/insert_file_contents_hooks.rs` `insert_file_contents_replace_matches_gnu`); GNU's shortcut for codings that need no decoding (4452-4672) reaches the same text but signals the insertion with point before it; VM2 always takes the decoding path. Filesystem open/status errors precede the remaining refusals; MUSTBENEW confirmation on a directory signals `file-error` / `File is a directory`, while `excl` preserves EEXIST. Everything else SIGNALS `(error "VM2 ... is unsupported")` before the buffer or the file changes: detection that reaches a CCL-category system (the one detector VM2 lacks), a decision for a coding type VM2 has no decoder for (ISO-2022 and its EUC variants, emacs-mule, CCL; the refusal names the system chosen), raw bytes for a multibyte buffer (whose text cannot hold eight-bit characters), post-read conversion and decoding translation tables, REPLACE of a visited missing file, non-regular input files, before-change/first-change and text-property modification/insertion hooks on non-visiting reads with a positive requested range (including empty-file reads without explicit END) and after-change hooks on non-visiting nonempty insertion, write annotations, `selective-display` t for a buffer write, a write coding the native encoder lacks (ISO-2022, CCL, SJIS, Big5 and emacs-mule types, translation tables, pre-write conversion, BOM-pair detection; these signal before the file is opened, even for ASCII text that `encode-coding-string` passes through), MUSTBENEW other than `excl` on an existing file. Successful visits and writes now record native nanosecond modtime and byte size. `visited-file-modtime`, `set-visited-file-modtime` and `verify-visited-file-modtime` implement unknown/missing flags, stat-only verification with the size guard, and explicit timestamps disabling the size guard. File name handlers are called as GNU calls them: `insert-file-contents`' handler inserts and GNU's `handled:` tail follows (visit bookkeeping without modtime, `after-insert-file-set-coding`, `format-decode`), `write-region` calls FILENAME's handler, else VISIT's, and records the visit, and the visited-modtime primitives call theirs. `current-time-list` defaults to nil and selects dotted nanosecond ticks versus a four-component list; legacy dotted HIGH/LOW/USEC and ignored extra components are supported, and finite float conversion floors the exact binary value. VM2 timestamp conversion does not consume radix-read bignums, and output ticks beyond fixnum range explicitly refuse (VM2-TIME1). Failed writes retain the old timestamp rather than GNU's post-failure fstat update. Native `MODIFF`/`CHARS_MODIFF`/`SAVE_MODIFF` counters now back `buffer-modified-p`, both modified-state setters and both tick functions; successful VISIT reads/writes establish SAVE_MODIFF. Text, byte, fast-append and ordinary property edits advance counters; inverse edits back to identical bytes stay modified. Internal yank property-only normalization does not yet independently advance MODIFF. Native undo recording/replay is absent; real GNU undo can clear modified through its `(t . visited-modtime)` save marker, rather than text equality. Native visits retain truename and device/inode for validated alias lookup; `get-file-buffer` itself intentionally preserves GNU expanded-path-only matching. NOT modelled and NOT signalled: `insert-file-contents`' `buffer-file-coding-system` bookkeeping. An interactive write (`noninteractive` nil) names its file through `message` as fileio.c:5859 does: `Wrote` (VISIT's file for a VISIT string), `Added to` for APPEND, `Updated` at an offset, nothing for a quiet VISIT or an auto-save; batch is silent and the value is nil. Names expand as `expand-file-name` does, against the current buffer's `default-directory`, through Lisp where a file name handler applies (VM2-PATH2); `write-region` locks and unlocks as GNU does (VM2-SAVE-LOCK). The C variables both DEFUNs read are now VM2 specials with GNU's C values (`forwarded.rs`; `default-directory`, `buffer-file-name`, `buffer-read-only`, `buffer-file-format`, `buffer-file-coding-system` per-buffer); GNU's `-Q` image fills `file-name-handler-alist`, `file-coding-system-alist` and `set-auto-coding-function` from Lisp, VM2 keeps nil. The old byte-buffer-only VM2 `insert-file-contents` (`Primitive::InsertFile`, any arity-1 call, no coding, no errors) is gone. Corpus: `tools/primh/fileio-specs`, recorded from the pinned oracle; every non-PASS row is one of the refusals above or the bare-registry regime, pinned | **User-observable**: input coding refusals and the output deadline remain narrower than GNU. **Package-observable**: `find-file`-style Lisp that relies on detection of non-UTF-8 files or CRLF files fails loudly. Packages can observe narrower timestamp forms, failed-write freshness, undo absence, and internal property ticks. FIFO no-reader ENXIO and the one-second write deadline are observable to both users and Lisp error handlers; GNU waits interruptibly instead. Plain supported UTF-8 visits match GNU bytes; writes choose and encode as pinned GNU does (`crates/romacs-vm/tests/real_ert_load.rs` `write_region_coding_matches_gnu`). | <!-- symbols: insert-file-contents, write-region, visited-file-modtime, set-visited-file-modtime, verify-visited-file-modtime, buffer-file-name, last-coding-system-used, coding-system-for-read, coding-system-for-write, file-name-handler-alist, file-coding-system-alist, select-safe-coding-system-function, buffer-file-coding-system -->
| VM2-FILEIO2 | GUI open and save commands | `romacs FILE` and the desktop entry's `%F` visit FILE with `find-file` (startup.el `command-line-1`, files.el); GNU `find-buffer-visiting` checks expanded name, truename and revalidated file identifier. `C-x C-s` runs `save-buffer`, which makes backups, runs save hooks, checks visited modtime, routes conflicts through `ask-user-about-supersession-threat`, and clears modified state. GNU `kill-buffer` runs query functions, aborts on nil, runs the kill hook, detaches the buffer and repairs windows. Modification state compares SAVE_MODIFF with MODIFF, not text; real undo may explicitly restore clean via saved-modtime undo metadata | While GNU files.el is unavailable the GUI uses explicitly named romacs adapters over native primitives. `romacs-gui-visit-file` uses native exact-name, saved-truename and revalidated device/inode identity, reusing hardlinks, symlinks, equivalent paths and direct Lisp native visits without losing edits. Failed GUI visits are cleared/killed rather than adopted as successful empty buffers. Startup consumes `+LINE[:COLUMN]` and `--`. Save is a no-op when native `buffer-modified-p` is nil; otherwise native stat-only `verify-visited-file-modtime` checks nanosecond time and recorded size, without disk-content reads. Conflicts install future-key y/n/C-g overwrite confirmation: y writes and refreshes metadata; n/C-g preserve both versions. Same modtime/size content changes are deliberately not interrogated, matching GNU. Removed files also prompt here, unlike GNU recreation policy. A dynamic mode-line `*`/`-` reflects native modification counters, including inverse edits to saved bytes. Existing GNU mode-line-format is preserved. In the workspace without GNU Lisp (with it, C-x k is GNU's `kill-buffer`, which asks before killing a modified file buffer), C-x k now truly kills the captured buffer after y (clean buffers immediately), runs native query/hook lifecycle and honors vetoes; dead identities remain buffer objects, leave registries, detach markers/overlays and are replaced in windows. C-x C-c/native close confirm modified GUI visits, including hidden visits. Non-answer/prefix input or buffer switch cancels pending consent instead of GNU modal reprompting; GUI confirmation is an asynchronous adapter, not GNU userlock.el. Native kill lacks process/indirect/autosave/lock/update-hook lifecycle and GNU window-history policy (see crates/romacs-vm/GNU-DIVERGENCES.md). Native alias lookup is named `romacs-find-buffer-visiting`, not full GNU `find-buffer-visiting` predicate/handler support; noncurrent anonymous visits are not enumerated. No native undo record/replay, backups or save hooks; the save reports the write's GNU `Wrote` message. GNU display-column customization/ctl-arrow/C1 controls are not fully implemented for startup positions | **User-observable and package-observable**: ordinary supersession, confirmed/vetoed kill, hardlink reuse and counter-based modified indication are implemented. Packages can call native get-file-buffer, modified-state and kill primitives, but do not acquire GNU save-buffer/find-file/userlock/undo Lisp through these adapters. Exact timestamp, callback-list mutation, omitted kill subsystems and alias lookup limits remain observable. A reverse edit remains modified; a real GNU undo-to-save may become clean and cannot yet run in VM2. FIFO no-reader/deadline behavior remains a deliberate divergence described in VM2-FILEIO1. | <!-- symbols: find-file, save-buffer, get-file-buffer, kill-buffer, buffer-modified-p, buffer-modified-tick, buffer-chars-modified-tick -->
| VM2-GUI-INSTALL1 | GUI adapter command registration | GNU loads command libraries through its Lisp startup/load/require paths; `defun` with `interactive` defines commands, and `define-key` can replace bindings in `current-global-map` (`lisp/subr.el`, `src/keymap.c`) | The romacs workspace and `--loadup` additionally evaluate sorted immediate `.el` files from sibling `lisp-bundled/romacs-gui-extensions` after initialization. `romacs-gui-install-command` accepts a character vector and quoted zero-argument `romacs-gui-` named interactive defun, installs the real function, and defines its key in the map actually used by dispatch. Existing bindings other than the same symbol, missing/non-keymap prefixes, function arguments and interactive argument specifications are rejected. `--gnu-lisp` skips this path | Deliberate temporary adapter, not GNU command loading or find-file parity. New modules need no central startup edits; remove registrations as GNU libraries become usable. See `crates/romacs-vm-gui/COMMAND-MODULES.md` | <!-- symbols: romacs-gui-install-command, current-global-map -->
| VM2-ALIAS1 | `fset`/`defalias` macro descriptors | `fset` publishes a list definition without rejecting conses (`src/data.c:903-916,928-930`); ordinary `defalias` delegates to it (`:993-998,1015-1017`), so `(symbol-function S)` is the stored descriptor itself, `eq` on every read | VM2 `defalias` and `fset` store any other list as given: a `(lambda ...)` list is called as GNU's funcall_lambda calls it, with dynamic binding (`tests/list_functions.rs`), and `(autoload ...)` descriptors load on call. `(macro . EXPANDER)` stores EXPANDER in the function cell and marks the slot as a macro; it does not retain the cons wrapper, so `symbol-function` and `indirect-function` reconstruct a fresh `(macro . EXPANDER)`: copying a definition preserves expansion, but repeated reads are not `eq`. A list function is evaluated to a closure on each call (no cache keyed on a mutable cons) | **Package-observable, rarely:** only code comparing two reads of a macro's `symbol-function` with `eq` sees the difference. Calling a list function costs one compilation per call, which old-style code that stores lists pays | <!-- symbols: fset, defalias, symbol-function, indirect-function, funcall -->
| VM2-SRCCODE1 | Source file coding in `load` | `load` of a `.el` file goes through `load-source-file-function` (`load-with-code-conversion`, `lisp/international/mule.el`): `insert-file-contents` decodes it by the `coding:` cookie or, without one, by `set-auto-coding` and the detection walk over `coding-system-priority-list` (pinned GNU batch: a file of valid UTF-8 is `utf-8`, `caf\xe9` is `iso-latin-1`, `x\x85y` is `japanese-shift-jis`, and `leim/quail/Punct.el`, whose comment holds two stray GB2312 bytes, is `raw-text`, so its strings read as unibyte bytes). GNU's `utf-8` and `utf-8-emacs` share `decode_coding_utf_8` (`src/coding.c:1270`): characters beyond Unicode up to MAX_CHAR decode (`quick-cns.el`'s U+114019), and a byte that starts no character is a raw-byte character | The loader decodes source itself (`crates/romacs-vm/src/loader.rs` `decode_source`), from a cookie in the first two lines: `utf-8` and `utf-8-emacs` through a transcription of `decode_coding_utf_8`, Latin-1, US-ASCII, and the `-unix`/`-dos`/`-mac` EOL variants; any other named system is refused. Without a cookie, bytes that decode as UTF-8 with no raw byte are UTF-8 (GNU's walk finds UTF-8 first); any other file is decided by the same detection walk `insert-file-contents` uses (`prim/leaf/coding.rs` `coding_detect`, VM2-FILEIO1) and decoded by the chosen system, so Latin-1, raw-text (`leim/quail/Punct.el`) and Shift-JIS sources read as in GNU (`tests/source_detection.rs`); a system VM2 cannot decode (ISO-2022, emacs-mule) refuses, and the bare registry, where no category has a system, decides raw-text, which only mule-conf.el defines. Raw-byte and beyond-Unicode characters keep their codes in symbol names as in strings: VM2's Rust-side symbol keys carry them through an injective escape (`crates/romacs-vm/src/symkey.rs`) that `symbol-name`, `intern`, `intern-soft`, `unintern`, `make-symbol`, the printer and `format` decode (a symbol argument is looked up by its decoded name; the printer escapes every piece of Lisp text it copies, so its output decodes only symbol escapes), so Lisp sees GNU's names, identity and printed text (`raw_bytes_in_source_symbols_keep_their_characters`, `tests/symbol_names.rs`); `intern` of a unibyte name with bytes above 127 is still refused (`not-unicode-string`) | **Package-observable:** a cookie-less ISO-2022 or emacs-mule source fails to load where GNU decodes it; none is in the GNU tree | <!-- symbols: load, load-with-code-conversion, load-source-file-function -->
| VM2-FACEFN1 | A function as a face's `:height` | `internal-set-lisp-face-attribute` (`src/xfaces.c:3217`) stores the function in the lface and validates it through `merge_face_heights (VALUE, 10, nil)`, which calls it with `safe_calln` (a signal counts as nil); realization merges it at each step, `merge_face_heights` calling it with the height beneath it (the default face's, or a larger face's it is merged over, as for `superscript` inside a heading). `tex-mode.el`'s `superscript`/`subscript` faces use `tex-suscript-height` | The lface keeps the function (`face-attribute` returns it) and validation calls it the same way (`crates/romacs-vm/src/prims/face.rs`). The display theme (`romacs-face`) holds data only, so the frame's attribute is mirrored as the function's value over the default face's height on that frame at the time it is set (`display_tests.rs` `a_function_height_stays_in_the_lface_and_realizes_over_the_default_height`) | **User-observable:** a function-height face merged over a face other than the default is drawn at the default-relative size, and a later change of the default height does not recompute it. Lisp sees GNU's values | <!-- symbols: internal-set-lisp-face-attribute, set-face-attribute, face-attribute -->
| VM2-ALIAS2 | `defalias` optional DOCSTRING | A non-nil third argument replaces the symbol's `function-documentation` property (`src/data.c:1021-1022`) | VM2 accepts a third argument but never stores it (`crates/romacs-vm/src/bootstrap.rs:48,67-79`). After `(defalias 'audit-f 'car "audit-doc")`, `(get 'audit-f 'function-documentation)` remains nil, or retains a pre-existing property | **Silent, package-observable metadata loss:** alias-specific help is missing or stale although assignment succeeds. `(documentation-property 'audit-f 'function-documentation)` returns `"audit-doc"` on GNU; VM2 currently lacks that callable query as well. Explicit `(put 'audit-f 'function-documentation "audit-doc")` stores the property; this does not implement the missing query | <!-- symbols: defalias, function-documentation, documentation-property -->
| VM2-KM2 | `key-binding` NO-REMAP and POSITION arguments; `command-remapping` | Uses POSITION to choose active maps and command remapping, and non-nil NO-REMAP to bypass remapping (`src/keymap.c:1836-1852`); `command-remapping` looks `[remap COMMAND]` up with `key-binding` (NO-REMAP t) or, given KEYMAPS, `lookup-key` (`src/keymap.c:1216`) | VM2 `key-binding` searches only the buffer's local map and the global map and never applies command remapping, so a nil NO-REMAP returns the unremapped binding; a non-nil POSITION signals `key-binding POSITION not supported`. `command-remapping` with KEYMAPS follows GNU; without them it sees only those two maps. `lookup-key` runs a menu item's `:filter` as GNU's lookup does (rlisp `romacs--menu-filter`); `key-binding` and `define-key` refuse a binding that needs one | **Package-observable:** a `[remap ...]` binding in a minor-mode or overriding map is not found, and `(key-binding [97] nil nil 1)` fails. Code that names its keymaps, as `keymap-lookup` callers usually do, gets GNU's answer | <!-- symbols: key-binding, command-remapping -->
| VM2-EMPTY1 | zero-length strings and vectors are not unique objects | Every zero-length string is one of two shared objects and every zero-length vector is `zero_vector` (`src/alloc.c:2329-2330,2358-2359,2903,3424`): `(eq "" "")`, `(eq (copy-sequence "") "")`, `(eq (vector) [])` and `(eq (substring "abc" 3) "")` are all t, and `(substring "" nil nil)` returns its argument. | VM2 allocates a fresh object for each empty string or vector, so all of those are nil. Pinned in the bulk `core` corpus as `eq/0476`, `eq/1067`, `substring/1585`, `substring/1669`, and found independently by primh's `alias` family as `substring/8251`, `substring/8263` (`tools/primh/evidence/substring.json`; all class `VALUE`). | **Package-observable identity:** code that tests `(eq s "")` instead of `(string-empty-p s)`/`(equal s "")` takes the other branch. Contents, length, `equal` and printing are identical. Canonicalising empties is an allocator change (a rooted shared object per kind), deliberately not folded into the primitive batch that found it. | <!-- symbols: eq, substring, copy-sequence, vector, make-string -->
| VM2-FMTALIAS1 | `format` never returns its format string itself | When formatting produces no change, `styled_format` returns the FORMAT argument itself (`src/editfns.c:4301-4304` at pin `45b0f7699df`, `if (! new_result) { val = args[0]; ...}`): `(let ((s (copy-sequence "abc"))) (list (eq (format "%s" s) s) (eq (format s) s) (eq (format "%s" "") "")))` is `(t t t)` on GNU 32.0.50. | VM2's `format` always allocates a fresh string: the same form is `(nil nil nil)` (measured, `romacs-vm --eval-stream`, lane `vm2/bulk1`). `string-trim` and `string-replace` share GNU's no-change identity but are void in VM2 today, so they are not yet a divergence. | **Package-observable identity only:** code that tests `(eq (format ...) s)` to detect "nothing changed", or that mutates the result expecting to leave the argument intact (GNU would mutate the argument), behaves differently. Contents and properties match. Not fixed in the `core` batch, which does not touch `format`; recorded because it was known and unrecorded. | <!-- symbols: format, format-message, string-trim, string-replace -->
| VM2-CLOSEQ1 | a closure is not a sequence in VM2 | A closure is a vector-like object in GNU 32.0.50, so `length`, `append` and `mapcar` accept it as a sequence: `(length (lambda (x) x))` is 5 for the byte-compiled closure and 3 for the interpreted one (both regimes recorded; `src/fns.c:154` at pin `45b0f7699df`, `Flength` counts a `CLOSUREP` object's slots). | Ordinary VM2 closures remain opaque objects: all three signal `wrong-type-argument`. Explicit `make-interpreted-closure` values and marked OClosures expose their supported metadata slots; this does not make ordinary closures sequences. `aref` of slot 2, the environment, answers for a closure made where no lexical binding is visible (a top-level `defun` or `lambda`): GNU's is then the dialect's alone, `(t)` or nil, which nadvice reads to run an advised command's computed interactive form (`tests/closure_environment.rs`); under a `let` it still signals, as does slot 1, the body. Slot 0 answers the lambda list as written, which `help-function-arglist` (eldoc, `describe-function`) reads; code images do not record it, and there it still signals. Pinned in the bulk `core` corpus as `length/0071`, `append/1041`, `mapcar/0257` (class `VALUE`; the probes catch the type error as -99 so the closure is never printed). | **Package-observable, rarely:** only code that measures or walks a function object's slots (OClosure internals, some debuggers) sees the difference; `cl-print` prints an ordinary closure as `prin1` does (`rlisp/romacs-cl-print.el`). General closure sequence operations remain out of scope. OClosure uses the explicit metadata boundary described in VM2-OCLOSURE1. | <!-- symbols: length, append, mapcar, closurep -->
| VM2-MACROFN1 | calling a symbol whose cell is a macro: `invalid-function` without data | `funcall`/`apply` of a symbol whose function cell is a macro signals `(invalid-function SYMBOL)` (`src/eval.c` `funcall_general`): `(progn (fset 'q '(macro . car)) (condition-case e (funcall 'q 1) (error e)))` and the same through `(assoc 2 '((1 . a)) 'q)` both give `(invalid-function q)` on GNU 32.0.50. | VM2 refuses the call with the text fault "invalid function: macro is not callable" (`crates/romacs-vm/src/threaded.rs` `invoke_full`, `t2_resolve`), so the condition is `invalid-function` with NO data: `(invalid-function)`. Funcalling the literal `(macro . car)` matches GNU. Pre-existing (present at `0aded923`); found by the second Sol review of `vm2/bulk1`, not changed by it. | **Package-observable data only:** handlers that dispatch on `invalid-function` still catch it; code reading `(cadr e)` to name the offending function sees nothing. | <!-- symbols: funcall, apply, invalid-function, defmacro -->
| VM2-KEYMAPLISP1 | `accessible-keymaps` and `keymap--get-keyelt` are Lisp functions | Both are C subrs (`src/keymap.c:2010`, `src/keymap.c:646`), and `where-is-internal` walks `Faccessible_keymaps` in C. `get_keyelt` follows a circular `(STRING . DEFN)` or `menu-item` wrapper forever | VM2 defines both in its built-in prelude (`rlisp/vm/prelude.el`), since the walk calls `map-keymap`'s Lisp callback (PRIMITIVE-DOCTRINE section 3); `where-is-internal` walks `accessible-keymaps` as GNU's does. Their values, order, cycle rule, meta-prefix folding, string prefixes and `:filter` application match pinned GNU (`tests/journey_primitives.rs`); a circular wrapper signals `Circular menu item` | **Package-observable metadata:** `subrp` of either is nil and their arity errors are a closure's, as for `mapcar` (VM2-MAPCAR1) | <!-- symbols: accessible-keymaps, keymap--get-keyelt, where-is-internal -->
| VM2-READEVAL1 | `eval-buffer` and `eval-region` are Lisp functions | Both are C subrs over lread.c's `readevalloop`, which `load` also uses (through `load-with-code-conversion`): `load` of a source file binds `eval-buffer-list` to its reading buffer | VM2 defines both in its built-in prelude (`rlisp/vm/prelude.el`) over one Lisp `readevalloop`: GNU's dialect choice (the file's cookie, then `internal--get-default-lexical-binding-function`), eager expansion through `internal-macroexpand-for-load` with a `progn`'s forms taken one at a time, a top-level `(defvar NAME)` special for the later forms (passed as `eval`'s environment, as Fdefvar extends GNU's), READ-FUNCTION and `load-read-function`, PRINTFLAG streams and `values`, narrowing, `eval-buffer-list` and `load-history` (`tests/journey_primitives.rs`). `load` stays VM2's Rust loader, which reads the file's bytes and binds no reading buffer, so `eval-buffer-list` is nil while a file loads. A partial `eval-region` merges every new definition into an existing `load-history` entry; GNU's build_load_history keeps only the last | **Package-observable metadata:** `subrp` of either is nil and their arity errors are a closure's (VM2-MAPCAR1); code that inspects `eval-buffer-list` during `load` sees it empty | <!-- symbols: eval-buffer, eval-region, eval-buffer-list, load-history -->
| VM2-EWCLOAD1 | Compile-time requires of source-loaded GNU Lisp | GNU loads its bundled Lisp as `.elc`, where an `(eval-when-compile (require 'X))` left nothing: X loads only if something else needs it. So eshell's `esh-util.el` (and `term.el`) do not load `ange-ftp` | VM2 loads that Lisp from source and compiles each function when it is defined, so macros from X must exist then: the `require` runs, and X's load-time effects happen. Requiring `esh-util`, as `eshell` and `project-eshell` (`C-x p e`) do, loads `ange-ftp`, which adds `ange-ftp-set-buffer-mode` to `find-file-hook`; it tests each visited name with `posix-string-match` and changes nothing for a local file. Likewise the first source load of `bytecomp.el` and `byte-opt.el` (cc-mode's compile-time requires reach them when a C file is visited) runs their load-time self-compilation blocks, which print `compiling FUNCTION...done` for each function; the `byte-compile` calls themselves return the already compiled functions (VM2-BYTECOMP1). Skipping such requires is unsound while a function body cannot pick up a macro defined after the body was compiled (GNU's evaluator expands it when the call is evaluated; VM2 signals `invalid-function`) | **Package-observable:** `(featurep 'ange-ftp)` and `find-file-hook` differ after eshell loads; visiting behaves as GNU's. File-name completion (`~/` typed in the middle of a path, `~USER`) loads neither tramp nor ange-ftp, as in GNU (`tests/journey_primitives.rs`) | <!-- symbols: eval-when-compile, ange-ftp, find-file-hook, esh-util -->
| VM2-LOADCOMPILED | Which loads run compiled code | GNU's `load` picks a file's `.elc` (or `.eln`) by its suffix rules; an installation ships its Lisp compiled by `make`, and a package's `.elc` is made when it is installed. | In an interactive session (by default; `ROMACS_COMPILED_LISP=off` turns it off) and in a batch run with `ROMACS_COMPILED_LISP=on`, a load GNU would satisfy with compiled code (a file of the installation's Lisp trees, or one beside an `.elc` that `load-prefer-newer` accepts) runs a compiled-Lisp artifact: the file walked by bytecomp's file rules in a compile process, each top-level form compiled to VM2 code, and the units run in order under GNU's compiled-load bindings and unwind (`crates/romacs-vm/src/compiled_lisp`). A load with no artifact reads source in an interactive session, which compiles the file in the background later (VM2-LOADPRECOMPILE), so no load waits for a compile; a batch run compiles it synchronously, then loads it. A session without a store of its own (a fresh HOME's batch run) uses what is compiled and reads the rest from source, compiling nothing. Artifacts are never stale: every observed input (files loaded and resolved at compile time, host reads, inherited environment variables) is checked again at load, and a change recompiles. | GNU runs a stale `.elc` with a warning; here a changed source or dependency always recompiles. With compiled loads, VM2-EWCLOAD1 applies to source-kind loads only. | <!-- symbols: load, require, load-prefer-newer -->
| VM2-LOADENV | The environment compile-time code sees | `make` compiles an installation's `.elc` files in the builder's environment; `package.el` compiles a package inside the user's session, with its HOME, environment and loaded state. | A compile process runs in the compile environment: its own world (the batch world of that environment), a HOME and `TMPDIR` inside the store, its working directory that HOME, the C.UTF-8 locale and no user identity variables. It inherits only `PATH`, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, `ROMACS_RUNTIME_ROOT` and `ROMACS_LISP_DIR` from the session, and a compile-time read of one of those is an observed input. So `(eval-when-compile (getenv "HOME"))` gives the store's HOME in every session, as GNU's installed `.elc` gives the builder's, and the session's own macros, advice and variables never reach a compile. | One artifact serves every HOME and session: what a compile makes of a file depends on the file and its observed inputs only. A package whose compile-time code needs the user's HOME or loaded state gets the compile environment's, as an installed `.elc` does. | <!-- symbols: eval-when-compile, eval-and-compile, getenv, byte-compile-file -->
| VM2-LOADPRECOMPILE | When compiled code is made | `make` compiles an installation's `.elc` before first use; a package's are made at installation. | The installation's store (`EXECUTABLE.lisp/` beside the executable, shared by every HOME; else the user's cache) is filled ahead of use while an interactive session is idle (`rlisp/romacs-precompile.el`, `romacs-precompile`, `romacs-precompile-idle-delay`): a host thread of the session, which never touches the Lisp heap, starts one compile process at a time at the idle CPU and I/O priority once the session has been idle that long, first for the files loads just read from source for want of an artifact, then the files compiled loads used before, then every file of the installation's Lisp trees. | A first `require` finds its artifact made, without an install step; the session itself does no work for it, between keys or while idle. | <!-- symbols: romacs-precompile, romacs-precompile-idle-delay, romacs-precompile-start, romacs-precompile-stop -->
| VM2-LOADNAME | File names of a compiled load | `load-file-name`, `load-true-file-name`, the `load-history` entry, `symbol-file` and the `after-load-functions` argument name the `.elc`. | They name the source `x.el`: there is no `x.elc` on disk, and naming one would point code at a file that does not exist. | An `eval-after-load` or `after-load-alist` key written with an explicit `.elc`, or an `after-load-functions` hook testing for `.elc`, fires in GNU and not here. In `gnu-lisp/` and `rlisp/` no key names `.elc`, and every one of the 14 sites matching `\\.elc` against a file name accepts `.el` too. | <!-- symbols: load-file-name, load-true-file-name, load-history, symbol-file, after-load-functions, eval-after-load -->
| VM2-LOADNBC | `no-byte-compile` beside an `.elc` | GNU loads an existing `.elc` even when its source now sets `no-byte-compile`. | Such a file loads as source: a compile refuses it, as `byte-compile-file` would. | Only an `.elc` left from before the cookie was added differs. | <!-- symbols: no-byte-compile, load -->
| VM2-LOADUNIT | Constant identity across top-level forms | GNU's `.elc` shares an `equal` quoted constant between the forms of one pending output unit; a source load never does. | Equal quoted constants of different top-level forms are never `eq`; within one function they are coalesced, as bytecomp's constant vector is. | Code that tests `eq` between literals of two top-level forms; none is known. | <!-- symbols: eq -->
| VM2-DOCREP | Variable and face documentation of a compiled load | An `.elc` stores `variable-documentation` as a `(FILE . OFFSET)` reference that `documentation-property` resolves. | The documentation is an inline string in the plist; `documentation-property` gives the same text. | Code reading the raw property sees a string, not a reference. | <!-- symbols: documentation-property, get -->
| VM2-LOADINLINE | `defsubst` and `define-inline` in compiled loads | A compiled caller inlines the body bytecomp recorded, and keeps it after the callee is redefined; a defsubst loaded as source is inlined from its interpreted closure's body; a callee defined by `defalias` to a non-lambda is not inlined. | A compiled caller inlines by calling a frozen copy of the definition in force when it was compiled, and keeps it after a redefinition, as GNU. A defsubst whose definition was loaded as source (a `no-byte-compile` file) is called, not inlined: VM2 cannot read a compiled closure's source back. A `defalias` to a non-lambda records `(NAME . t)` and later calls are not inlined, as bytecomp.el does. The compile log lists calls not inlined (`not-inlined`). | Calls to a source-loaded defsubst behave as calls; only a later redefinition of that defsubst reaches them, as with any function. | <!-- symbols: defsubst, define-inline, defalias -->
| VM2-MAPCAR1 | `mapcar`, `mapc`, `mapcan` and `mapconcat` are Lisp functions in VM2 | They are the C subrs `Fmapcar`, `Fmapc`, `Fmapcan` and `Fmapconcat` (`src/fns.c:3484`-`3585`, arities `(2 . 2)` and `(2 . 3)`): `(subrp (symbol-function 'mapcar))` is t, and a wrong argument count signals `(wrong-number-of-arguments #<subr mapcar> N)`. | VM2 defines all four in its built-in prelude (`rlisp/vm/prelude.el`) because a Rust primitive must not call Lisp from a loop (GC doctrine): `subrp` of them is nil, `symbol-function` returns a closure, and a wrong count signals the compiled-closure form `(wrong-number-of-arguments (2 . 2) N)`. Their traversal (`length` first, a list until the length or a tail the function shortened, a vector, string or bool-vector by index), element order, `mapconcat`'s `identity` shortcut and separator, `mapcan`'s `nconc` and their signals match GNU case for case on the bulk `core` corpus and the `mapc`/`mapcan` primitive evidence, except the arity rows `mapcar/0203`-`0206`, `mapc/0048`, `mapc/0049`, `mapc/0078`, `mapcan/0243`, `mapcan/0244` (pinned `DATA`/`PRINTER`) and a closure used as the SEQUENCE (`mapcar/0257`, VM2-CLOSEQ1). The frame a callback sees under the mapping function's is the function's own, as on GNU (`tests/backtrace.rs`, `tests/map_functions.rs`). | **Package-observable metadata:** code inspecting `subrp`/`subr-arity` of these, or reading the function datum of their arity errors, sees a closure. Ordinary calls over lists, vectors, strings and bool-vectors are indistinguishable. | <!-- symbols: mapcar, mapc, mapcan, mapconcat, subrp, symbol-function, wrong-number-of-arguments -->
| D004 | `%S` of control characters | Emacs escapes | JSON control escapes | one escape convention |  <!-- symbols: format, prin1-to-string, message -->
| [D005](crates/romacs-vm/GNU-DIVERGENCES.md#d005--reader-raw-byteunicode-mixtures-resolved) | reader string mixtures | BYTE8 pseudocharacters retained | resolved for Lisp strings and, through VM2-RAWTEXT1, for buffer text; source-file decoding remains separately bounded | canonical 22-bit string codec | <!-- symbols: read, read-from-string, eval-buffer, load -->
| — | `transient` | bundled | **removed**; `(require 'transient)` fails | emoji was its only real consumer; Magit/gptel take it from MELPA |
| — | circular face structures and input budgets | **SIGSEGV** at ~32,931 `merge_face_ref` frames; circular plist tail hangs instead; no native payload cap | typed `BoundedDatum` boundary rejects cycles, depth >32, >2^20 conses and >16 MiB expanded strings/symbols; printed source capped too | bounded native work instead of a crash or OOM |
| — | face remapping cycles | merge-point fallback/truncation | accepts transitive NORMAL/REMAP inheritance backrefs as GNU does (`xfaces.c:1976-2000`); rejects cycles *between distinct remapping entries*, returning the original paintable theme plus a diagnostic | keep valid solaire/mode-line remaps without silently accepting genuine entry cycles |  <!-- symbols: face-remapping-alist, face-attribute -->
| — | anonymous face plists | `:inherit` position can override own attributes | own attributes always win | one precedence rule |  <!-- symbols: face-attribute, defface -->
| — | Custom theme stack | enabled-theme/user priority stack, `custom-set-faces`, `theme-face` records | one immutable theme spec composed with one explicit `UserOverrides` value; user preferences survive theme replacement | R90 removes the mechanism and keeps the preferences; **no speed comparison is claimed** (the earlier 3.7 ns figure was retracted — it compared a prebuilt swap against GNU's full rebuild) |
| — | display-condition target | frame/backend conditions including tty, greyscale, monochrome and actual GTK feature | fixed truecolour graphical Wayland; `romacs`, `graphic`, `pgtk`, `gtk` match; `supports` always true; no tty/greyscale/mono | one render target. **Observable and deliberate:** selects GUI attributes for `custom-button` and 8 other specs that today's romacs leaves blank, because it reports window-system `romacs` while `faces.el` matches only `graphic` |
| — | face height functions | invokes Lisp during merge | rejected | immutable face data, no evaluator callback |  <!-- symbols: set-face-attribute, face-attribute, defface -->
| — | face `:font` | graphical font backend interprets font values | ignored here; font objects belong to `romacs-font` | explicit separate font boundary |  <!-- symbols: set-face-attribute, face-font, defface -->
| — | dashed face family names | setter may split foundry/family | preserves the literal family, leaves foundry unspecified | explicit data, no implicit name parsing |  <!-- symbols: set-face-attribute, face-family -->
| — | undefined inherited face | C skips it; Lisp `face-attribute` can abandon the remainder | skips missing parents in both merge and query paths | follow redisplay's C behaviour |
| — | realized `reset` query | Lisp `face-attribute` can expose `reset`; C substitutes the default | realized queries substitute the default | never paint a sentinel |
| — | window-filtered face refs | considers window parameter context | never matches; there is no window in this `View`/cache key | do not pretend a window-free cache is window-sensitive |  <!-- symbols: face-remapping-alist, face-attribute -->
| — | ambiguous aliases within one theme/user value | mutable setter/replay order picks a winner | duplicate canonical face keys, cyclic aliases and duplicate user-alist faces are all rejected | deterministic explicit values, not hidden precedence |  <!-- symbols: defface, face-attribute, face-remapping-alist -->
| — | tty / greyscale / monochrome display specs | supported | gone | one render target |  <!-- symbols: defface, display-graphic-p, display-color-p -->
| — | overlay enumeration order | GNU tree order | deterministic, not GNU's | tree internals are not a contract |  <!-- symbols: overlays-in, overlays-at, next-overlay-change, previous-overlay-change -->
| — | reversed `overlays-in` bounds | tolerated | swapped | — |
| — | `overlays-in` end beyond `point-max` | tolerated | errors | loud over quiet |
| — | marker/overlay in structured results | rich payload | opaque `{"type":"marker"}` tags | internals are not a wire format |  <!-- symbols: make-marker, make-overlay, point-marker -->
| H1 | indirect `maphash` (`apply`, computed `funcall`) on a non-empty table | calls FUNCTION | signals `(error "VM2 cannot call maphash indirectly on a non-empty table" F)`; direct and `(funcall #'maphash …)` calls work | primitives cannot call Lisp; loud over skipped entries |
| H2 | `make-hash-table` with a `define-hash-table-test` test | accepted; user functions called on lookup | a test whose predicate VM2 has natively is that predicate, with a native hash that agrees with it, and `hash-table-test` answers its name: `function-equal` with any HASH (profiler.el's `profiler-function-equal`; closures hash by their code), and `equal-including-properties` with `sxhash-equal-including-properties` (tab-bar.el's). HASH is never called, which no table whose HASH agrees with its test can observe; such a table refuses to freeze. Any other test signals `(error "VM2 does not support user-defined hash table tests" NAME)` | same reason as H1 |
| H3 | `equal` on closures (hash keys, `equal`) | element-wise: identical lambdas from different sites are `equal` | `equal` only for the same compiled lambda with `equal` captures | closures are function identity plus captures |
| H6 | `hash-table-keys`/`-values` without `(require 'subr-x)` | void-function | defined natively, GNU's (reversed slot) order | no subr-x load needed |
| D-ML1 | VM2 redisplay-time `set-window-vscroll` | `src/window.c:Fset_window_vscroll` changes the live window immediately; `src/xdisp.c:display_mode_element` calls `dsafe_eval`. GNU 32.0.50 PGTK direct `format-mode-line`: `inside=7 after=7` (`s34-gnu-mid-build.txt`); real redisplay: `inside=7 after=0` (`s50-gnu-redisplay.txt`) | VM2 phase-one `:eval` reads committed vscroll (`inside=0`), queues the pixel setter with one diagnostic naming `:eval` and `set-window-vscroll`, then explicit post-paint journal application commits 7 for frame N+1 (`after=7`). Last write wins, including writing the original value. Direct `format-mode-line` is not yet implemented | **Package-observable and deliberate:** two-phase immutable layout cannot accept a mid-build window mutation; this is the S34/GUI-FLIP ruling, not a GNU-parity claim | <!-- symbols: set-window-vscroll, window-vscroll, mode-line-format -->
| VM2-DC1 | `defcustom` stores the raw default form where GNU stores a thunk, and `:set` is never invoked | Measured on GNU 32.0.50 for `(defcustom e-opt (progn (setq n (1+ n)) 7) "d" :type 'integer)`: `(get 'e-opt 'standard-value)` is `((funcall #'#[nil ((progn (setq n (1+ n)) 7)) (t)]))` — a list whose car is a **form that calls a closure** wrapping the default — so `(eval (car (get 'e-opt 'standard-value)) t)` returns `7` and re-runs the side effect. A `:set` function runs **once at definition**. `custom-variable-p` answers non-nil | VM2 stores the **raw unevaluated form**: `standard-value` is `((progn (setq n (1+ n)) 7))`. `(eval (car (get 'e-opt 'standard-value)) t)` **signals `void or non-function`** rather than returning 7, and the side effect does not re-run. A `:set` function is recorded under `custom-set` but **never invoked** — the setter counter stays 0 where GNU's reaches 1. `custom-type` and the other keyword properties match GNU, and **`custom-variable-p` matches GNU exactly** (`t` for a `defcustom`-defined symbol, `nil` otherwise). No theme machinery (removed by R90) | **Package-observable** in two ways. Code that recomputes a default via `(eval (car (get 'sym 'standard-value)) t)` — which is what Custom itself does — gets an error instead of the value. Code relying on `:set` running at definition time silently does not get its setter called, so a variable whose setter has side effects beyond `set-default` is left uninitialised. Ordinary reads of the variable's value are identical, and `custom-variable-p` is not a difference. Revertible: wrap the stored form so `eval` of the car yields the default, and invoke `custom-set` at definition | <!-- symbols: defcustom, standard-value, custom-set, custom-type, custom-variable-p -->
| VM2-VD1 | A local `(defvar x)` reaches `let` but not lambda parameters | GNU's `funcall_lambda` (`src/eval.c:3433`) binds arguments by consing onto `lexenv` whenever `lexenv` is non-nil and **never** consults a local-special marker, while `Flet`/`FletX` (`eval.c:1109`, `:1183`) check `declared_special` and `Fmemq (var, Vinternal_interpreter_environment)`. A one-argument `Fdefvar` enters the interpreter environment only | VM2 matches GNU: the compiler's parameter-binding path does not consult local specials, while its `let` path does. Verified on live GNU 32.0.50 interpreted with `lexical-binding: t` on line 1 — the lambda-parameter form signals `void-variable`, the `let` form answers 18 | **Not a divergence in behaviour**; recorded because VM2 briefly implemented the symmetric (wrong) rule and a reader may expect symmetry. Both directions are pinned by `local_declaration_does_not_reach_lambda_parameters_but_does_reach_let` and mutation-proven | <!-- symbols: defvar, let, lambda -->
| VM2-VD2 | A globally special name binds lambda parameters dynamically | GNU's `funcall_lambda` (`src/eval.c:3433-3436`) binds every parameter of a lexical closure by consing onto `lexenv` whenever `lexenv` is non-nil, without consulting `declared_special`; the byte compiler also binds such a parameter lexically. Live GNU 32.0.50 over `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/file-local-special/flr.el` (`(defvar flp-x 0)`, then `(funcall (lambda (flp-x) (flp-get)) 42)` where `flp-get` reads the dynamic value): `(0 43)` interpreted and `(0 43)` byte-compiled (`gnu-cache-kinds.txt`) | VM2's compiler binds a parameter dynamically when its name is globally special (`declare_special`/`defvar` with a value), so the same fixture answers `(42 43)` (`vm2-af1209d0-probes.txt`): the callee sees the argument instead of the global value. `let` matches GNU (dynamic) | **Package-observable** for code that names a lambda or `defun` parameter after a global special variable (the byte compiler warns about it) and relies on callees NOT seeing the argument; found while reviewing VM2-FILESPECIAL1's cache key, which now records global and file-local declarations separately because they compile differently. Revertible by binding parameters lexically regardless of global specialness in the compiler's parameter path | <!-- symbols: lambda, defun, defvar, funcall -->
| VM2-FILESPECIAL1 | A file-local `(defvar x)` reaches the rest of its file only when it is a bare top-level form | `Fdefvar` with one argument (`src/eval.c:990-996`) conses SYMBOL onto `internal-interpreter-environment` when that is non-nil; `readevalloop` (`src/lread.c`) binds the variable once for the whole file and evaluates each form with `eval_sub`, which does not rebind it, so the declaration makes SYMBOL dynamically bound by every LATER `let` and closure of that file and of no other file. A `progn`, `when` or binding-less `let` around it does not rebind the environment either, so `(progn (defvar x))`, `(when t (defvar x))` and `(let () (defvar x))` at top level all persist; `(when nil (defvar x))` does not run and declares nothing. Live GNU 32.0.50: `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/file-local-special/` (`gnu-transcript.txt`, `nested-forms.txt`) | VM2's loader records a top-level form that is exactly `(defvar SYMBOL)` once it has evaluated, and seeds the root compilation of every later form of the same file with those names (`crates/romacs-vm/src/loader.rs`); it also conses the name onto the file's binding of `macroexp--dynvars`, as GNU's `macroexpand--all-toplevel` does, so a macro that evaluates code at expansion time (`eval-and-compile` inside `pcase-defmacro`) hands the declaration to `eval`, saving and restoring the set around each `load`; the source cache keys its validity on them, so an identical form in a file without the declaration is recompiled. A declaration nested inside a top-level `progn`/`when`/`let` still reaches only the rest of its own form (the compiler's existing local-special scope, VM2-VD1), so GNU's `(1 2 3 unbound)` for the four nested shapes above is VM2's `(unbound unbound unbound unbound)` past the form. `eval-buffer`/`eval-region` are not covered. Before this row (fixed on `vm2/defvar-local`) even the bare form reached nothing past itself, which made cl-macs.el's `(defvar cl--loop-args)` (line 939) ineffective. The source caches (in memory and persistent) key a cached form on each name's declaration KIND, lexical / file-local / global (`Vm::special_kind`), and the persistent cache's portable compiler reproduces file-local declarations as file-local (Sol review of af1209d0: it had made them global, so a warm load bound a lambda parameter dynamically where the cold load bound it lexically; `tests/file_local_special.rs`). Also different, pre-existing and not introduced by this row's fix (measured by that review, `evidence/file-local-special/fld.el`, `gnu-cache-kinds.txt`): `(defvar nil)` and `(defvar t)` signal `cannot declare a constant` where GNU returns `nil` and `t`; `(special-variable-p 'nil)` and `(special-variable-p 't)` are nil where GNU answers t; and a one-argument `(defvar x)` executed inside a function that is called at top level declares nothing for later forms, where GNU's later `let` binds x dynamically (fixture: GNU `(t t 9 7)`, VM2 `(nil nil 9 unbound)`) | **Package-observable** for files that declare a variable special inside a wrapping top-level form and bind it in later forms; the bare `(defvar x)` that cl-macs.el and most of `lisp/` use behaves as GNU | <!-- symbols: defvar, let, internal-interpreter-environment, load -->
| VM2-KM1 | VM2 keymap skeleton is installed lazily | GNU builds `global-map`, `esc-map`, `ctl-x-map`, `function-key-map`, `key-translation-map` and `special-event-map` while loading `lisp/subr.el` and `lisp/bindings.el`, so by the time any user Lisp runs these variables are already bound to keymaps (`src/keymap.c:syms_of_keymap` defines the variables; the maps themselves come from Lisp) | VM2 boots without loading `subr.el` and installs the same skeleton on **first use of any keymap primitive** rather than in `Vm::new`. Reading `global-map` (or the other five) before calling any keymap primitive signals `void-variable`, where GNU returns a keymap; after any keymap primitive runs, the variables are bound and behave as GNU | **Package-observable** only by Lisp that reads one of these six variables without first calling a keymap function, which `bindings.el` and ordinary configuration do not do. Chosen so a freshly constructed VM allocates nothing: the collector's tests assert exact heap contents and arena indices, and a constructor-time skeleton silently redefines "empty heap" for all of them. Revertible by calling `initialize_keymaps` from `Vm::new` and converting those GC tests to arena-relative indices | <!-- symbols: global-map, esc-map, ctl-x-map, function-key-map, key-translation-map, special-event-map -->
| VM2-CMD1 | VM2 single-event command loop | `keyboard.c:command_loop_1/read_key_sequence` accumulate prefixes, translate events, invoke hooks and call `call-interactively` (`callint.c:Fcall_interactively`) on the binding | `Vm::command_event` (the stdin protocol and test adapters) translates native Unicode/control/named events, consults existing keymaps and runs the bound command through the real `call-interactively` primitive (VM2-CALLINT1 lists its spec subset), without prefix accumulation, command hooks, prefix-arg transfer or macro-specific undefined signalling; its `this-command-keys` is a single-event vector. Any closure with an `(interactive ...)` form, whether made by `defun`, `defalias`, `fset` or an anonymous `lambda`, is a command. [Exact contract/evidence](crates/romacs-vm/COMMAND-LOOP.md) | <!-- symbols: interactive, this-command-keys, last-command-event, key-binding -->

| VM2-GUIERR2 | GUI command-loop errors in the echo area | `keyboard.c` `command-error-default-function` prints the error with `print_error_message` into the echo area and rings the bell; `command_loop_1` clears the echo area when the next command starts. `call-interactively` of a key bound to a symbol with no function signals `(wrong-type-argument commandp SYMBOL)` (GNU 32.0.50: `Wrong type argument: commandp, romacs-nonexistent-cmd`) | The GUI host echoes `error-message-string` of the error rebuilt from VM2's printed condition and data, clears it when the next non-prefix command starts, and rings the bell. | VM2-G1B | VM2 mode-line/vscroll subset | `src/xdisp.c:display_mode_element` (`:28458`) and `decode_mode_spec` (`:29429`): buffer-local formats per window, propertized strings with their own text properties, conditionals, field widths/precision, risk filtering, every percent escape over the C state (coding systems, frame name, recursion depth, processes, save point); `src/window.c:Fwindow_vscroll/Fset_window_vscroll` support character units, numeric scroll values and preserve-vscroll | Tier B evaluates each window's format with that window's buffer current, its window point installed as buffer point (`redisplay_window`, `xdisp.c:20620-20641`), and the window temporarily selected (`display_mode_lines`, `xdisp.c:28114-28156`); the real selection is rooted across collecting `:eval` forms, and buffer point and selection are restored after evaluation, including error/throw/quit, before choosing the active/inactive face (same-buffer split oracle `crates/romacs-vm-gui/evidence/tierb/gnu/point-context.txt`): buffer-local formats, `:eval`/`:propertize` (face symbol or first face of a face list), conditionals, integer field widths and precisions, risky-variable filtering, `*too-deep*`/`*invalid*`, and `%b %f %* %+ %& %l %c %C %p %P %o %q %i %I %n %m %M %@ %- %%` exactly as GNU 32.0.50 formats them (oracle `crates/romacs-vm-gui/evidence/tierb/gnu/mode-line.txt`). Without the C state GNU reads: `%z` is `-` and `%Z` is `-` plus `eol-mnemonic-undecided` (GNU: the buffer's coding mnemonic, `UUU:` for utf-8-unix); `%F` is `Emacs` (GNU tty `F1`, GUI frame title); `%e` is empty, which is GNU's answer on a `SYSTEM_MALLOC` build such as glibc GNU/Linux even while `memory-full` is non-nil (`xdisp.c` `decode_mode_spec` `case 'e'`; oracle `crates/romacs-vm-gui/evidence/tierb/gnu/standard-head.txt`), where a gmalloc build shows `!MEM FULL! `; `save_modiff` stays 0 (no `set-buffer-modified-p`/save), so any edit shows `**` until the buffer dies; text-property changes (`put-text-property`, `remove-text-properties`) do not count as modifications, where GNU's `modify_text_properties` (textprop.c) bumps MODIFF when a property actually changes, so a buffer changed only in its properties shows `--` here and `**` in GNU; `mode-name` has no buffer.c default `"Fundamental"`. Assigned `%m`/`%f` values and `%Z`'s explicit EOL mnemonic accept both multibyte and unibyte strings; unibyte high bytes are projected to octal glyph text before field-width/precision handling (not GNU's raw-byte character counting). A string element's own `face` (a symbol, or a face list's first symbol) shows where its `:propertize` gives none, as GNU merges PROPS onto a non-risky string's properties; its other properties and anonymous face plists are not projected onto the band. GNU stops producing band glyphs at the window's last visible column (`xdisp.c:30105`); VM2 builds band text up to four times the frame's columns and the painter clips it at the window edge, so the visible band is the same, and both keep evaluating `:eval` elements past the edge. Field widths and integer limits wrap to a C `int` as GNU's do (`xdisp.c:28663`, `:28879`; 32.0.50 transcript `crates/romacs-vm-gui/evidence/tierb/gnu/field-wrap.txt`: a limit of 2305843009213693951 is -1, a one-column precision, so the next element stays visible), then VM2 clamps them to 2^20 columns; the clamp only changes text beyond the band's four frame widths, which neither side shows. A format string's literal run is selected by columns (TAB `tab-width`, other control characters 2, GNU `c_string_width`) and then counts its characters toward precision and padding, as `store_mode_line_string` does; `%`-spec results and literal strings truncate and pad by characters (`crates/romacs-vm-gui/evidence/tierb/gnu/control-width.txt`). A control character that fits is painted `^X`; GNU's painted form of a TAB in a mode line has not been compared. Band faces follow GNU's `CURRENT_MODE_LINE_ACTIVE_FACE_ID`: the selected window draws `mode-line-active`, others `mode-line-inactive` while `mode-line-in-non-selected-windows` is non-nil; because faces.el (which defines `mode-line-active` inheriting `mode-line`) is not loaded, an undefined `mode-line-active`/`mode-line-inactive` falls back to `mode-line`. Vscroll requires explicit pixelwise non-nil and a nonnegative u32 integer; other units, floats, negative clamp and fourth setter argument are rejected. `format-mode-line` (`Fformat_mode_line`, `xdisp.c:29058`) runs the same engine for GNU's string targets (`MODE_LINE_STRING`, `MODE_LINE_NOPROP`, `src/display.rs` `format_mode_line`): GNU's argument checks, the empty unibyte answer for a nil FORMAT or in batch, WINDOW selected and BUFFER current while it runs, no clipping and no `^X` forms, `%-` as `--`, each piece's `mode-line-elt-no` (GNU's global element count, restarted by a nested call) and its face: the `:propertize` face with FACE appended, else FACE, where FACE t is the window's `mode-line-active`/`mode-line-inactive` and an integer drops all properties (oracle: `tests/format_mode_line.rs` against pinned GNU in batch and, recorded by `tests/fixtures/format-mode-line-tty.py`, on an 80x24 terminal frame). A string element contributes its own `face`, `local-map`, `keymap`, `help-echo`, `romacs-segment`, `romacs-icon` and `romacs-pill`, and a `:propertize` its face and those, which win over the string's (`keymaps_and_help_reach_the_string`); `mouse-face`, `display` and every other property is dropped. The status bar's band carries the last three to its painter (VM2-STATUS-BAR); a window's band carries none. Batch is the Lisp variable `noninteractive` (GNU reads its C flag, which binding the variable does not change). Neighbouring pieces with equal properties share one interval, where GNU's concatenation keeps them apart; `equal-including-properties` cannot tell `window-vscroll` and `set-window-vscroll` answer and set in the frame's canonical lines (a float when not whole) or with PIXELS-P in pixels, truncated and never negative, as window.c does; PRESERVE-VSCROLL-P is accepted but not recorded, and a VSCROLL that is no number signals a plain `error`. | **Package-observable**: `%z`/`%Z`/`%F`/`%m` text, the modified flag after a save, and band faces from a string's anonymous face plists differ; `format-mode-line` strings lack the non-face properties above. Escapes, constructs and per-window evaluation follow GNU within the restrictions above | <!-- symbols: mode-line-format, set-window-vscroll, window-vscroll, mode-line-inactive, mode-line-in-non-selected-windows, format-mode-line -->

Once a host lays the window out, `romacs-layout2` continues and truncates rows as GNU does, showing the break in the window's right fringe or, without one, with the `\` or `$` glyph, and redisplay counts the rows that layout paints, reported with each paint: a start that shows point on a whole row outside `scroll-margin` (exempt at BEGV or with ZV shown, as `try_window` checks) stays; else, from the rows laid out around point as painting lays them out, minimal scrolling takes the start showing point just inside the margin at the top or bottom, which may begin a continuation line, and recentring follows `move_it_vertically_backward` over those rows (back by lines, then row by row to half the text area); a forced start stands with point moved to the row reaching half the text area when no whole row shows it, and scrolls as an unforced one when point is on a partly shown row or in a margin; before a window's first paint, and in a vertically scrolled window, the display iterator over the host's layout (VM2-WTPS1) chooses (`harness/lines_live.py`: C-n through a buffer of continued lines several windows tall keeps the cursor on screen); where that iterator refuses the text, rows are logical lines as before. `window-line-height` answers from the same rows, with empty rows of the default height past the text's end. Positive integer `scroll-conservatively` values choose minimum scrolling within that many lines; values above 100 always reveal minimally. The owned GUI defaults this Lisp policy to 101, so ordinary navigation does not jump to the centre; zero retains automatic centering. Refused GUI vertical movement at the first/last accessible line preserves point. `scroll-step`, `scroll-preserve-screen-position`, `make-cursor-line-fully-visible` and `auto-window-vscroll` remain ignored; a partially visible last row cannot hold point | **Observable by a package** that sets the scroll variables or relies on continuation lines; with GNU's defaults and lines narrower than the window, `window-start` after redisplay matches GNU | <!-- symbols: window-start, set-window-start, scroll-conservatively, scroll-step, scroll-margin, window-line-height -->

| SERVER2 | Server-first lifetime | GNU's `--daemon` starts with no frame on a terminal of its own; `emacsclient -c` makes frames, and the last one's deletion keeps the daemon; a plain `emacs` is a separate process | `romacs --server NAME` (the launcher's default, `scripts/romacs`) connects to server NAME's native socket (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/romacs/NAME/server.sock`, the MCP endpoint with the client methods `romacs/frame`, `romacs/eval`, `romacs/stop`), starting it as the transient user unit `romacs-server-NAME` (else detached with `setsid`) when none listens. The server (`--serve NAME`) is the GUI session with no window until a client asks; its one top-level frame is the host frame, whose window is made on request (`romacs--native-window`), and closing it (or `C-x C-c`, `save-buffers-kill-terminal`, after offering to save) keeps the session unless `romacs-server-exit-with-last-frame`; the next client shows it again. A second client while it shows asks for the focus and visits its files there: one window, not one per client (several top-level frames are VM2-WIN-SUBSTRATE's). It also runs server.el on `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/emacs/NAME` (`romacs-server-gnu-socket`). A native client waiting on files is kept out of `server-clients` in a buffer-to-waiters index; `server-edit`, `server-done` and `kill-buffer` finish its buffers with GNU's save question and recursion guard | **Visible to the user** (one process and one window across launches); Lisp sees `romacs-server-name` | `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/server_live.py` (lifecycle, waiters, unit, probe) | <!-- symbols: romacs-server-start, romacs-server-name, romacs-server-exit-with-last-frame, romacs-server-gnu-socket, romacs--native-window, server-edit, server-done, save-buffers-kill-terminal -->
| AGENT1 | Agent sessions and their tools | GNU has no agent sessions; MCP servers for GNU Emacs are packages | `rlisp/romacs-agent.el` runs a harness (Claude Code's stream-json CLI) under a supervisor of its own, `romacs-agent-host` (a transient user unit, else detached), which journals every line under `$XDG_STATE_HOME/romacs/agents/ID/`; a server attaches to it and replays the journal, so a session outlives a closed window, a server stop and a crash. The harness reaches Romacs through `romacs-mcp --reconnect`, which keeps its MCP session across server restarts: a call in flight when the server went away is answered `outcome unknown`, one made while no server listens `not executed`, a call unanswered past `romacs-agent-reply-timeout` while the editor is busy is withdrawn (`not executed` when it was still queued, else `outcome unknown`), and `romacs_call_outcome` reads the call's record in the session's call log (`calls.jsonl`, appended per state). The harness runs with the user's own configuration and permission mode; Romacs passes none and has no permission layer: an approval the harness asks for is shown in the chat (`rlisp/romacs-agent-ui.el`) and the user's answer is written back. `romacs_eval` takes `budgetMs` (default `romacs-agent-eval-budget`), a deadline that quits the call at its next safepoint as C-g does and never reaches a later command; timers, which admit tool calls, run busy, so C-g quits an agent's evaluation. Edits through `romacs_buffer_edit` and `romacs_file_write` are one undo group each and keep the file's earlier content once per session | **Visible to the user** (the agents dashboard and chat buffers); Lisp sees `romacs-agent-*` and the tools | `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/agent_live.py` (session, survive, outcome, budget, deadline, unit, probe), `fake_harness.py` | <!-- symbols: romacs-agent-start, romacs-agent-send, romacs-agent-interrupt, romacs-agent-respond, romacs-agent-stop, romacs-agent-resume, romacs-agent-restore, romacs-agents, romacs-agent-eval-budget, romacs-agent-reply-timeout, romacs--call-deadline -->

| VM2-SCAN1 | syntax.c scanning engine | `src/syntax.c` `forw_comment`, `back_comment`, `Fforward_comment`, `scan_lists`, `scan_sexps_forward` read each character's syntax through gl_state, whose interval bookkeeping can lag the scan position in rare backward-then-forward property cases; `parse_sexp_propertize` and `find_defun_start` call Lisp mid-scan | `crates/romacs-vm/src/syntax_scan.rs` transcribes the scans with every comment style, nesting, fence, escape and prefix flag, and looks syntax up at the exact position (GNU's intent). | VM2-EV2 | GNU printing subset (`prin1`, `princ`, `print`, `prin1-to-string`) | `print.c:738-915`, `print_object` support all GNU objects, controls and graphs. | VM2 now honors list/vector length, list level, quote/function shorthand, newline/control/multibyte escapes, supported overrides and shared/cyclic cons/vector/nonempty-string numbering with `print-circle`. NaN payloads are preserved by these entry points. Without `print-circle`, cyclic lists and vectors still refuse instead of GNU's `#0` ancestor notation; depth over 100 refuses. Hash-table print-length refuses; circular/shared hash-table traversal is not modeled. Backquote/comma shorthand and full GNU symbol-number recognition remain incomplete. Obarrays, text-property strings and fresh/uniform character tables are represented, including property-value cycles with `print-circle`; sparse/historical table layout remains VM2-CT1. Markers print as print.c does (`#<marker at N in BUFFER>`, `(moves after insertion)`, `in no buffer`); other unsupported structural layouts (syntax tables, overlays, frames, terminals) still refuse; ordinary compiled closures print opaquely as `#<interpreted-function>`, without exposing source, capture cells or array slots. The owned cl-print method delegates to this native printer. Existing frame/terminal diagnostic representations remain VM2-FRAME-PRINT. | Partial serialization contract, not full GNU printing parity. Existing circular-printer pins in other corpora remain distinct from the new `tools/primh/printing-specs` finite live-GNU matrix. | <!-- symbols: prin1, princ, print, prin1-to-string, print-circle, print-escape-newlines, print-length, print-level -->
| VM2-PRINTSTREAM1 | Output destinations and callback timing | GNU `print.c:print_prepare`, `Fterpri` and `printchar` accept buffer/marker streams, preserve point, run modification hooks and output objects incrementally. | Romacs emits to buffer streams, markers in multibyte buffers, rooted T2 callback functions/symbols and batch stdout. `t` is the echo area, as on GNU's terminal, only where `noninteractive` is nil and the embedder declares it renders one (`Vm::set_echo_area_host`: the live GUI); a print there extends the message printing made, and any other message starts a new one (`message_buf_print`). Every other host, `noninteractive` or not, prints `t` toBuffer/marker output follows GNU point motion and restores the current buffer on errors; unibyte-buffer markers explicitly refuse. Output that runs no Lisp is inserted natively and recorded in `buffer-undo-list`; when change hooks or `undo-auto--undoable-change` can run it goes through `insert`'s bracket (`romacs--print-insert`), as print_finish's insert_1_both and signal_after_change do, including the after-change of empty output. `terpri` with non-nil ENSURE refuses, including non-function streams (only the function refusal matches GNU). Rendering is snapshotted before callbacks, so callback mutation of remaining graph/print controls is not reflected. Quoted non-ASCII unibyte strings to callback/stdout streams refuse; `princ` raw strings retain their actual character codes. `write-char` matches GNU uint32 callback conversion, but unsupported stdout character codes refuse. | Buffer/marker comparisons are in `tests/printing_streams.rs` and `tests/print_change_hooks.rs`; ENSURE and incremental callback mutation parity remain outside the supported boundary. | <!-- symbols: prin1, princ, print, terpri, write-char, standard-output -->
