Each measured exception is pinned in the new printing corpus. | <!-- symbols: prin1, princ, print, prin1-to-string, float-output-format, print-gensym, print-continuous-numbering, print-number-table, print-integers-as-characters, print-unreadable-function, print-charset-text-property, print-symbols-bare -->
| VM2-PRINTUNREAD1 | When `print-unreadable-function` is called | `print_vectorlike_unreadable` (`src/print.c:1700`) calls it as the print reaches each unreadable object (a buffer, marker, window, frame, process, overlay, obarray, primitive...), with the variable bound to nil, and prints its string, nothing, or the object as usual. A print the function itself makes shares the outer print's ` prin1` buffer: in pinned GNU, `(let ((print-unreadable-function (lambda (_o _e) (prin1-to-string "inner") 'skip))) (prin1-to-string (list 1 (make-marker) 2)))` is `"(1 ner\" 2)"` | `prin1`, `princ`, `print` and `prin1-to-string` first list the unreadable objects their print meets (a Rust pass that runs no Lisp), call the function on each in that order (`romacs--print-unreadable`, `rlisp/vm/prelude.el`, so a `throw` from it leaves the print as in GNU), then print with the answers. The calls see neither the print's OVERRIDES bound (GNU's see, say, `print-length` as `(length . 3)` sets it) nor, under `print-circle` with `print-continuous-numbering`, the labels the print is assigning in `print-number-table`. The function's own prints never leak into the outer one: the example is `"(1  2)"`. `format`'s `%S` and `%s` print unreadable objects as usual, and a print that cannot call Lisp (an error message, `%S` of a propertied string) refuses when it meets one while the function is set | **Package-observable, rarely:** the function runs before any of the print's output is emitted, so a stream callback or buffer stream sees the output after all the calls rather than interleaved with them; `readablep`, `cl-print` and help's value printing are unaffected (`tests/journey_primitives.rs`, GNU's `print-tests.el`) | <!-- symbols: print-unreadable-function, readablep, prin1, prin1-to-string, princ, print -->

| VM2-BUFNAME-RAW1 | Buffer names with raw bytes | A buffer name is any Lisp string; `(get-buffer-create "\\351x.txt")` names the buffer with the raw byte, which `buffer-name`, `get-buffer`, `rename-buffer` and `generate-new-buffer-name` round-trip and the mode line and `#<buffer ...>` show as `\\351` | VM2 keys buffers by a Rust string, so a raw-byte character of byte B stands in the model's name as the character U+10FF00+B (`crates/romacs-vm/src/buffer.rs` `buffer_name_text`/`buffer_name_value`); the name primitives convert at the Lisp boundary and the printer and mode line show `\\NNN`. | VM2-PROC1 | Subprocesses, waits and timers | process.c and callproc.c: process objects (`make-process`, `make-pipe-process`, `make-network-process` for every socket kind, `make-serial-process`), filters and sentinels run by `wait_reading_process_output` in every wait (`accept-process-output`, `sleep-for`, `sit-for`, and the command loop's idle wait), which also runs ordinary and idle timers (keyboard.c `timer_check`); SIGCHLD wakes the wait; `call-process` reads its output incrementally, with DISPLAY redisplaying | Process objects, `make-process` (pipes or a pty), `make-pipe-process`, `call-process`, `call-process-region`, signals, filters, sentinels and the wait are GNU's transcribed (`crates/romacs-vm/src/subprocess.rs`, `prims/process.rs`, `rlisp/vm/subprocess.el`). Limits, each an explicit refusal or a stated difference: `make-network-process` makes TCP stream servers and clients only, refusing datagrams, local sockets, `:local`/`:remote` addresses, numeric families, non-numeric service names, `:tls-parameters` and `:use-external-socket`; a `:nowait` client connects without blocking and the wait reports its outcome (the `open` sentinel or `failed`, after trying each further address) as GNU does; `make-serial-process`, `process-attributes`, `list-system-processes`, `set-process-thread` and the datagram functions are void. Lisp waits never read the keyboard. Timers are keyboard.c's `timer_check` in Lisp (`rlisp/vm/subprocess.el`), shared by those waits, the GUI host's command loop and its key reads (prompts, and `read-event` with a time limit, hence `sit-for`): ordinary timers run as they fall due; while the GUI waits idle for a key (`timer_start_idle` until the next key press, `timer_stop_idle`), idle timers run too and `current-idle-time` is the idle duration; Lisp a command runs is never idle, so its waits run idle timers only inside a timer that itself runs while idle, as GNU's. The command loop's idle wait and the host's key reads (`romacs--read-host-char`) also read the processes' ready output, finish their connections and report their status changes (`romacs--process-idle`), as read_char's wait_reading_process_output does with the keyboard; the command loop redisplays after any timer ran or process output came and otherwise blocks until the next timer, key, ready process descriptor (`Vm::process_wait_fds`, `ProcessWatch`) or native wake. `make-network-process` is provided with the subfeatures it supports: `(:server t)`, `(:service t)`, `(:family ipv6)`, `(:family ipv4)` and `(:nowait t)`. `call-process` reads all output before inserting it, so DISPLAY never redisplays; a quit does not kill its child. Exited children are found by a polled `waitpid` and pidfds, not SIGCHLD; children a dropped VM leaves are reaped by the host's one shared reaper thread where GNU's exit leaves them to init. ID)`, event format and errors are GNU's in Lisp (`rlisp/vm/inotify.el`, installed with the process prelude), beside the special-event queue that keyboard.c's `insert-special-event` stores into and the keyboard waits drain. Different: (1) the kernel queue is the keyboard buffer: records are read only when a keyboard wait handles them (the GUI's idle loop, `romacs--read-host-char`, batch reads per VM2-KBD1), so a watch removed before then drops its pending events, and `inotify-valid-p` stays t until the IN_IGNORED of a deleted file is read; (2) `input-pending-p` does not see pending file notification events, so they never cut a redisplay or `while-no-input` short (GNU lists `file-notify` in `while-no-input-ignore-events`, defined with GNU's value less the D-Bus and thread events Romacs lacks); (3) an error in a callback run while the GUI idles is reported as the command loop reports one, and the events after it stay queued; (4) `report_file_notify_error`'s text for an unknown aspect or invalid descriptor is always "Invalid argument" where GNU prints whatever `errno` held | **Package-observable** only through (1)'s timing and (2); filenotify.el, autorevert, eglot and the rest use it unmodified. `crates/romacs-vm/tests/inotify.rs` equals pinned GNU 32.0.50 on the same steps; GNU's `filenotify-tests.el` and `inotify-tests.el` under real ERT | GNU's backend over the existing keyboard waits; no second event queue | <!-- symbols: inotify-add-watch, inotify-rm-watch, inotify-valid-p, insert-special-event, file-notify-handle-event, special-event-map, while-no-input-ignore-events -->
| VM2-THREAD1 | Lisp threads | The pinned GNU is built with threads (`THREADS_ENABLED`): `make-thread`, mutexes and condition variables (`src/thread.c`), `(featurep 'threads)`, and `main-thread` the main thread's object; a build without threads keeps `main-thread` and binds it to nil (`thread.c:1307-1312`), and url-http.el keys its connection pool on it (`url-http-find-free-connection`) | VM2 has no thread objects and does not provide `threads`, so `main-thread` is the threadless build's nil: url-http keys connections by host and port, as GNU without threads does | **Package-observable**: `main-thread`, `(featurep 'threads)` and the thread primitives' absence. `crates/romacs-vm-gui/tests/url_retrieve.rs` matches pinned GNU through the connection pool | <!-- symbols: main-thread, current-thread, make-thread -->
| VM2-PROC2 | `undecided` decoding of process output | detect_coding chooses the decoding of output read with an `undecided` coding system from the bytes and `coding-system-priority-list`, possibly a non-UTF-8 system | VM2 runs the coding owner's detection (`coding_detected_system`, the one `insert-file-contents` uses) over the output (`prim/leaf/process.rs` `decode_output`): ASCII output keeps the undecided coding system, and other bytes decode as the system detection chooses, e.g. iso-latin-1 for `\351x`, as GNU does; bytes an explicitly UTF-8 coding system cannot decode become raw-byte characters (VM2-RAWTEXT1) | None known | <!-- symbols: accept-process-output, call-process, make-process -->
| VM2-DISPLAY1 | Display queries (`display-graphic-p`, `display-color-cells`, `display-pixel-width`, `display-monitor-attributes-list`, ...), colors (`color-values`, `color-defined-p`, `defined-colors`) and `frame-set-background-mode` | frame.el and faces.el dispatch on the frame type to `x-`, `w32-`, `ns-`, `pgtk-`, `haiku-`, `android-` and `tty-` C primitives; a batch session answers for its initial terminal, approximating colors to 8 tty colors (`tty-colors.el`) | rlisp/romacs-frame.el answers every one of them from one capability record, `romacs-display`. Without a GUI the record is pinned GNU's batch answers (not graphic, 0 color cells, 3 planes, `mono`, `dark`, the 8 standard colors), so package tests see GNU's batch values; colors keep tty-colors.el's names and approximation over the record's palette. No `tty-*` terminal primitives exist. A frame realizes no fonts (VM2-WIN5), so `face-font` is nil, except that on a graphic record the frame's font is the default face's: `(face-font 'default)` is the frame's `font` parameter, as GNU's is until that face's font changes, and simple.el's `default-font-height` and `default-font-width` then answer the frame's character size without `font-info` | **Package-observable** in the GUI until it sets the record: graphic-only features stay off. Frame geometry, edges, z-order and mouse-position queries are not yet owned | <!-- symbols: display-graphic-p, display-color-cells, display-color-p, display-grayscale-p, display-planes, display-visual-class, display-pixel-width, display-pixel-height, display-mm-width, display-mm-height, display-monitor-attributes-list, color-values, color-defined-p, defined-colors, tty-color-alist, frame-set-background-mode, romacs-display -->

Nil in batch and without a graphic `romacs-display` record (VM2-WIN5) | **Package-observable** through defface `(supports ...)` clauses (`italic`, `underline`, `completion-preview-common`); matches pinned GNU (X11) on 27 queries in a GUI session. | VM2-WINSYS1 | Window-system identity | A GNU build provides its window system as a C feature (`pgtk` from `pgtkterm.c:7550` in pinned GNU, a PGTK build, also in batch), loadup.el preloads that system's files (`loadup.el:357`: `pgtk-dnd`, `touch-screen`, `term/common-win`, `term/pgtk-win`), and a frame on that display has `window-system` `pgtk`, which frame.el, faces.el and select.el dispatch on to `pgtk-` C primitives | Romacs takes pinned GNU's build identity: the loadup bootstrap provides `pgtk` and preloads the same four files (`rlisp/romacs-loadup-bootstrap.el`), so `term/pgtk-win.el` and GNU code testing `(featurep 'pgtk)` load as in GNU. Romacs's display is its own Wayland-native one, described by `romacs-display` (VM2-DISPLAY1): `window-system` stays nil on every frame (VM2-WIN5) and no `pgtk-` C primitive exists, so the `(eq window-system 'pgtk)` paths never run. `mwheel` is not preloaded (loadup.el's `x-create-frame` test) | **Package-observable:** `(featurep 'pgtk)` is t; code that calls a `pgtk-` primitive on that test alone, without checking `window-system`, signals `void-function` (none in the GNU tree: `select.el` only adds to a hook, `browse-url.el` also checks `(window-system)`) | <!-- symbols: featurep, window-system, pgtk-initialize-window-system -->
| VM2-PROC1 | Subprocesses, waits and timers | process.c and callproc.c: process objects (`make-process`, `make-pipe-process`, `make-network-process` for every socket kind, `make-serial-process`), filters and sentinels run by `wait_reading_process_output` in every wait (`accept-process-output`, `sleep-for`, `sit-for`, and the command loop's idle wait), which also runs ordinary and idle timers (keyboard.c `timer_check`); SIGCHLD wakes the wait; `call-process` reads its output incrementally, with DISPLAY redisplaying | Process objects, `make-process` (pipes or a pty), `make-pipe-process`, `call-process`, `call-process-region`, signals, filters, sentinels and the wait are GNU's transcribed (`crates/romacs-vm/src/subprocess.rs`, `prims/process.rs`, `rlisp/vm/subprocess.el`). Limits, each an explicit refusal or a stated difference: `make-network-process` makes TCP and local (Unix domain) stream servers and clients, refusing datagrams, Internet `:local`/`:remote` addresses (a string address is a local socket's path, as GNU's), numeric families, non-numeric service names and `:tls-parameters`; `:use-external-socket` names the socket a daemon was started with, of which there is none; a `:nowait` client connects without blocking and the wait reports its outcome (the `open` sentinel or `failed`, after trying each further address) as GNU does, and one whose connect fails at once has GNU's `(failed ERROR)` status and no sentinel; an accepted connection's name, contact and `open from` sentinel are GNU's for each family (a local peer has host `t`, no service and a connection-counter name); `delete-process` leaves a local server's socket file, as GNU does (server.el and `romacs-mcp-stop` delete theirs); `format-network-address` is Lisp (`rlisp/vm/subprocess.el`); `make-serial-process`, `process-attributes`, `list-system-processes`, `set-process-thread` and the datagram functions are void. Lisp waits never read the keyboard. Timers are keyboard.c's `timer_check` in Lisp (`rlisp/vm/subprocess.el`), shared by those waits, the GUI host's command loop and its key reads (prompts, and `read-event` with a time limit, hence `sit-for`): ordinary timers run as they fall due; while the GUI waits idle for a key (`timer_start_idle` until the next key press, `timer_stop_idle`), idle timers run too and `current-idle-time` is the idle duration; Lisp a command runs is never idle, so its waits run idle timers only inside a timer that itself runs while idle, as GNU's. The command loop's idle wait and the host's key reads (`romacs--read-host-char`) also read the processes' ready output, finish their connections and report their status changes (`romacs--process-idle`), as read_char's wait_reading_process_output does with the keyboard; the command loop redisplays after any timer ran or process output came and otherwise blocks until the next timer, key, ready process descriptor (`Vm::process_wait_fds`, `ProcessWatch`) or native wake. `make-network-process` is provided with the subfeatures it supports: `(:server t)`, `(:service t)`, `(:family ipv6)`, `(:family ipv4)`, `(:family local)` and `(:nowait t)`. `call-process` reads all output before inserting it, so DISPLAY never redisplays; a quit does not kill its child. Exited children are found by a polled `waitpid` and pidfds, not SIGCHLD; children a dropped VM leaves are reaped by the host's one shared reaper thread where GNU's exit leaves them to init. Process output is decoded per VM2-PROC2 | **Package-observable:** asynchronous network code beyond TCP and local stream sockets (datagrams, TLS, serial ports) cannot run; batch runs have no keyboard wait, so idle timers never run there and `current-idle-time` is nil (keyboard.c:4949 answers nil whenever Emacs is not idle); process filters and sentinels run while the GUI waits for a key, never during a command except inside its Lisp waits, as GNU's | <!-- symbols: current-idle-time, run-with-idle-timer, make-process, make-pipe-process, make-network-process, make-serial-process, accept-process-output, call-process, call-process-region, process-attributes, list-system-processes, set-process-thread, sleep-for -->

The bar is every root-frame window's mode line: no ordinary window of that frame draws a band (a child frame's windows keep theirs, as GNU's), so `window-mode-line-height` is 0 and each body is a line taller than GNU's, in either look (`romacs-classic-chrome` keeps the bar); a batch frame has no bar, so GNU's bands and geometry stand there and GNU's tests do not move. Unlike GNU's, no window's point is installed in its buffer: an `:eval` form reading `(point)` for another window sees its buffer's point. A nil or `none` format contributes nothing. The bar is expanded again only when GNU's redisplay would update a mode line: `force-mode-line-update` or `set-buffer-modified-p`, another window, buffer, buffer name or format, the buffer's text or modified state, the window's point, start or size, the narrowing, the minibuffer's state, faces or the bar's columns, or any of these for a folded window; a variable set without either call shows at the next such change, as in GNU. Then, while visible, each other ordinary window of the root frame whose own format differs from the default (a buffer-local `mode-line-format` such as keymap-popup's, or a `mode-line-format` window parameter), in the frame's order: its line, expanded the same way, after ` │ `, on `mode-line-inactive`'s background, its `right-align` markers plain text, a `context` segment dropped with its separator first when the bar is too narrow. The bar is painted over that text: a 3-pixel block of the accent (`romacs-pane-focus`) at its left edge, dimmed while a child frame's window holds the focus; each `romacs-segment` run named by a `romacs-pill` face drawn on a rounded background of that face's colour; a character a `romacs-icon` alist names drawn as that icon while Romacs Icons is there, or dropped where it names `none`; segments with a `romacs-priority` dropped, lowest first, until the text fits; the part after a `right-align` segment flush with the right edge and whole, the part before it clipped where that starts (`romacs-status-bar-string` shows the same, spaces between). A mouse button released on the bar runs the binding at the clicked character of the window whose line it is (`romacs--status-bar-click`, `romacs--status-bar-target`): the `local-map` or `keymap` there in the bar's text as last painted, else the global map's, under `[mode-line BUTTON]`, with a `mode-line` position in that window whose object is that text and column; the press runs nothing (so `[mode-line down-mouse-1] mouse-drag-mode-line` never starts), wheel and drag are not delivered; the text carries the properties the mode line projects (VM2-G1B: `local-map`, `keymap`, `help-echo`), so a package reading another from `posn-string` sees nil; the `right-align` marker's room and a separator run nothing; while a prompt reads input the GUI host delivers no pointer event to Lisp. Layers, the completion panel and toasts keep clear of the bar: above one at the bottom (`romacs-chrome-bottom`), below one at the top (`romacs-chrome-top`), restacked when it moves. Nothing in the bar animates yet: a segment's change shows at once (a pulse belongs to the pane-animation work) | **User-observable** (one mode line per frame) and **package-observable** through `frame-root-window`'s height, `window-mode-line-height` 0 and the taller bodies of every root-frame window on a window system | One line describes the window being worked in, and the windows keep all their rows; GNU's mode-line API, not its per-window rendering | <!-- symbols: romacs-status-bar-window, romacs-status-bar-string, romacs-chrome-bottom, romacs--status-bar-click, romacs--status-bar-target, romacs--status-bar-text, romacs-status-bar-height, romacs--mode-line-click, romacs-status-bar-position, romacs-chrome-top --> |

Showing, moving, resizing or hiding a child frame never resizes a window of its parent or a terminal; window configurations are the host frame's. A child frame's cursor is drawn only while one of its windows is selected. A child frame shows its parent's faces, what `x-create-frame-with-faces` computes for a frame on the same display, so `copy-face` and `face-attribute` work on it; the bare primitive therefore answers for named faces where GNU's bare `x-create-frame` has only `default` (`tests/child_frames.rs` `a_child_frame_has_its_parents_faces`). It shares them, rather than copying them, until a face change on it alone would make it differ (`internal-set-lisp-face-attribute`, `internal-make-lisp-face` or `internal-copy-lisp-face` with the frame); then it gets its own copy, as GNU's frame has from the start. While it shares, `face-spec-recalc` there does nothing (romacs-frame.el advice), a change for all frames reaches it through its parent, `frame--face-hash-table` is the parent's, and its `foreground-color` and `background-color` parameters, unless set, are the parent's (`romacs--frame-shares-faces-p`; `a_child_frame_shares_its_parents_faces_until_one_is_set_on_it`). A theme or a `defface` therefore recomputes faces on one frame however many layers are open; GNU recomputes them on every frame, child frames included. The display realizes every frame's faces from the root frame's: an edit on a child frame alone is not mirrored into the display's faces. A child frame's `background-color` colours only its display: unlike GNU's (xfaces.c `update_face_from_frame_parameter`), setting it leaves the frame's default face, so a glass layer keeps sharing its parent's faces; on the root frame, as in GNU, a new `foreground-color` or `background-color` becomes the default face's, and a new default background recomputes the background mode (`frame-set-background-mode`). Text with an unspecified background on a child frame shows its `background-color` (frame.c `gui_set_background_color`); `face-remapping-alist` is not applied to any frame yet. Romacs's own parameters `romacs-layer-radius`, `romacs-layer-glow` and `romacs-layer-shadow` round a layer's corners (leaving its border as unpainted padding) and light it (VM2-ECHO-LAYER, VM2-MINI-COMPLETION-LAYER). Pointer events over a child frame are not delivered to it (the GUI host delivers no pointer events to Lisp yet), descendants are deleted without their own `delete-frame-functions`, and `window-size-change-functions` and `window-configuration-change-hook` do not run for a child frame (Romacs runs `window-size-change-functions` from redisplay when a window's size changed, as GNU does: `rlisp/vm/window-change.el`) | **Package-observable:** posframe-style and keymap-popup's child-frame code runs and paints (`harness/popup_live.py --child-frame`, `--which-key` through `romacs-layer.el`), but a top-level frame cannot be made, a popup can be smaller than GNU allows, and it cannot be clicked | A layer over the frame that never resizes the windows or terminals below it is the UI direction; one top-level frame is what the host displays | <!-- symbols: make-frame, romacs--create-frame, romacs--frame-shares-faces-p, face-spec-recalc, delete-frame, frame-parent, frame-ancestor-p, frame-position, set-frame-position, set-frame-size, set-frame-width, set-frame-height, frame-text-width, frame-text-height, frame-text-cols, frame-text-lines, frame-total-cols, frame-total-lines, redirect-frame-focus, frame-focus, frame--set-was-invisible, frame-after-make-frame, set-window-fringes, set-window-scroll-bars, select-frame, visible-frame-list, raise-frame, lower-frame -->
| VM2-FRAME-MINI | Saved minibuffer selection is unsupported | GNU `minibuffer-selected-window` (`src/window.c:7153`) returns the live saved selection only during an active, selected minibuffer; `src/minibuf.c:827` updates it on real recursive entry | Unregistered: direct calls and funcall signal `(void-function minibuffer-selected-window)` rather than inventing nil from a depth counter | Package-observable refusal, not matching GNU's successful result. The overlay has a lexical calling-window but no native recursive-entry/unwind selection ownership | Preserve this refusal until the actual minibuffer lifecycle publishes and restores a traced selection; live GNU/VM evidence in `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/frame-refusals.json` | <!-- symbols: minibuffer-selected-window -->
| VM2-FRAME-VIS | The host frame's visibility is not modelled | GNU `frame-visible-p`, `make-frame-visible`, `make-frame-invisible`, `iconify-frame`, `raise-frame` and `lower-frame` (`src/frame.c:3505-3700`) track and change a frame's visibility through its terminal; a batch terminal frame always reads visible and ignores the requests | VM2's host frame always reads visible, `visible-frame-list` lists it, the requests change nothing (so does `x-focus-frame`, which frame.el's `select-frame-set-input-focus` asks of a multi-frame display: the host's one window has the focus whenever the session does), and making the sole frame invisible without FORCE signals GNU's error. `tests/frame_visibility.rs` matches pinned GNU batch. A child frame's visibility is modelled: `make-frame-visible`, `make-frame-invisible` and a `visibility` parameter show and hide it, `frame-visible-p` and `visible-frame-list` report it, and `raise-frame` and `lower-frame` restack it among its siblings (VM2-CHILD-FRAME) | **Package-observable** only in the GUI, where iconifying or hiding the window is not performed | Model visibility with the GUI backend's window state when it owns frame lifecycle (VM2-WIN-SUBSTRATE) | <!-- symbols: frame-visible-p, make-frame-visible, make-frame-invisible, iconify-frame, raise-frame, lower-frame, x-focus-frame, visible-frame-list -->
| VM2-TERM-GEOMETRY | Terminal pty size under a grown mini-window | `term.el` and eat resize their pty through `window-adjust-process-window-size-function` on every window size change, so a minibuffer that grows for fido-vertical or icomplete candidates shrinks the shell's grid and restores it on exit (pinned GNU `-nw` with `term` in a 40-row tty: `stty size` 37, then 29 during `C-x C-f`, then 37) | `romacs-term`'s pty follows its controlling window's stable body: its size with a one-line mini-window, or with none while a layer hosts the minibuffer (VM2-MINI-COMPLETION-LAYER) (`window.rs` `stable_window_body`), less its bottom divider (VM2-PANE-CHROME), reconciled at every geometry publication for every terminal (`terminal_host.rs` `reconcile_terminal_geometry`). The controlling window is the selected window (the one a minibuffer was entered from, while it is selected) when it shows the terminal, else the first window showing it in tree order; a hidden terminal keeps its size. The pty changes size on a host resize, a split, a delete or a switch of controlling window, never for the mini-window. While a grown mini-window covers the grid's bottom rows, the window shows the grid from the row that keeps the cursor's row last, and from the grid's first row again once restored. `tests/terminal_host.rs`; `harness/terminal_live.py --prompt-grow` | **User-observable** (the shell sees no SIGWINCH for a prompt, as in Alacritty), and to a package only through `stty size`/`LINES` in the child | A shell redraws on every resize; following transient mini growth made the prompt jump and, with resizes driven from stale geometry, stick at the wrong row | <!-- symbols: romacs-term -->
| VM2-TERM-KEYS | Keys in `romacs-term` | GNU's `term` in char mode binds every key in `term-raw-map` to `term-send-raw` but its escape character (C-c), and sends from the command loop; its line mode edits the line in the buffer. vterm and eat send from their mode maps too, less an exception list (`vterm-keymap-exceptions`). | Every key goes through the command loop. While a window shows the terminal with point on its cursor, a generated keymap sends each key's xterm bytes (`romacs--term-send-event`, `terminal_keys.rs`), except `romacs-term-keymap-exceptions` (C-c C-x C-u C-g C-h C-l M-x M-o C-y M-y C-v M-v), which keep their Emacs bindings; on the alternate screen only `romacs-term-alternate-exceptions` (C-x M-x C-g) do. The keymap is the buffer's local map for one key sequence, chosen when its first event is read (`romacs--sequence-start-functions`) and only over `romacs-term-mode-map`, so a prefix keeps its route whatever arrives before its next key, minor modes and overriding maps come first (as vterm's local map), a local map another installs (a confirmation's answers) is kept, and `romacs-term-mode-map` bindings win over the sent keys. A command that moves a window's point off the cursor, or its start off the grid, leaves that window a plain read-only buffer that output does not move (`terminal_host.rs` `terminal_track`); typing there puts it back on the cursor and sends the key. C-y sends `current-kill` as Alacritty pastes (bracketed and less ESC and ETX when the program asks, else with line ends as RET); M-y chooses a kill and sends it, replacing nothing; C-c C-c and C-c C-k send ETX. Escape reaches every buffer as the function key `escape`, made ESC by `function-key-map` where unbound, as GNU's X frames deliver it. In the workspace (no GNU Lisp), whose key lookup has only local and global maps, C-u's digits reach the program. `tests/terminal_mode.rs`, `tests/terminal_loadup.rs`, `tests/terminal_host.rs`, `tests/terminal_keys_parity.rs`; `harness/terminal_live.py`; `harness/terminal_latency_live.py` | **User-observable**: a terminal is one buffer, with no line or char mode; a package sees `romacs-term-mode-map`, the exception lists and the per-sequence local map. A plain passthrough key (one the command loop would only send) is written by the host after a native lookup through the same keymaps in GNU's precedence (`terminal_host.rs` `terminal_passthrough_key`), without the command loop: global `pre-command-hook` and `post-command-hook` do not run for it, nor do `this-command`, `last-command-event` or `recent-keys` change, since its effect is in the pty, not the buffer (vterm and eat run Lisp per key); a buffer-local command hook, a keyboard macro, an input method, an overriding, emulation, minor-mode or text-property map that binds the key, or a remapping sends it through the loop. Measured (`harness/terminal_latency_live.py`, `--loadup`, 5 pairs of 250 keys): ingress to the byte in the pty about 0.05 ms slower at p50 than the host router it replaces (0.37 against 0.42 ms, base spread 46%), no added frame; the native check costs about 3 us hot but about 50 us in a session, where each key, 30 ms after the last, finds its keymaps and variables out of cache. | One mode with Emacs keys everywhere (vterm's model) and the user's keymaps honoured; the host router it replaces sent keys before any keymap saw them. | <!-- symbols: romacs-term, romacs-term-mode-map, romacs-term-keymap-exceptions, romacs-term-alternate-exceptions, romacs-term-update-keymaps -->
| VM2-ORDERLESS | The `orderless` completion style ships with Romacs, matched natively | GNU has no `orderless` style; users install orderless.el from GNU ELPA, which matches each component's regexp through `all-completions` and `completion-regexp-list` and highlights with `string-match` | `rlisp/romacs-orderless.el`, a port of orderless e271a8e (provenance in the file), registers `orderless` in `completion-styles-alist` with orderless's options, dispatchers, faces and public functions; `orderless-compile` still returns `(PREDICATE . REGEXPS)`. A component whose styles are builtin (`orderless-literal`, `-literal-prefix`, `-regexp` without special characters, `-flex`, `-initialism`, `-prefixes`) is matched natively (`src/orderless.rs`, `romacs--orderless-filter`, `romacs--orderless-highlight`), with the same matches and highlighted groups as its regexp under GNU's search; any other component, and initialism or prefixes over non-ASCII candidates, goes through its regexp. With no predicate, a collection's candidates, and those of `read-file-name-internal` or `completion-file-name-table` (kept while the directory's modification time, link count and inode are unchanged), are listed once and filtered natively; with a predicate the table filters through `completion-regexp-list` as orderless does. Highlighting follows `completion-lazy-hilit-p`. `tests/orderless_oracle.rs` (pinned GNU with orderless.el e271a8e) | To a package: `orderless` is a style without installing orderless; the feature is `romacs-orderless`, so an installed orderless.el replaces it by its own registration; `orderless--highlight` leaves the match data alone; `orderless--compile` and `orderless--compile-component` return matchers beside the regexps; a file listing that changes without changing its directory's attributes is not seen until the next one does | Per-key matching over thousands of candidates in Lisp and regexps cost most of a completion key; Romacs matches orderless in Rust | <!-- symbols: orderless-all-completions orderless-try-completion orderless-compile orderless-filter orderless--highlight -->
| VM2-FZF | The `fzf` completion style ships with Romacs, matched natively | GNU has no `fzf` style; its `flex` style matches subsequences and ranks by a Gotoh cost through `completion--adjust-metadata` | `rlisp/romacs-fzf.el` registers `fzf` in `completion-styles-alist` (interactive sessions complete with it by default: VM2-FZF-DEFAULT). The query is fzf's extended syntax (space-separated AND terms, `'exact`, `'boundary'`, `^prefix`, `suffix$`, `^equal$`, `!negation`, `|` OR, `\ ` for a space), each term matched and scored by fzf's algorithms (`src/fzf.rs`, a port of fzf 26ff936d, MIT: FuzzyMatchV2 with V1 past fzf's slab, ExactMatchNaive/Boundary, Prefix/Suffix/EqualMatch). `romacs-fzf-smart-case` (default t): a term with an upper-case letter respects case, others ignore it; nil: `completion-ignore-case` decides; folding through the buffer's case-canon table. Matches come best first (score, then trimmed length, then table order); `completion--adjust-metadata` composes a table's own `display-sort-function`/`cycle-sort-function` with that rank as `flex`'s does. Candidates are listed as orderless lists them (`romacs-completion-candidates`, the file table cache) and copied into Rust buffers; from `romacs-fzf-parallel-threshold` (2000) candidates, scoring is split across threads with the same order. Highlighting (`romacs-fzf-match`) follows `completion-lazy-hilit-p`. Try-completion: the prefix completion when there is one, else the one match, else the input. `tests/fzf_oracle.rs` (pinned GNU: `flex` for single fuzzy terms, the terms' meaning for extended queries, boundaries, file tables); fzf's algo/pattern tests in `src/fzf.rs` | To a package: `fzf` is a style without installing anything; fzf's `--tac`, `--scheme` other than default and Latin normalisation are not offered; a typed tab stays a tab in a term (fzf turns it into a space); a pass whose completion generation advances signals `romacs-completion-stale` | Romacs offers fzf matching beside orderless; per-key scoring over thousands of candidates belongs in Rust | <!-- symbols: romacs-fzf-all-completions romacs-fzf-try-completion romacs-completion-candidates -->
| VM2-FZF-DEFAULT | Interactive sessions complete with `fzf` first, by deliberate default | GNU's `completion-styles` is `(basic partial-completion emacs22)`, `completion-category-overrides` nil, and `fido-mode` completes with `(flex)` in its minibuffers, with `flex` put first in each `completion-category-defaults` entry | An interactive `--loadup` session (with or without `-Q`) makes these standard values before the user's init (`rlisp/romacs-completion-defaults.el`): `completion-styles` `(fzf basic partial-completion emacs22)`, GNU's styles answering where fzf finds nothing (`/u/l/b`, a prefix TAB); `completion-category-overrides` `((file (styles fzf partial-completion)) (project-file (styles fzf substring)))`, GNU's `project-file` entry with fzf first; `completion-category-defaults` is GNU's, untouched. In a fido minibuffer, while the global `completion-styles` begins with `fzf`, the minibuffer completes with that value and with `fzf` where fido put `flex` (`romacs-completion--fido-fzf`); with any other styles fido's `flex` stays. `custom-reevaluate-setting` and Custom show the values as standard. Batch and noninteractive runs (`--batch`, ERT, the GNU corpus) never load the file and keep GNU's values. Parity harnesses that load the file into GNU pin GNU's completion in both engines (`parity_live.GNU_COMPLETION`). `crates/romacs-vm-gui/tests/gnu_completion.rs` | **User-observable**: M-x, C-x C-f and every prompt rank fzf's way and take fzf's query syntax. To restore GNU's: `(setopt completion-styles '(basic partial-completion emacs22) completion-category-overrides nil)` in the init; setting `completion-styles` to anything not starting with `fzf` also returns fido to `flex` | Deliberate Romacs default: fzf matching is a Romacs feature, on by default where people type | <!-- symbols: completion-styles completion-category-overrides romacs-completion--fido-fzf -->
| VM2-TEXT-ANIMATION | The display animates a block of buffer text itself | GNU shows a buffer's text as it is; an animation rewrites the text from a Lisp timer, one redisplay per frame | `rlisp/romacs-animate.el` keeps each animation's block of a window's cells (rows by columns from a visual row) in `romacs-animate--elements`; every frame the evaluator freezes carries the entries whose window still shows their buffer (`Vm::text_animations`), and the render thread draws exactly the frame's animations, keeping the clock, the motion time and the frames, over its copy of that frame at every draw (`romacs_display_protocol::text_animation`, `render_thread/text_animations.rs`): no Lisp runs and no redisplay happens per frame, and a frame that no longer shows the buffer draws none. The buffer holds a still frame, which Lisp redraws only when the size of a window showing it changes; each cell's colour is the kind's, drawn in a face like the one the evaluator laid the block out in. Every window of the selected frame that shows the buffer has its own animation, whichever is selected or where its point is; the block moves only while Lisp says it may (`rlisp/romacs-animate.el`: shown in the selected frame, the frame focused, not paused, `romacs-animate-motion-p`) and the native window has focus and is not hidden; it stops at once and resumes without catching up. A display on a software rasterizer (every headless harness and GUI test) draws none unless `ROMACS_LAYER_ANIMATION` is 1 (`romacs--display-animates`), so screenshots and parity do not depend on frame timing. The Romacs logo (`crates/romacs-logo`, `romacs--logo-frame`: the mark and wordmark in braille cells, a sweep of light, drifting dots) is the one kind; the *Romacs* landing (GUI-STARTUP1) its first user. `crates/romacs-layout2/tests/text_animation.rs`, `render_thread::text_animations` tests, `crates/romacs-logo` tests, `harness/landing_anim_live.py` (the path forced on a software rasterizer; `ROMACS_HARNESS_WLR_ENV` runs it on a GPU, with split windows) | **User-observable** only: the block's pixels show frames its buffer text does not hold; Lisp reading the buffer sees the still frame | Deliberate design: animation as frozen data drawn off the evaluator, so editing never notices it and a hidden window costs nothing | <!-- symbols: romacs-animate--elements romacs--display-animates romacs--logo-frame romacs-animate-start -->
| VM2-OOB-DEFAULTS | Interactive sessions turn on built-in modes and options GNU leaves off, by deliberate default | GNU's standard values: `recentf-auto-cleanup` `mode`, `repeat-echo-function` `repeat-echo-message`, `electric-pair-mode`, `savehist-mode`, `save-place-mode`, `recentf-mode`, `repeat-mode`, `global-auto-revert-mode`, `column-number-mode` and `which-key-mode` off (which-key in a side window); no line numbers, `display-line-numbers-type` `t`; backups beside each file and auto-saves in its directory (`backup-directory-alist`, `auto-save-file-name-transforms` keeping only remote files in `temporary-file-directory`); `save-interprogram-paste-before-kill`, `kill-do-not-save-duplicates`, `read-extended-command-predicate`, `imenu-auto-rescan`, `imenu-flatten` and `use-package-enable-imenu-support` nil; `treesit-enabled-modes` nil, so files open in the regex modes | An interactive `--loadup` session without `-Q` turns `romacs-defaults-mode` on before the user's init (`rlisp/romacs-defaults.el`, `startup.rs` `before_init`): every entry of `romacs-defaults-settings` becomes its option's standard value and is set through its :set, as `setopt` does, the modes the first frame does not need and that turn off harmlessly before they were ever on (`savehist-mode`, `repeat-mode`, `global-auto-revert-mode`) at the first idle moment or before the first command, whichever comes first (so the first prompt already has the saved history), unless the init turned that mode on or off itself: those modes on; `display-line-numbers-type` `relative` and, through `romacs-defaults--line-numbers` on `prog-mode-hook` (which the switch adds and takes away, leaving the user's own entries), line numbers in every programming-mode buffer, not in text, Org, terminal or special buffers; `use-package` forms in a Lisp buffer's imenu under "Packages" (`use-package-enable-imenu-support`; the world holds `use-package-core`, whose `use-package` theme for `:custom-face` is a settings theme and leaves the stock theme enabled); which-key's popup in a layer over the frame (`which-key-popup-type` `custom` with `romacs-layer-which-key-*`), where the window system has layers; backups in `user-emacs-directory`'s `backups/`; GNU's remote auto-save rule, then every other file's auto-save in `auto-save/` there (made when the mode turns on); the five options above t, `command-completion-default-include-p`, t and `prefix`; `treesit-enabled-modes` the ts modes whose grammars Romacs ships (VM2-TREESIT-BUNDLED: bash, c, c++, c-or-c++, css, go, js, json, mhtml, python, rust, toml, tsx, typescript and yaml ts modes; markdown-ts-mode not yet), through its :set, so `major-mode-remap-alist` sends their regex modes to them (`treesit` is in the world with the other libraries); `recentf-auto-cleanup` 300, so the recent-files list is cleaned up when idle, not at startup; `repeat-echo-function` `repeat-echo-mode-line`, so a pending repeat shows in the mode line, not as a message (a toast). The libraries are in the GUI's world, loaded with the session's definitions, and `savehist-file`, `save-place-file` and `recentf-save-file` are computed again in each session from its per-user directory. `-Q`, batch and noninteractive runs keep GNU's values. `(romacs-defaults-mode -1)` gives each option its GNU standard value and value back, except one whose value is no longer Romacs's. Parity harnesses, the bench's real-init profile and perfbench's GUI sessions turn the mode off in Romacs (`parity_live.ROMACS_DEFAULTS_OFF`); the bench's stock profile runs `-Q`. Delete-selection was already on in every Romacs GUI session (`rlisp/romacs-editing.el`) and is not part of the switch. `tests/oob_defaults.rs` | **User-observable**: Rust, Python, C and C++, JavaScript, TypeScript, JSON, TOML, YAML, Go, shell, CSS and HTML files open in their tree-sitter modes; typing an opening delimiter inserts its pair; minibuffer history, recent files and places in files persist across sessions in `user-emacs-directory`; C-x o o repeats; prefix keys show their bindings in a layer after a pause; visited files revert when they change on disk and are unmodified; no `foo~` or `#foo#` files beside edited files; M-x hides commands for other modes. To restore GNU's: `(romacs-defaults-mode -1)` in the init, or override any one setting as a GNU default | Deliberate Romacs default: built-in essentials on out of the box, so a new user needs no configuration and no package for them | <!-- symbols: romacs-defaults-mode romacs-defaults-settings romacs-defaults-start -->
| VM2-MINI-COMPLETION-LAYER | `romacs-complete-mode`: the minibuffer and its candidates in a layer, no minibuffer line | A frame with a minibuffer keeps the minibuffer window as its bottom line: the root window ends above it, `resize-mini-windows` grows it by taking lines from the root windows (running `window-size-change-functions`), and vertico or fido-vertical show their candidates in its overlays, so the root windows shrink while a prompt is open | With `romacs-complete-mode` on (on by default on a window system, VM2-COMPLETE-DEFAULT; `rlisp/romacs-complete.el`, a port of vertico c60d802 with vertico-sort and vertico-directory, provenance in the file), a child frame hosts the minibuffer window (`romacs--host-minibuffer`, `romacs--minibuffer-host`) and the frame reserves no line for it: the root window is the frame's whole height and never resizes for a prompt. The minibuffer stays GNU's buffer and window (`minibuffer-window`, `active-minibuffer-window`, `minibuffer-contents`, `minibuffer-prompt-end`, point, `minibuffer-setup-hook`, `minibuffer-exit-hook`, recursive minibuffers, history); `window-frame` of the minibuffer window is still the frame it serves, and its edges are where the layer draws it, in that frame's coordinates. The layer is one panel, whatever its position: centred across the frame, 42% of its width (`romacs-complete-center-width`, at least 60 columns, at most the frame's width), input on top, a hairline, then the candidates, with the same keys; `romacs-complete-position` only moves it vertically: `top`, by default, near the frame's top; `center`, centred; `bottom`, near the bottom, its far edge a line above the mode line of the window the minibuffer was entered from, or above `romacs-chrome-bottom` (the status bar) when that window is elsewhere; it is centred on the frame's native width and placed in its native pixels, outside the internal border. It is placed at its tallest (`romacs-complete-count` rows), so its input stays put and it grows downward. It shows while a minibuffer is active and is hidden otherwise; it is glass (`romacs-layer-glass`: rounded, padded, on `romacs-layer-glass`'s background, the default background lifted 8% toward the foreground unless the theme sets it, glowing in `romacs-layer-glow`, casting a soft shadow; the display reads both faces as it paints, and a toast's background is `romacs-layer-toast`'s) and its rows look like a picker's (Zed's file finder, ideas and values only): the selected row a subtle tinted band, rounded and inset, which the host draws and glides (`romacs-layer-selection`), matched characters in the accent (`romacs-complete-match`, in place of the completion style's faces), a file candidate shown as its name then its directory, muted (`romacs-complete-directory`; RET and TAB take the candidate itself), and the prompt's label and count receding (`romacs-complete-prompt`); no footer of key hints yet; Zed's 4px row padding is not drawn, since layout does not apply `line-spacing` yet; a minibuffer that grows (`resize-mini-windows`) grows the layer downward. `completing-read` shows vertico's candidates in the layer's own window, 12 rows (`romacs-complete-count`), from `completion-all-completions` with `completion-styles` and the reader's own predicate; keys as vertico's (C-n/C-p, M-n/M-p history, TAB inserts, RET exits or enters a directory, M-RET exits with the input, DEL deletes a directory in a file prompt). Turning the mode on turns `fido-mode`, `fido-vertical-mode` and `icomplete-mode` off; enabling icomplete afterwards gives icomplete the minibuffer, still in the layer. `tests/child_frames.rs` (`a_hosted_minibuffer_takes_no_line_of_the_frame`); `harness/completion_live.py` (`--fido` for fido-vertical in the layer) | **Package-observable** (with the mode on): code assuming the minibuffer window sits at the frame's bottom edge gets these answers. `frame-root-window`'s height is the frame's height and `window-pixel-top` of the minibuffer window is the layer's place, so the root window's and the minibuffer's edges overlap instead of tiling the frame; `window-in-direction` with MINIBUF finds the minibuffer only where its layer is. `fit-window-to-buffer` never resized the minibuffer window (it is not a combined window in GNU either) and fits other windows as before, with the frame's last line now theirs. `window-at` is not provided by Romacs (with or without the mode). `posn-at-point` in the minibuffer is relative to its window, as in GNU. `window-size-change-functions` never ran for minibuffer growth in Romacs and no window size changes for a prompt now. While the layer shows it covers the lines of the windows under it, never the selected window's mode line in the `bottom` position nor a message toast, which shows above it | Deliberate Romacs UI: completion and prompts as a layer over the frame, so windows and terminals never resize for a prompt | <!-- symbols: romacs-complete-mode, romacs--host-minibuffer, romacs--minibuffer-host, minibuffer-window, frame-root-window, fit-window-to-buffer, window-at, posn-at-point -->
| VM2-COMPLETE-DEFAULT | Interactive sessions show the minibuffer and completion in a layer, by deliberate default | GNU's interactive default displays `completing-read` in the minibuffer window with `*Completions*` on TAB; Romacs's defaults file used to turn on `fido-vertical-mode`, which grows the mini-window | An interactive `--loadup` session makes `romacs-complete-mode` t a standard value before the user's init (`rlisp/romacs-completion-defaults.el`, in place of `fido-vertical-mode`): prompts and their candidates show in a layer (VM2-MINI-COMPLETION-LAYER) and no window or terminal resizes for a prompt. Without a window system (a layer is a child frame) or where the mode is absent (GNU loading the file for a parity harness) the file turns `fido-vertical-mode` on as before. Batch and noninteractive runs never load the file. A user init overrides it: `(romacs-complete-mode -1)` returns the minibuffer line, and `(fido-vertical-mode 1)` with the mode left on gives icomplete's display inside the layer. Parity harnesses that compare with GNU pin GNU's fido-vertical state in both engines (`parity_live.GNU_COMPLETION`). `harness/completion_live.py` (and `--fido`), `harness/completion_perkey_live.py` | **User-observable**: every prompt is a layer over the frame; packages observe VM2-MINI-COMPLETION-LAYER's answers by default | Deliberate Romacs default: completion as a layer over the frame; per-key cost measured within its budget on the 5k-file fixture (C-x C-f, see the commit) | <!-- symbols: romacs-complete-mode fido-vertical-mode -->
| VM2-LAYER-ANIMATION | Layers animate on the host | GNU shows, moves, resizes and deletes child frames at once; a toolkit may animate their windows, Emacs does not | The display animates child frames from the host frame clock, never from Lisp (`romacs-layer-animation`, `rlisp/romacs-layer.el`: t, `reduced` or nil; nil when the file is not loaded): a layer fades in over 140ms (ease-out cubic) with an 8px slide from the edge it is anchored at (its `romacs-layer-edge` parameter), fades out over 90ms, tweens its rect over 80ms when its size changes, its content placed where it settles and clipped to the moving rect, and its selection band (the row its `romacs-layer-selection` parameter names, which `romacs-complete` sets) glides between rows over 60ms; `reduced` keeps the fades without the slide. Lisp sees each layer's final geometry and state at once (`frame-position`, `frame-text-height`, `romacs-layers`); no Lisp runs for an animation frame; a key ends every animation in flight. A display on a software adapter (every headless harness and GUI test) draws none, so screenshots and parity do not depend on frame timing, unless `ROMACS_LAYER_ANIMATION=1` (`harness/layer_anim_live.py`). `tests/child_frames.rs` (layout2), `render_thread/child_frames` tests | **User-observable** only: for at most 140ms a layer's pixels lag where Lisp put it; a screenshot taken in that time shows the animation | Deliberate Romacs default (design note ui-layer §j; Zed gpui's `Animation` and easing are the concept reference, no code copied) | <!-- symbols: romacs-layer-animation romacs-layer-show -->
| VM2-ECHO-LAYER | Messages while the minibuffer is in a layer | `message` shows its text in the echo area, the minibuffer window's line, until the next command or message clears it | With `romacs-complete-mode` on (the interactive default on a window system, VM2-COMPLETE-DEFAULT) the echo area has no line of its own: the host still writes each message into the minibuffer window's buffer (`current-message`, `*Messages*` and `message-log-max` unchanged) and runs `romacs-echo-area-functions` with it, or with nil when the echo area clears; the mode shows each new message as a toast (`romacs-layer-toast`, `rlisp/romacs-layer.el`): a glass layer in the frame's top-right corner inside its internal border, never over a mode line, the status bar or the minibuffer (a toast that would reach `romacs-chrome-bottom` waits hidden), newest on top of a stack of at most `romacs-layer-toast-max` (3), each fading after `romacs-layer-toast-timeout` seconds (4); the same message again keeps the newest toast up longer, and the echo area's clearing leaves a toast to its own timeout. Prompts (`y-or-n-p`, `read-string`) read in the minibuffer's panel. In every session the host shows a message once the command returns, after timers run, and before a wait inside an evaluation (a timer's `read-event` or `sit-for`) redisplays, where GNU's `message` redisplays the echo area at once; a message a timer leaves while a minibuffer is active is not shown, and the prompt's next key clears it as GNU's does, where GNU shows it over the minibuffer until that key. `tests/gnu_completion.rs` (`romacs_complete_is_the_default_on_a_window_system_and_return_takes_the_typed_input`), `crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/timers.rs` (`idle_runs_ordinary_then_idle_timers_as_gnus_command_loop`), `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/timers_live.py` | **User-observable:** a message shows as a toast for its timeout, whatever the next command does to the echo area; a package sees `current-message` as in GNU; a timer's message during a prompt is not shown, and the prompt's next key discards it | Transient notifications instead of a reserved line (deliberate Romacs default) | <!-- symbols: message, current-message, romacs-echo-area-functions, romacs-layer-toast -->
| VM2-WRAP-WORDS | Line ends of Romacs's own text surfaces | Echo-area messages and minibuffer input continue at the window's edge (`word-wrap` nil); a window without fringes, as a child frame made with zero fringes is, shows `\` at a continued row's end and `$` at a truncated one's, unless a display table sets its `wrap` or `truncation` slot | On a window system Romacs's own text surfaces wrap at words and never show GNU's `\` or `$` (`rlisp/romacs-layer.el`): the echo area's buffer from its first message and each minibuffer as it is entered get `word-wrap` t, `truncate-lines` nil and `romacs-layer-display-table`, whose `wrap` slot is a blank and `truncation` slot an ellipsis (`…`), as do message toasts; every layer buffer without a display table of its own and the completion panel's candidates show that table. The mini-window keeps its frame's fringes, so its rows show no glyph either, and resizes to the word-wrapped rows (VM2-MINIWIN1); one the completion panel hosts keeps the panel's zero fringes, so its input aligns with the candidates and no fringe shows on the panel's background, and shows the blank wrap glyph instead; a toast is as tall as its window wraps its text, at most six rows. Batch keeps GNU's values | **User-observable:** a long message, prompt or input breaks at spaces like prose, with no `\` at its rows' ends; `word-wrap` reads t in those buffers. `crates/romacs-vm/tests/mini_window.rs` (`word_wrap_counts_the_rows_broken_at_words`), `crates/romacs-vm/src/mini_window.rs` (`rows_break_as_the_window_wraps_them`), `crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/measure.rs` (display-table line glyphs agree with the host's paint) | Prose-like messages instead of GNU's continued echo area (deliberate Romacs default) | <!-- symbols: word-wrap, buffer-display-table, romacs-layer-display-table, romacs-layer-wrap-words -->

| VM2-WTPS1 | `window-text-pixel-size` and `buffer-text-pixel-size` | GNU `window_text_pixel_size` (`src/xdisp.c:11742`) runs the display iterator (`move_it_to`, `src/xdisp.c:10928`) over the window's buffer: on batch's initial terminal frame in columns and lines (`src/term.c` `produce_glyphs`), on a GUI frame with its fonts and images | Until a host lays the frame out, which batch never does, VM2 runs a port of that iterator for a terminal frame (`src/prim/leaf/display_motion.rs`, `display_elements.rs`): `char-width-table` widths, tabs, control and escape sequences, display tables, `nobreak-char-display`, `glyphless-char-display`, invisibility and ellipses, `display` strings, spaces (`:width`, `:align-to`, `:relative-width`), margin and fringe specs, overlay strings and their `window` property, line and wrap prefixes, truncation, continuation and `word-wrap`, with GNU's FROM, TO, X-LIMIT, Y-LIMIT, MODE-LINES and IGNORE-LINE-AT-END, in units of the frame's character size. VM2 draws no tab line, so MODE-LINES adds only the header and mode lines (VM2-WIN3). It signals `VM2 window-text-pixel-size ... is unsupported` for a `(when FORM ...)` display spec with a FORM other than t or nil (the primitive cannot evaluate Lisp), `(min-width ...)`, physical width units, the `composition` property, `selective-display`, `display-line-numbers`, a horizontally scrolled window, `word-wrap-by-category`, an image line prefix, a unibyte buffer, a unibyte string with non-ASCII bytes under `unibyte-display-via-language-environment`, and strings from two overlays that share a start: GNU orders those by when its overlay tree last inserted each (`move-overlay`, or insertion before a front-advance start, reinserts), which VM2 does not record. Automatic compositions are not applied (VM2-WIDTH1). After a host has applied a layout, the same motion runs over the host's layout (`src/prim/leaf/display_hosted.rs`), in a window-system frame's pixels: within the call the VM hands the attached `LayoutHost` the display elements and the faces its snapshot paints, and the host answers each element's advance and row height from the code that paints it (`romacs-layout2` `LayoutEngine::measure`), presenting nothing. A window's snapshot publishes those same elements, as GNU's iterator delivers them (`Leaf::shown_elements`): buffer characters, `display` strings, spaces and empty replacements, invisible text left out with its ellipsis, overlay before- and after-strings, each in the face GNU merges for it (the text's `face`, through categories and `char-property-alias-alist`, under the faces of the overlays there by priority; a string's own `face` over that, face_at_string_position), with the buffer bytes each shows, so the cursor, rows and window end map back; a window showing only its characters publishes them alone. The host does not lay text on a cell grid: fallback and proportional fonts advance per glyph, faces have their own sizes, and a row is as tall as its tallest face and at least the default's; GNU's row is as tall as its glyphs' fonts, fallback fonts included (a row of CJK text 29 pixels where its face's font gives 25), so beside such text a window's start after scrolling can differ from GNU's by a row. It paints a TAB as a stretch to GNU's window-system tab stop (`tab-width` spaces of the TAB's face, the next stop when the nearer is less than a space away, `gui_produce_glyphs`; `romacs_layout_input::tab_advance`), and a `(space ...)` as GNU's stretch (`romacs_layout_input::stretch_advance`, shared with the motion: `:width` in columns of the frame's character width or `(N)` pixels, `text` the text area, `:align-to` its target from the line's start, at least a pixel unless zero is allowed, a pixel short of the last visible x on a row that continues, produce_stretch_glyph); another control character as its escape (`^X`, octal beyond ASCII, in `escape-glyph`) where a window publishes only its characters. A window whose text holds a character GNU shows as other glyphs (a control or escape sequence under `ctl-arrow` and `display-raw-bytes-as-hex`, a raw byte, a no-break character under `nobreak-char-display`, a `glyphless-char-display` character) or that has a display table mapping characters or setting its escape or control glyph publishes elements, each glyph in its own face (`escape-glyph`, `nobreak-space` or `nobreak-hyphen`, a glyph code's face, over the text's), point on the first; a glyphless character is a stretch of produce_glyphless_glyph's pixels (a thin space's one, an empty box's columns of its face's base width, a code's or acronym's halves in `glyphless-char` with the box's bars), painted blank where GNU draws its box and code. A glyph from a string shows in the string's face, without its own. It continues a row where its next glyph would pass the text area less the `\` glyph, a TAB that does not fit continuing from the area's edge, and truncates at the `$` glyph (at the text area's edge in a window with a right fringe), hiding the rest, as GNU's iterator does; under `word-wrap` (`visual-line-mode`) a row that would pass that edge breaks instead after the last run of spaces and TABs on it (a stretch or string is one by the text under it, IT_DISPLAYING_WHITESPACE), the next row starting at the first glyph after them, or at the edge where the row has no such point, as GNU's wrap points do (`romacs-layout2` `WordBreaks`). Line numbers (`display-line-numbers` t or `relative`) start every row, in the `line-number` faces, as maybe_produce_line_number makes them: the label of digits enough for the window's last row (`display-line-numbers-width` the least), blanks on a continuation row or past ZV, point's line absolute under `display-line-numbers-current-absolute`; a TAB stops and an `:align-to` aligns from after them, `text` is the area less them, and `line-number-display-width` answers them in digits, frame columns or pixels. GNU sizes the label from its desired matrix's rows, from the frame's smallest loaded font, where VM2 takes the frame's line height; a row's label longer than that estimate (lines hidden above it) still shows in a window too narrow for it, where GNU drops the number and estimates anew; it ignores `display-line-numbers-disable`, and shows no major or minor tick faces. A display table that sets only its ellipsis (Org's `org-ellipsis`) is drawn, each glyph in its glyph code's face over the text's. A stretch's `:align-to` and `text` count the line number only once the row has produced it, as calc_pixel_width_or_height does. "
"`vertical-motion`, and the recentering, scrolling and `move-to-window-line` built on it, never signal such a refusal: what the host's layout cannot measure moves as on a terminal frame, by the frame's character cells (GNU's line-based paths), and what the iterator cannot follow at all by buffer lines; `line-pixel-height` answers the frame's line height there. `pos-visible-in-window-p` and `posn-at-point` place a position a replacing display covers where pos_visible_p does (on the display line where it begins, at the first element at or after the position) and one an ellipsis shows where the character before it ends, and a string's glyph names the string in the position. Refused, not guessed, where the host does not draw what GNU does: a display table's truncation or continuation glyph code with a face of its own, where the host draws one that is a character in the default face (`with a display table's faced truncation or continuation glyphs`); a line or wrap prefix (`of a line or wrap prefix`); a space with `:height`, `:relative-height` or `:ascent` (`of a space with its own height`), a `:relative-width` space over a TAB or newline and a width in a font's units (`width`, `height`), which GNU takes from the glyph's font (`of a space of relative width`, `of a font-relative display width`); any other `:relative-width` space (Org's table separators) is FACTOR times the host's glyph of the character it covers; visual, widened or offset line numbers, and a `line-number` or `line-number-current-line` face whose digits and spaces differ in width or whose row is taller (`with visual line numbers`, `with widened or offset line numbers`, `with a line-number face of its own widths or height`); line numbers on batch's terminal frame, where GNU counts them in their width but not in the iterator's x (`with line numbers`); a display table that sets characters' glyphs or the truncation, continuation, escape or control glyph (`with a display table`); `from a vertical offset`; and, as on the terminal frame, strings from two overlays that share a start, where the window shows its characters alone. Overlay faces stack in the order `get-char-property` uses, which reads a cons `priority` as 0 where GNU's compare_overlays compares its primary and secondary priorities. A face's geometry is its merged family and size: weight and slant are not realized (the host paints them upright and regular, which Iosevka's advances share), nor is `face-remapping-alist`. The mini-window still splices its overlay strings by its own older mechanism (`src/mini_window.rs`), which these elements should replace. The last visible x is the text area, less the continuation or truncation glyph's advance in a window without a right fringe, as on a GUI frame. MODE-LINES adds the host's band: the last painted band's height, else the mode-line face's (GNU's CURRENT_MODE_LINE_HEIGHT). With no host layout attached a displayed window signals `VM2 window-text-pixel-size in a displayed window is unsupported` (`buffer-text-pixel-size` names itself) | **Package-observable** in the GUI only for the refused forms; keymap-popup's and `fit-window-to-buffer`'s plain popups and emacs-hermes's chat rule size from the host's layout. `crates/romacs-vm-gui/harness/wtps_live.py` asks pinned GNU (pgtk, no fringes) and Romacs, both in Iosevka 14, the hermes chat rule in its work buffer and in a window (`(space :width 2)`, faced text, an `:align-to right` fill, X-LIMIT, Y-LIMIT, MODE-LINES), the `(space :width text)` separator, `:align-to` columns and a tabulated list, invisible text and ellipses, overlay strings, an isearch overlay face, wrapped lines under `visual-line-mode` (`kill-visual-line`, the visual line's ends, `vertical-motion`, `posn-at-point`) and under plain continuation, truncated lines, `display` strings, empty replacements and taller overlay faces, and every answer is GNU's; `crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/measure.rs` compares the hosted answers with the host's painted glyphs, stretches, cursors, rows and band for text in several fonts, sizes, widths and `tab-width`s, and for those display elements, and each refusal with a twin that answers; `harness/lines_live.py` checks every painted glyph of a visited gnu-lisp/ file with TABs at its GNU column; `harness/popup_live.py` fits a hermes keymap popup in a native session and judges it by the painted scene. | VM2-HSCROLL1 | Horizontal scrolling | GNU `w->hscroll` and `w->min_hscroll` (`src/window.c:1278`, `:1313`, `scroll-left`/`scroll-right` `:7101`) shift a window's lines left, truncated on both sides (`init_iterator`, `display_line`, `insert_left_trunc_glyphs`); `hscroll_window_tree` (`src/xdisp.c:16625`) keeps the cursor out of `hscroll-margin` by `hscroll-step` under `auto-hscroll-mode` (`current-line` scrolling only point's row past `min_hscroll`), held after an explicit scroll until point leaves `window-old-point`; `pos_visible_p`, `buffer_posn_from_coords` and `vertical-motion` count from the scrolled column | VM2 keeps the same state per window (saved in window configurations, reset by `set-window-buffer`) and its `scroll-left`, `scroll-right`, `set-window-hscroll` and `window-old-point`. Otherwise it signals `VM2 find-composition-internal on a static composition is unsupported` or `... with automatic composition is unsupported` | **Package-observable** for combining characters, emoji and scripts in a displayed buffer: `string-glyph-split` and `find-composition` signal there | Needs the composition engine (lgstring construction) | <!-- symbols: find-composition-internal, find-composition, string-glyph-split -->

A bignum operand together with a DIVISOR signals `wrong-type-argument numberp`, and an exact quotient past 2^127 signals `overflow-error`. Existing pins in `tools/primh/evidence/round.json` and `ceiling.json` retain their recorded result, printer and signal differences. | **Package-observable** only for bignum division and astronomically large quotients | The u128 quotient covers every fixnum and float operand pair whose result is not itself a huge bignum | <!-- symbols: floor, round, ceiling, truncate -->

| VM2-BATCH2 | `emacs --batch` command line and `kill-emacs` | GNU emacs.c takes `--batch`, startup.el `command-line` the leading options (`-Q`, `-q`, `--no-site-file`, ...), then `command-line-1` runs `-L`, `-l`, `-f`, `--eval` and the rest and `(kill-emacs t)` ends the session (`lisp/startup.el:1748-1751`); an uncaught error goes to `command-error-function`, whose batch default prints it and exits 255. `Fkill_emacs` (`src/emacs.c:2953`) runs `kill-emacs-hook` (safe_run_hooks in batch), stuffs a string ARG as terminal input, re-executes Emacs for RESTART, and exits with an integer ARG's code, else success | `romacs-vm-gui [-Q] --batch ARGS...` runs the GNU-loadup chain `--loadup` shares with the batch package runner, on GNU's 80x25 batch frame, restored from its batch world (`src/batch.rs`), takes the same leading options, then GNU's own `command-line-1` over the rest, then `(kill-emacs t)`; errors reach `command-error-function` as keyboard.c cmd_error sends them. The rest of `command-line` is not run: no `site-run-file` load and no package activation (a batch session loads no init file either). `--batch RLISP_DIRECTORY` stays Romacs's stdin test runner. `kill-emacs` (`src/prims/emacs.rs`) runs the hook as safe_run_hooks in batch and through `run-hook-query-error-with-timeout` interactively, as GNU does, exits 0 for a string ARG, refuses RESTART, and without a session host (a library embedder, `romacs-gnu-ert`) signals `VM2 kill-emacs without a session host is unsupported`. The batch entry and the live GUI attach one; the GUI's drops the VM, hanging up its children as shut_down_emacs does, closes the window and exits with the status, so C-x C-c (`save-buffers-kill-emacs`) asks to save, runs `kill-emacs-query-functions` and `kill-emacs-hook` and exits as GNU does (`harness/command_errors_live.py`). A package supplying a live reference cannot split using that reference's buffer and geometry, and a package restoring deleted window identities cannot reuse those objects: the call errors before splitting. Retired VM2 window objects retain their per-window parameters; the unsupported operation is reference-based splitting and resurrection, not parameter retention. Revertible by supporting live references and reattaching eligible deleted windows and parents. |

### Planned, not yet in effect

Recorded now so they are not a surprise later. See
`milestones/01-lisp-machine/SURFACE-REMOVAL.md` in project-context.

| mechanism | GNU | planned | observable by |
|---|---|---|---|
| charset **definition** | charsets definable, aliasable, prioritisable at runtime | fixed table built at startup; queries unchanged | a package calling `define-charset-internal`, `unify-charset`, `set-charset-priority` — none in the 7-package corpus, but CJK input methods do this |
| coding-system **configuration** | priority list and aliases mutable at runtime | fixed; codecs and all 11 hot queries unchanged | a package calling `set-coding-system-priority` or `define-coding-system-alias` |
| terminal parameters | per-terminal parameter alist | removed; `terminal-live-p` kept | a package calling `terminal-parameter` |
| category **definition** | `define-category` at runtime | fixed set; `char-category-set` and `modify-category-entry` kept | a package defining a new category |

---

## Not observable by a package

Internal choices that produce identical Lisp-visible behaviour. Listed so
nobody re-litigates them.

- Plist ordering internally, where GNU's own order is unspecified.
- `reset` resolved to the default face's value, matching GNU's C.
- Dependent provider coverage invalidated immediately.
- Identical overlays given a deterministic final tie-break.
- H4: weak hash entries freed by a collection go back on the free list in slot
  order, not GNU's address-dependent bucket order.
- H5: keys whose hash reads a moving heap index are re-hashed after compaction;
  differs from GNU only for keys mutated after insertion (undefined in GNU).
- H7: `make-hash-table :size` is recorded exactly but slots are materialized on
  first use; `:size` ≥ 2^30 fails before allocating.
- Regexp matching runs a linear-time Pike VM for a forward search once its
  candidates cost more than the text justifies, and for any catastrophic
  candidate; backreferences, intervals, `??`, nullable non-greedy loops and
  POSIX patterns stay on the backtracker.  Same matches and registers as GNU's
  backtracker (`crates/romacs-regex-engine/GNU-DIVERGENCES.md`).

---

## Withdrawn

Kept visible on purpose: a corrected claim is worth more than a quietly
deleted one.

- **VM2-CVAR-PRED1 — `scroll-up-aggressively` and
  `scroll-down-aggressively` accepted any value.** Withdrawn 2026-10-30.
  Every `DEFVAR_PER_BUFFER` now checks GNU's predicate on a set: `fraction`'s
  number in [0.0, 1.0] with `wrong_range`'s error, `vertical-scroll-bar`'s and
  `overwrite-mode`'s `choice` with `wrong_choice`'s, and `integerp`,
  `stringp` and `symbolp` (romacs-vm-gui `tests/buffer_locals.rs`).

- **VM2-BOOT1, VM2-BOOT2, VM2-BOOT3, VM2-BOOT6, VM2-BOOTSTRAP1 — bootstrap
  rewrites of GNU's own Lisp.** Withdrawn 2026-10-20 (Phase 0, lane
  `vm2/oracle`). Each row described an edit made IN PLACE to a GNU file under
  `lisp/` (`byte-run.el`'s `eval-and-compile`; `subr.el`'s
  `buffer-local-restore-state`, `dolist` length guard, octal/`logior`/`setf`
  spellings; `macroexp.el`'s `cadr`), which R221 forbids: a number measured on
  an edited oracle file is not a number. GNU's files are restored byte for
  byte (`tools/lisp_oracle.py check`), so the divergences no longer exist; the
  VM gaps they hid are tickets G1-G8 in `reference/lisp-oracle/VM-GAPS.md`.
  The old row text is in git history (`git show 18e249d7:DIVERGENCES.md`).
- **VM2-EXCUR1, VM2-RESTRICT1 — a unibyte buffer with a saved excursion
  or restriction stayed unibyte.** Withdrawn 2026-10-29. Unibyte buffers
  now hold markers in an anchor tree, as text buffers do (`bytes.rs`), so
  `save-excursion` and `save-restriction` save real markers there and
  `set-buffer-multibyte` carries every marker across: converting inside the
  form answers pinned GNU's `(t 2 3 t)` (`real_ert_load.rs`), and
  `tests/unibyte_markers.rs` matches GNU on setting, moving, insertion
  types, deletion and conversion.
- **VM2-RUNHOOKS1 — `run-hooks` could not call a quoted `(lambda ...)` hook
  function.** Withdrawn 2026-10-28. The engine now calls a `(lambda ...)` list
  as GNU's funcall_lambda does (VM2-ALIAS1), so the six pinned `run-hooks`
  rows and `run-hook-with-args/0518`, `/0519`, `/0529` match GNU and are
  unpinned; `prims::t2_tests::run_hooks_calls_lambda_lists_whole` logs which
  object each hook call received.
- **D003 — `not` retains the primitive `null` value.** Withdrawn. The
  comparison used GNU *interpreted* against VM2 *compiled*. GNU byte-compiles
  `(not x)` to `Bnot` and agrees with VM2. There is no divergence.
