| VM2-ERRTEXT1 | Rendered text of an uncaught error | GNU renders an uncaught `wrong-type-argument` as `Wrong type argument: listp, 1` (`src/print.c` error printing via `print_error_message`), and `--batch` also emits a backtrace on stdout | VM2 renders the same condition as `wrong value type (expected tag 3)` on stderr with no backtrace; the exit status is identical at 255. Uncaught `void-function` and `void-variable` now render their full condition list, e.g. `(void-function missing-name)` and `(void-variable missing-name)`, rather than anonymous internal prose; their Lisp signal data matches `src/eval.c:2752` and `src/data.c:1657` respectively. GNU's human-readable messages/backtraces still differ | Narrow and NOT the usual concern: the Lisp-observable parts AGREE. Both signal symbol `wrong-type-argument` with data `(listp 1)`, measured, and a `condition-case` on `wrong-type-argument` matches under both. What differs is only the human-readable rendering, which Lisp can reach through `error-message-string` where available, plus the absent batch backtrace. A package matching on message TEXT rather than on the condition will not match; one using `condition-case` is unaffected. Revertible by formatting the message from the symbol and data rather than from the internal tag. | <!-- symbols: wrong-type-argument, error-message-string -->
| VM2-EVAL1 | Primitive arity preflight remains after argument evaluation; head-resolution order repaired | GNU `eval_sub` resolves the function head before arguments and checks subr arity before evaluating them. `(let ((x 0)) (list (condition-case e (car (setq x 1) (setq x 2)) (error e)) x))` => `((wrong-number-of-arguments car 2) 0)` at GNU pin `45b0f7699df36b3849edc3bdef37d4cb9e75b9ea` | Named call heads now resolve before operands, preserve the resolved function through operand-side redefinition and GC, and diagnose void/invalid heads using the original symbol. The 25 former MISSING pins in `eval.json` now agree with GNU and are removed after all 529 rows were independently re-recorded unchanged. Remaining arity witness => `((wrong-number-of-arguments #<subr car> 2) 2)`: primitive arity is still checked at invocation, not head preflight | **Package-observable remaining gap:** effects in operands of a wrong-arity primitive run before its signal, whose function datum is the subr rather than the called symbol. Live-GNU evidence and explicit negative row: `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/head-order/gnu.json` (`primitive-arity-before-args`). This is not counted among the 17 exact head-order rows; interpreted `eval_sub` parity is not claimed in full | <!-- symbols: eval, funcall, car -->
| VM2-EVAL2 | `eval` and macro expansions copy list structure into source | GNU `Feval` (`src/eval.c:2548`) evaluates the datum itself, and a macro's expansion is evaluated as the object it returned: quoted objects anywhere in it are `eq` to the originals, any object (closures, buffers) may appear in it — `(eval (list (lambda (x) (* x 2)) 21) t)` => 42, `eval-when-compile` (byte-run.el:674) may return a list holding a closure — a circular form is only walked as far as evaluation goes, and bare symbols in a LEXICAL alist mark local specials | A non-symbol atom, a top-level `(quote X)` and an alist-bound symbol return the identical object, and a non-symbol head or dotted argument list is diagnosed on the datum before conversion, as GNU does. Any other form, and every macro expansion, is converted to compiler source. Objects with no source spelling (closures, primitives, buffers, markers, hash tables, ...) are now carried as themselves, in `eval` data, macro expansions and lazily held bodies: they evaluate to themselves, are `eq` to the originals when quoted, and a closure or primitive in head position is called, as GNU does (this closed the `source datum required` limit that stopped macroexp.el:955; pin `eval/0509` removed). In `eval`, every `(quote X)` in the datum, and every alist value, is the identical object, so a form that rewrites the structure it quotes, as esh-cmd.el's `eshell-do-eval` does to record its progress, sees its own rewrite. Still different: in macro expansions, and outside `quote` in an `eval` datum, list cells, strings, vectors and floats are copied, so they are not `eq` to the originals (`(eq L (eval-when-compile L))` is nil, GNU t); circular forms signal `circular-list`; alist entries become fresh `let` bindings (not shared with the alist); a bare alist symbol is locally special for the form and its closures, as a file's `(defvar NAME)` is (VM2-FILESPECIAL1), so only `let` binds it dynamically, while an alist binding of the same name stays lexical and is what references read; a circular or dotted alist signals only for a symbol form, as GNU's `assq` would, while other forms then evaluate without it. LEXICAL nil compiles every binding dynamically | **Package-observable identity limits:** code that relies on `eq` of list, string or vector structure spliced into an `eval`ed form or a macro expansion (or mutates such a quoted constant expecting the original to change) differs. Splicing live objects (closures, buffers) now behaves as in GNU. The harness pins 8 printer rows (circular data) and 2 arity-data rows | <!-- symbols: eval, eval-when-compile, macroexpand -->
| VM2-EVAL3 | A repeatedly evaluated mode-line datum compiles once | GNU's mode line evaluates an `:eval` form (and `%@`'s `file-remote-p` test) with `safe_eval` at each redisplay, expanding its macros each time | The code for each such datum is cached by its structure (`cached_datum`, crates/romacs-vm/src/source_cache.rs) and reused while its guard holds: a macro it expanded is unchanged, the special-ness of each symbol it names is unchanged, and no call in it became a macro. A datum holding an object with no spelling compiles for each use. Copied constants (VM2-EVAL2) are therefore created once and shared by every evaluation of that structure, as GNU shares the datum's own objects | **Package-observable limit:** a macro whose expansion reads mutable state (not just its definition) expands once per definition here, at each redisplay in GNU. Before this, every mode-line `:eval` compiled a request per frame, and those requests forced collections while typing | <!-- symbols: format-mode-line, mode-line-format -->
| VM2-REDISPLAY2 | What tells redisplay that a window is unchanged | GNU's mutators set `windows_or_buffers_changed`, `update_mode_lines` and per-buffer redisplay flags; a redisplay with none set and point still on screen only moves or redraws the cursor (blink-cursor-mode's toggles, `try_cursor_movement`) | Each window's last full snapshot records a key (`ShownKey`, crates/romacs-vm/src/display.rs): its buffer, that buffer's modification tick (text and properties) and accessible region, an overlay-change count, start, geometry, selected window, mark, a terminal's output revision, collection count and 17 buffer-local display variables (`DISPLAY_VARIABLES`); face and font changes and `force-window-update` drop the record. With the key unchanged and point unmoved, or moved onto a row the window's last paint under the same key showed whole (VM2-RD1's rows) with no active region and no scroll margin, the window keeps its text, faces and start, no text is walked or fontified, and only its mode line is evaluated again, at every redisplay as before | **Package-observable limit:** a change that affects a window's text display through state outside the key, made with no buffer edit, overlay change, face change or `force-window-update`, shows at the next full redisplay of that window | <!-- symbols: force-window-update, internal-show-cursor, blink-cursor-mode -->
| VM2-VARALIAS1 | `defvaralias` state VM2 does not model | `Fdefvaralias` (`src/eval.c:631-726`) refuses `SYMBOL_FORWARDED` built-in C variables (`Cannot make a built-in variable an alias`), runs variable watchers (`notify_variable_watchers`) and records the alias in `load-history` (`LOADHIST_ATTACH`); the alias is part of the dumped/pdumped image | VM2 forwards only the built-in variables it defines (VM2-CVAR1): aliasing one of those, e.g. `fill-column`, now signals exactly as GNU does; aliasing any other GNU built-in still succeeds (`(defvaralias 'tab-width 'y)` => `y`, GNU signals `Cannot make a built-in variable an alias: tab-width`). `defvaralias` notifies the alias's variable watchers before it makes the alias, as GNU does; VM2 has no `load-history`, so that is not updated; a code image does not persist aliases (image load rebuilds `variable_redirects` without them). `indirect-variable` follows the chain as GNU's does; `internal-delete-indirect-variable` is absent (void-function). Every other check and effect is GNU's, in GNU's order, and 910 of 958 harness rows match exactly (48 PRINTER pins, all circular-list rows) | **Package-observable** only for code that aliases a C built-in VM2 does not define, watches variables, inspects `load-history`, or calls `indirect-variable`; ordinary `define-obsolete-variable-alias` and `defvaralias` of Lisp variables behave as in GNU | <!-- symbols: defvaralias, indirect-variable, internal-delete-indirect-variable, load-history, add-variable-watcher -->
| VM2-WATCH1 | Variable watchers of native writes run with collection held | `add-variable-watcher` (`src/data.c:1857`) accepts any function; `notify_variable_watchers` calls it before every `set`, `let`, `unlet`, `makunbound` and `defvaralias` of the variable, with WHERE the buffer of a buffer-local binding | VM2 keeps GNU's `watchers` property and `get-variable-watchers`/`remove-variable-watcher`, and calls every watcher before `set`, `set-default` and `defvar` (as `set`), `let`, `unlet`, `makunbound`, `kill-local-variable` and `defvaralias`, with WHERE the current buffer when the variable is local if set there (`local-variable-if-set-p`), and the variable untrapped while they run. It walks the live list, which `remove-variable-watcher` splices as `Fdelete` does. Bindings follow `specbind`/`do_one_unbind`: a `let` records the binding before its watchers run; restoring a localized or per-buffer default binding, or binding a per-buffer default, reports `set` with WHERE nil; restoring a global forwarded variable reports `set` then `unlet`, and its `set-default` reports twice; restoring a killed local, and `kill-local-variable` of a forwarded variable, report nothing. A native leaf subr runs in place; any other watcher is funcalled at the write point. A write from compiled code (`setq`, `let`, a `(lambda ...)` list's parameters, their unbinding) suspends its engine and roots the value, so the watcher may collect; a native writer's watchers (`set`, `set-default`, `defvaralias`, `kill-local-variable`, a load's own bindings) run with collection held until they return, because that writer's locals are not rooted (`tests/variable_watchers.rs`, `tests/gc_reentry.rs`, VM2-GC-INHIBITED). A watcher's signal aborts the write or restore and reaches its handlers once; its `throw` leaves `setq`, `let` and `set` but is refused with a VM2 error when a native primitive (`set-default`, `defvaralias`, `kill-local-variable`) made the write. A code image of a world with watchers is refused | **Package-observable**: memory a long-running Lisp watcher of a native write allocates is not reclaimed until it returns; a `throw` out of a watcher of a native write signals instead | <!-- symbols: add-variable-watcher, remove-variable-watcher, get-variable-watchers, set-buffer-redisplay -->
| VM2-FRAME1 | Initial-terminal frame parameters | `Fframe_parameters` (`src/frame.c:4083`) reports the frame's `buffer-list`/`buried-buffer-list` selection history; `modify-frame-parameters` gives `buffer-list`, `minibuffer`, `parent-frame` and colour parameters their side effects (`store_frame_param`, `update_face_from_frame_parameter`); `internal-show-cursor` hides the cursor at the next redisplay | VM2 reports GNU's computed entries for a frame without a window system (colours, `font`, `name`, size, `modeline`, menu/tab-bar lines, `visibility`), on the GUI's display with its `font`, `font-backend` and `window-system` (VM2-WIN5) but nil buffer lists, stores every `modify-frame-parameters` entry as plain data (a child frame also applies its `left`, `top`, `width`, `height`, border and `visibility`, VM2-CHILD-FRAME), and records `internal-show-cursor`'s flag (`internal-show-cursor-p` reads it) without the GUI renderer consuming it yet | **Package-observable** for code reading a frame's buffer history or relying on colour-parameter side effects; blink-cursor-mode's toggling does not reach the GUI cursor | <!-- symbols: frame-parameters, frame-parameter, modify-frame-parameters, internal-show-cursor -->
| VM2-VARALIAS2 | `defvaralias` overwrite warning is printed, not dispatched | When both variables are bound to different values GNU calls `display-warning` with `(defvaralias losing-value NEW)` and the formatted message (`src/eval.c:686-698`); in batch that prints `Warning (defvaralias): Overwriting value of ‘NEW’ by aliasing to ‘BASE’` to stderr | VM2 writes that same line to stderr directly; `display-warning` is not called, so `warning-minimum-level`, `warning-suppress-types` and a redefined `display-warning` have no effect, and an interactive session gets no `*Warnings*` buffer | **Package-observable** only through warning customisation or a `display-warning` override; the variable state after the call is GNU's | <!-- symbols: defvaralias, display-warning -->
| VM2-AUTOLOAD1 | Autoload loading: what is still not GNU's | `Fautoload_do_load` (`src/eval.c:2494`) loads FILE through `load_with_autoload_queue` (`src/eval.c:2465`) when an autoload is called, funcalled, applied (`src/eval.c:2760,3176,3530`), macroexpanded (`src/eval.c:1326`), reached as a keymap (`get_keymap`, `src/keymap.c:218`), asked for its interactive form (`src/data.c:1196`) or run as a command (`src/keyboard.c:10448`). A failed load is undone by `un_autoload` (`src/eval.c:2447`), which re-reads each redefined symbol's previous definition from its `function-history` property (`add_to_function_history`, `src/data.c:933-967`, which keeps one entry per file); `defalias` also honours `defalias-fset-function` and records `load-history`; the "failed to define" error names `(car (car load-history))`, the file as `openp` found it | Since `vm2/autoload`, calls, `funcall`, `apply`, call requests from primitives (`Step::Call`) and the compiler's macro expansion load the file exactly as GNU (macro expansion now also follows symbol aliases to a macro or macro autoload, as `Fmacroexpand` does at `src/eval.c:1290-1305`; before this lane an alias of a macro compiled as a function call), `autoload-do-load` (1 . 3) matches GNU's argument contract, a failing load reverts function cells and `features` and keeps functions that had no previous definition, nested loads undo only their own level, `require` shares the undo, and match data is saved (`crates/romacs-vm/src/autoload.rs`, tests `tests/autoload_load.rs`, GNU transcript `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/autoload-load/gnu-transcript.txt`; primh row `autoload/0497` now matches and is unpinned). Remaining: (1) VM2 has no `function-history`, so the undo record is the old definition captured at `defalias` time; GNU's re-read differs only if Lisp edits that property mid-load or the same file redefines a symbol it defined on an earlier load; (2) keymap, `interactive-form` and command-loop autoloads are not loaded (VM2 `get_keymap` returns None for an autoload); (3) no `load-history`, so the "Autoloading file %s failed to define function %s" message names the canonicalized path VM2 loaded; (4) `defalias-fset-function` is not honoured; (5) VM2 macroexpands when it compiles the whole top-level form, so a failing macro autoload fails that form's compilation instead of signalling where the call is reached (an enclosing `condition-case` in the same form cannot catch it; see VM2-LAZY4) | **Package-observable** for autoloaded keymaps and commands invoked through the command loop before their file is loaded, for code inspecting `function-history`/`load-history`, and through symlinked load paths in the error text. Calling or expanding an autoloaded function or macro behaves as in GNU | <!-- symbols: autoload, autoload-do-load, defalias, load-history, function-history, require, provide, features -->
| VM2-PLIST1 | Symbol property lists: the condition projections, `overriding-plist-environment`, VM-internal command specs, image plists | GNU stores one plist per symbol object (`u.s.plist`, `src/lisp.h`; marked with the symbol, `src/alloc.c:6673`). `get` first consults `overriding-plist-environment` (`src/fns.c:2653-2663`); `put` is `plist_put` (`src/fns.c:2703-2741`). `symbol-plist` shows every property, including the `error-conditions`/`error-message` that `define-error` puts (live: `((error-conditions (#:c error) error-message "Made") "Made")`), a condition defined only by `setplist` reports its own message (live: `(caught #:c "C!: 1")`), and `(let ((overriding-plist-environment '((foo p 9)))) (get 'foo 'p))` is `9`. Transcripts: `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/plist/gnu-transcript.txt`, `divergence-probe.txt` | VM2 matches GNU for every other key and case in that transcript, on interned and uninterned symbols alike (`crates/romacs-vm/tests/symbol_plists.rs`): identity-owned plists, any `eq` key, replace in place, append at the end, `plistp`/`circular-list`/`symbolp` signals, nil/t/keyword plists, buffer-local tables kept off the plist. It differs in four ways. (a) `error-conditions` and `error-message` live in VM2's condition registry, not on the plist: `symbol-plist` omits them and `setplist` neither installs nor removes them, so a condition defined only through `setplist` is unknown to `signal` (it is still caught by an `error` handler, as undefined conditions are, but reports `peculiar error: 1` instead of `C!: 1`), and `symbol-plist` after `define-error` is nil instead of GNU's `(error-conditions ... error-message ...)`. (b) `overriding-plist-environment` has its native nil default and dynamic binding; bulk text-property authorization consults it, but general `get` still ignores it (the probe answers nil, GNU 9). (c) A command compiled from `(interactive ...)` carries the VM-internal property `#:interactive` on its plist (GNU keeps the spec in the function object), so `symbol-plist` shows it (live: `(#:interactive (""))` for `(defun f () (interactive) 1)`, GNU nil) and `setplist` removes it. (d) Properties GNU's own C and preloaded Lisp put on symbols are absent until the code that puts them runs in VM2: in batch GNU, `(symbol-plist nil)` is `(event-symbol-element-mask (nil 0) event-symbol-elements (nil) modifier-cache ((0)))` (keyboard.c) and `(symbol-plist t)` adds `cl--class` (cl-preloaded.el); VM2 answers nil for both (primh pins `symbol-plist/0000`, `0001`, `0024`, `0025`, VALUE) | **Package-observable** for (a) to (c): code that copies or inspects whole plists (`symbol-plist`, `setplist`, `cl-remprop`-style edits) sees the registry properties missing and a stray `#:interactive`; the byte compiler's `overriding-plist-environment` bindings have no effect. Image-published plists are now private at restoration, preserving aliases through `put` (former item (d), retired by `code_image::image_alias_tests`). Before this row VM2 had no `symbol-plist`/`setplist` at all and refused `get`/`put` on uninterned symbols with `(error "symbol required")` | <!-- symbols: get, put, symbol-plist, setplist, define-error, overriding-plist-environment -->
| VM2-LAZY1 | Non-eager regime caches a body's expansion at its first call | Before `internal-macroexpand-for-load` is fbound, `readevalloop` (`src/lread.c:2183-2188`) evaluates through `eval_sub`, which expands each body form **when it is reached, on every reach, with no cache**: a macro redefined after a function's first call is used by the next call, a macro's expander runs once per reach, and an unreached form is never expanded. Oracle `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/lazy-bodies-oracle.txt`: `(f1 t)` twice expands twice; after redefining the macro `(f1 t)` => `[1]` | VM2 holds the body unexpanded (traced heap data, `crates/romacs-vm/LAZY-BODIES.md`) and expands **the whole body once, at the first call**, then runs the cached compiled code; only forms whose expansion FAILS are deferred to their reach and re-expanded on every reach until one succeeds. So: an expander runs once, at the first call, not at each reach; a macro redefined after the first call is not seen by that function; a successfully expandable form in a branch that is never taken is still expanded at the first call | **Package-observable** only through macro side effects or macro redefinition between calls of a function defined before macroexp.el is loaded, i.e. during the bootstrap chain (subr.el and anything else loaded before `emacs-lisp/macroexp`). Files loaded after macroexp.el are in GNU's eager regime, where VM2 already matches GNU's timing. Compiling per form on every reach would mean interpreting | <!-- symbols: defun, defalias, lambda, macroexpand, internal-macroexpand-for-load -->
| VM2-LAZY2 | The eager regime is VM2's compiler, not `internal-macroexpand-for-load` | Once `internal-macroexpand-for-load` is fbound, GNU **calls** it on every top-level form (`readevalloop_eager_expand_eval`), so macroexp.el's Lisp expander, its `macroexp--pending-eager-loads` `skip` protocol and cycle detection decide expansion, and a failure is the error `Eager macro-expansion failure: ERR`. `eval` never expands eagerly | VM2 only tests `fboundp internal-macroexpand-for-load` to choose the regime (once per `load`, like GNU); in the eager regime its own compiler expands the whole form, so the function is never called, `macroexp--pending-eager-loads` is not consulted, and a failure reports VM2's own text (the macro's condition text, or a native-expander message), not `Eager macro-expansion failure: ...`. `eval` follows the regime of the load that is running (eager outside any non-eager load), where GNU's `eval` is always non-eager | **Package-observable:** a redefinition of `internal-macroexpand-for-load` does not change expansion; loadup's pcase `'(skip)` dance does not make pcase.el lazy; error text of an eager failure differs; an `eval`'d definition whose body fails to expand fails at the `eval` in eager context where GNU defers it to the call | <!-- symbols: internal-macroexpand-for-load, macroexp--pending-eager-loads, eval, load -->
| VM2-SPECIAL-ALIAS1 | A symbol whose function is a special form | `eval_sub` (`src/eval.c`) dispatches on the head's function when the form is evaluated: `(defalias 'my-progn (symbol-function 'progn))` makes `(my-progn ...)` the special form, as byte-run.el's `(defalias 'inline 'progn)` makes `inline` | A call whose head names, through aliases, a special form is compiled as that form, when the form is compiled (`Vm::special_form_alias`); an alias made later, or by the same top-level form that calls it, is called as a function and signals `(invalid-function #<subr progn>)` | **Package-observable** only for a special-form alias made after the code that calls it is compiled; bytecomp's `inline` and every alias GNU's Lisp makes before use behave as in GNU (`tests/bytecomp_function_value.rs`) | <!-- symbols: inline, defalias, special-form-p -->

| VM2-LAZY4 | Reach-time and lazy-definition error shapes | In the non-eager regime GNU signals whatever the reached form signals: an unknown head such as a not-yet-loaded `setf` is `(void-function setf)` (gv.el not loaded) or gv's own error, an invalid lambda list is `(invalid-function ...)` at the call, and the interpreted closure keeps only the variables it captures once cconv is loaded | A deferred form whose failure came from a Lisp macro re-signals that macro's structured condition (matches GNU, test `reach_time_expansion_keeps_a_non_error_condition_structured`); a failure from VM2's **native** expanders (native `setf` places, malformed special forms) signals `(error "MESSAGE")` with VM2's text. The one pinned `default-boundp` harness row, `default-boundp/0034`, is exactly this | **Package-observable** for code that makes a never-set variable automatically buffer-local and then reads its default; a `defvar` with a value first (the usual pattern) behaves as in GNU | <!-- symbols: make-variable-buffer-local, default-boundp, default-value -->
| VM2-CVAR1 | Only the built-in (C) variables the bootstrap reaches exist, with C-time values | GNU defines 948 variables in C (618 `DEFVAR_LISP`, 196 `DEFVAR_BOOL`, 74 `DEFVAR_INT`, 60 `DEFVAR_PER_BUFFER`); each symbol is `SYMBOL_FORWARDED` to C storage from startup, and a dumped GNU then runs `loadup.el`'s Lisp, which fills several of them (`minibuffer-local-map` gains its bindings, `window-persistent-parameters` gains `(context . writable)`, `load-file-rep-suffixes` gains `".gz"` from jka-cmpr-hook.el) | VM2 forwards the subset declared in `crates/romacs-vm/src/forwarded.rs`, with each entry citing its DEFVAR and the census or chain need that pulled it in, with GNU's forwarding semantics for `symbol-value`, `set`/`setq`, `let` (including unwinding on a signal), `default-value`, `set-default`, `makunbound` (`Built-in variable may not be unbound`), bool coercion, int `integerp` checks and per-buffer auto-local behaviour; GNU 32.0.50 transcripts in `crates/romacs-vm/evidence/c-variables/`. Values are the C initial values, not the dumped image's: `minibuffer-local-map` is `(keymap)`, `window-persistent-parameters` is `((clone-of . t))`, `load-file-rep-suffixes` is `("")`, so `(get-load-suffixes)` is `(".so" ".elc" ".el")` where `emacs -Q` gives `(".so" ".elc" ".elc.gz" ".el" ".el.gz")` (pinned rows `get-load-suffixes/0000`, `/0002`). Other GNU built-ins are ordinary symbols: void until Lisp sets them, not special unless `defvar`ed, and any value is accepted | **Package-observable:** reading an undefined built-in signals `void-variable`; a `let` of one binds lexically under `lexical-binding`; VM2 does not look for compressed `.el.gz` files. Grows as the census pulls variables in, never speculatively (R216), except that all 60 of buffer.c's `DEFVAR_PER_BUFFER` variables exist, as buffers own them together | <!-- symbols: symbol-value, set, setq, let, default-value, set-default, makunbound, boundp, special-variable-p, get-load-suffixes, load-file-rep-suffixes, load-suffixes, minibuffer-local-map, window-persistent-parameters -->
| VM2-CVAR2 | Buffer-local bindings of non-per-buffer built-ins | `make-local-variable` / `make-variable-buffer-local` of a `DEFVAR_LISP`/`BOOL`/`INT` variable turns it into `SYMBOL_LOCALIZED` over the forwarding (`src/data.c:2178-2300`, a `blv` whose `fwd` is the C storage), and set-buffer swaps the current buffer's binding into that storage, so C reads it: `(with-temp-buffer (make-local-variable 'undo-limit) (setq undo-limit 5) (list undo-limit (default-value 'undo-limit)))` => `(5 160000)` | VM2 localizes Boolean, integer and object built-ins through the traced local-binding tables, keeping the default in the native storage: a Boolean binding stores t or nil, an integer binding signals `(wrong-type-argument integerp X)` for a non-integer, as GNU's store does. Evaluation reads the current buffer's binding of `max-lisp-eval-depth` (eshell's `setq-local`). The collector's pacing reads only the defaults of `gc-cons-threshold` and `gc-cons-percentage`; every other reader goes through variable lookup and sees the binding. Localized Boolean defaults and saved defaults accept only nil or t; noncanonical writes, or promotion while a saved default is noncanonical, signal an explicit VM2 error. GNU can retain a raw default (`(set-default 'undo-limit 'z)` in a buffer where it is local) until a buffer transition synchronizes its C storage and signals; VM2 checks it at once. Code images retain the existing refusal of localized state | **Package-observable** for a buffer-local GC threshold, raw localized integer defaults and noncanonical localized Boolean defaults. eshell's `max-lisp-eval-depth`, compilation and grep buffers' `overlay-arrow-string`, GNU help's and JSON formatting's Booleans localize as in GNU (`tests/localized_bool.rs`, romacs-vm-gui `tests/buffer_locals.rs`) | <!-- symbols: make-local-variable, make-variable-buffer-local, undo-limit, enable-recursive-minibuffers, max-lisp-eval-depth, overlay-arrow-string, gc-cons-threshold -->
| VM2-SUFFIX1 | Invalid representation suffix signals instead of crashing | GNU 32.0.50 `Fget_load_suffixes` (`src/lread.c:961`) exits 139 (SIGSEGV) for `(progn (setq load-file-rep-suffixes (list 42)) (get-load-suffixes))`; its concatenation does not check the element type | VM2's checked `concat` signals `(wrong-type-argument sequencep 42)` instead. Regression `get_load_suffixes_rejects_gnu_crashing_rep_suffix` rejects a mutant that silently returns nil | **Intentional safety divergence:** malformed input is catchable with `condition-case`, never a process crash | <!-- symbols: get-load-suffixes, load-file-rep-suffixes -->
| VM2-EMPTYSTR1 | Empty strings are not one shared object | GNU has exactly two empty strings, `empty_unibyte_string` and `empty_multibyte_string` (`src/alloc.c:1557-1562`), and every length-0 string allocation returns one of them (`src/alloc.c:2330,2359`), including `copy-sequence` of `""` and a string emptied by `delete` (`src/fns.c:2137`): `(eq (copy-sequence "") (copy-sequence ""))` is t | VM2 allocates a fresh empty string each time, so that witness is nil (true before `delete` existed). `delete` itself follows GNU exactly: it returns SEQ when nothing matched and a new string of SEQ's kind otherwise; only the identity of an emptied result differs. The one pinned `delete` harness row, `delete/0680` (`(eq r a0)` for two `(copy-sequence "")`), is exactly this | **Package-observable** only through `eq` (or `memq`/`assq`) between separately produced empty strings; `equal`, `string=`, length and multibyteness agree. A canonical empty string belongs in the allocator, not in each primitive | <!-- symbols: copy-sequence, delete, substring, concat, make-string -->
| VM2-LOADHIST1 | Load-history contents | GNU's C maintains both while it loads: `load` specbinds `current-load-list` to `(FILE)` (`loadhist_initialize`, `src/lread.c:1031-1035`), `build_load_history` (`src/lread.c:2054-2114`) moves it into `load-history`, and `load` then calls `do-after-load-evaluation` (`src/lread.c:1539-1540`); `LOADHIST_ATTACH` (`src/lisp.h:4735-4739`) conses onto `current-load-list` from `defvar` (`src/eval.c:913`), `defalias` (`src/data.c:979`), `defvaralias` (`src/eval.c:721`), `provide` and `require` (`src/fns.c:3759`, `src/fns.c:3824`). Harness rows `defvar/036` and `defvar/038`: after `(defvar current-load-list 99)` GNU's `current-load-list` is a new cons, so `(eq current-load-list old)` is nil | Romacs binds `current-load-list` to `(FILE)` for each load, with FILE the name the file was found by (expanded, not resolved through symlinks, as GNU's `hist_file_name`), replaces the file's `load-history` entry with it when the load completes, then calls `do-after-load-evaluation`; `provide` funcalls its `after-load-alist` entry (`tests/real_ert_load.rs`). `defalias` attaches `(defun . SYMBOL)` (`tests/load_history_defun.rs`), so `symbol-file` finds a function's file; variable declarations can attach entries, but `autoload`, `defvar`, `provide` and `require` do not. Backtraces therefore lack source-file cross-references from load history. `eval-buffer` and `eval-region` remain unsupported, so `eval-buffer-list` stays nil. The two rows above remain pinned VALUE | **Package-observable** for code that inspects `current-load-list` or what `load-history` records about definitions (`symbol-file`, `unload-feature`, `find-function` by load history) | <!-- symbols: after-load-alist, current-load-list, load-history, provide, require, eval-after-load, with-eval-after-load, do-after-load-evaluation -->
| VM2-LOAD1 | Loader failures outside Lisp runtime execution remain textual | GNU runtime signals escaping `load`, `require` and `autoload-do-load` retain their condition and data | Lisp runtime loading now carries the original condition, data object and selected handler across loader reentry before cleanup. Live GNU comparisons cover ordinary errors, primitive type errors, cyclic aliases, require and autoload signals; returning-handler tests also retain the same error object through GC and unwind. The Rust embedder `load_file` interface still returns textual errors, and source-read/compile failures without a runtime escape use that interface. Compile-time macro-autoload placement remains VM2-LAZY4 | **Package-observable** for source-read/compile failures; ordinary runtime signals retain their payload | <!-- symbols: load, require, autoload-do-load, condition-case, signal -->
| VM2-LOAD2 | Re-entrant `load` of a file already being loaded | `Fload` refuses a file only when it is already being loaded more than three times (`src/lread.c:1309-1314`, "Recursive load"); `Frequire` refuses a feature only when it is already being required more than three times (`src/fns.c`, "Recursive `require' for feature `%s'"). `provide` takes effect when evaluated, so a file that provides before requiring its partner (calendar.el:163 then :164) satisfies the partner's `require` mid-load; a file whose partner requires it BEFORE it provides is loaded again, nested, and that is fine in GNU. Measured (`crates/romacs-vm/evidence/autoload-load/cyclic-require.txt`): loading calendar, org, org-agenda, gnus and vc-git from source makes 892 requires with 7 re-entrant ones, 6 already satisfied by an early `provide` and ONE genuine nested reload, `gnus-sum.el` required again from `gnus-group.el` | `provide` is immediate in VM2 too, so every early-provide cycle (calendar/cal-menu, cl-lib/cl-macs, cl-lib/cl-seq) behaves as in GNU, and the late-provide cycle errors as GNU does (condition `error`, VM2's text). But VM2's loader refuses ANY re-entrant load of a file still being loaded ("recursive source load"), so a file that loads itself, and the gnus-sum/gnus-group shape, fail where GNU succeeds (fixture: GNU `(gs 2 (gg gs))`, VM2 `(error)`) | **Package-observable** for Gnus (`gnus-sum`) and any file that re-enters its own load. Org and calendar do not need it. Not changed by vm2/autoload; belongs to the loader | <!-- symbols: load, require, provide, features -->
| VM2-EMPTYSYM1 | A symbol with an empty name prints as nothing | `print_object`, Lisp_Symbol arm (`src/print.c:2496-2500`): with `print-gensym` nil (or for an interned symbol) a zero-byte name prints `##`, so `(prin1-to-string (intern ""))` and `(prin1-to-string (make-symbol ""))` are both `"##"` | VM2's printer emits the empty name verbatim, so both are `""` (and `(list (intern "") (make-symbol ""))` prints `( )`); true before `keywordp` existed (the 23deaa2a binary gives the same rows). The symbols themselves agree: `symbol-name`, `eq` and `keywordp` (nil for both, harness rows `keywordp/0026`, `/0027`). The two pinned `keywordp` harness rows, `keywordp/0071` and `/0079`, are the printer family of these two values and call no `keywordp` | **Package-observable** only when printing the empty-named symbol (`prin1`, `format "%S"`), whose GNU output `##` reads back as that symbol and VM2's empty output does not. Belongs in the printer, not in any primitive | <!-- symbols: prin1, prin1-to-string, intern, make-symbol -->

| VM2-OBARRAY1 | Initial obarray projection | GNU's initial obarray is a bucket-ordered table, counts every symbol, resets an invalid current `obarray` to the initial table before signalling, and permits removing its nil/t symbols. | VM2's initial object lazily projects the existing compiler name table. `mapatoms` uses stable slot order (custom tables use GNU bucket order), its printed count includes only subsequent explicit insertions, nil/t remain canonical, and invalid current bindings are rejected without GNU's reset. Live witnesses `evidence/obarray-substrate/divergence-probes.json`: `invalid-current-reset` is GNU t / VM2 nil; filtered initial order differs. Removing compiler-known names also retains existing name-keyed compiler/variable metadata. | **Package-observable default-table limitations**, not full initial-obarray parity. Custom obarray lookup, identity, removal, iteration and GC are independently tested. | <!-- symbols: obarrayp, intern, intern-soft, unintern, mapatoms -->
| VM2-OBARRAY2 | Non-Unicode obarray names | GNU accepts arbitrary Emacs strings, comparing both exact bytes and character count. | Custom obarrays retain exact input-name objects and compare bytes/characters, but construction still requires a Unicode-decodable name for the existing symbol-slot metadata. `(intern (unibyte-string 255) (obarray-make))` signals `not-unicode-string`; GNU returns a symbol. The initial name table also conflates equal UTF-8 bytes with differing character counts. Live transcript: `evidence/obarray-substrate/divergence-probes.json`, `raw-name`. Existing name-based constant checks described in VM2-BOOT4 can also affect custom symbols. | **Package-observable symbol-substrate boundary**; no byte coercion or invented replacement symbol is returned. | <!-- symbols: intern, intern-soft, unintern, symbol-name -->
| VM2-OBARRAY3 | Reader and image integration of obarrays | GNU's reader interns into the current `obarray`, and its dumped runtime retains the initial obarray. | VM2's existing reader still interns source names into the compiler name table: `(let ((obarray (obarray-make))) (eq (car (read-from-string "obarray-gap-x")) (intern "obarray-gap-x" obarray)))` is GNU t / VM2 nil (live transcript in `evidence/obarray-substrate/divergence-probes.json`). First-class obarrays are mutable heap objects, so the existing immutable-string/cons-only code-image publisher refuses them; this slice does not add an image codec or alter bootstrap/loadup. Constructing an evaluated call whose head is a custom symbol also reaches the existing generated-symbol compiler restriction (`generated-call-head` in the same transcript: GNU 1, VM2 `symbol required`); ordinary `(funcall SYMBOL)` does not need that source conversion. | **Package-observable integration boundaries** outside the custom-collection API. Completion can consume an explicitly supplied obarray through the leaf cursor without relying on reader redirection. | <!-- symbols: read-from-string, intern, obarray-make -->

Each detail entry carries a live-GNU transcript. Adding a divergence to a
crate file **without** adding a row here is the one thing that is not allowed.


## VM2 format-message boundary

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-FORMAT-MESSAGE1 | General formatting and initial locale capability | `format-message` has all `format` conversions and text-property/identity behavior; the initial internal quoting flag follows the process locale. | Quote selection, template-only substitution, explicit capability bindings, display-table fallback and byte8 promotion are implemented. The shared formatter supports field numbers, flags, width and precision for `%s`, `%S`, `%c`, `%d`, `%i`, `%b`, `%B`, `%o`, `%x`, `%X`, `%e`, `%f`, `%g` and `%%`, including bignum integers and float rounding. Width, precision and field-number components are capped at 2^20. Text properties propagate as in `format` (VM2-FMT1), except on the path that promotes raw octets beside Unicode text, which returns no properties; GNU string-identity behavior remains unsupported. Initial capability is the VM UTF-8 default (`t`), without host non-UTF-8 locale detection, and the capability binding is not boolean-normalized. | `tests/format_numeric.rs` compares the formatting domain with pinned GNU; the separate 64-case quoting corpus does not establish text-property, identity or host-locale parity. | <!-- symbols: format, format-message, text-quoting-style -->
| VM2-LOCATE1 | `locate-file-internal` file-name handlers and predicates | GNU `lread.c:openp` checks the suffixed candidate for a `file-exists-p` handler, then uses `file-readable-p` when selected; expansion can also dispatch `expand-file-name`. `fileio.c:find-file-name-handler` selects the latest-starting matching regexp (first on ties), respects handler `operations` and operation-specific inhibition, ignores case-fold-search, and preserves match data. | As GNU: a selected `file-exists-p` handler makes `file-readable-p` decide, and a function predicate's non-`dir-ok` verdict asks `file-directory-p`, both through Lisp, so their handlers run. | Regression coverage: `tests/locate_file_handlers.rs`; oracle pin `45b0f7699df36b3849edc3bdef37d4cb9e75b9ea`, GNU 32.0.50. | <!-- symbols: locate-file-internal, find-file-name-handler, inhibit-file-name-handlers, inhibit-file-name-operation -->

## Observable by a package: mini-window display

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-MINIWIN1 | Overlay strings and mini-window height | Redisplay shows every window's overlay `before-string` and `after-string` (xdisp.c load_overlay_strings) and resizes the mini-window to its display within `max-mini-window-height` as `resize-mini-windows` says (resize_mini_window; loadup.el sets `grow-only`). | The mini-window shows its buffer's overlay strings in load_overlay_strings' order, with their `face` properties (a face list merged as xfaces.c does) and a `cursor` property placing point, and redisplay sizes it as resize_mini_window does, including `redisplay-adhoc-scroll-in-resize-mini-windows`; the shared chain sets `grow-only` after window.el as loadup.el does. Other windows show no overlay strings yet; strings' own `display`, `invisible` and image properties are not interpreted; a tab in a string shows as spaces and other controls as `^X`; a line wider than a row counts the rows its window breaks it into, at words under `word-wrap` as GNU's wrap points do, in columns of the frame's character width between the window's fringes (its frame's, as GNU's mini-window has; a mini-window a layer hosts has the layer's, none), less, without a right fringe, the continuation glyph's advance (the display table's `wrap` glyph, else `\`; the host's advance where it lays the frame out, else its columns), a tab to its next stop from the line's start, and leaving the outermost minibuffer resizes the mini-window at once (minibuf.c read_minibuf_unwind). Rows are a uniform line high: a face whose font is taller (GNU's `help-key-binding` in `fixed-pitch`) does not make its row taller, as GNU's mixed-font rows are. | Completion UIs (icomplete, fido) display their candidates this way. Other windows' point visibility and scrolling count buffer lines (redisplay.rs), which overlay strings would invalidate, so they stay out until that counts display lines. `crates/romacs-vm/tests/mini_window.rs`; the GUI journey `crates/romacs-vm-gui/tests/gnu_completion.rs` and `harness/completion_live.py`. | <!-- symbols: resize-mini-windows, max-mini-window-height, redisplay-adhoc-scroll-in-resize-mini-windows, before-string, after-string -->

## Observable by a package: circular completion tables

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-COMPLETION-CYCLE | Circular list completion tables | GNU traverses until an exact test match, a decisive common prefix, or user quit; some circular inputs never finish. | Decisive early results are preserved. Traversal that would revisit a detected cycle signals `circular-list`. | Bounded native traversal with callback state traced across GC. `tests/completion.rs` covers both early-result cases. | <!-- symbols: try-completion, all-completions, test-completion -->
| VM2-COMPLETION-CASE | Completion case folding, string comparison and casing | GNU uses its current case tables, with nil entries meaning identity. | Original native tables retain Unicode fallback for nil entries above ASCII before GNU enrichment. Explicit non-nil entries override it; ASCII and replacement-table nil entries mean identity. | Preserve the native Unicode startup policy while respecting explicit ASCII edits and replacement tables. Range-crossing and replacement-table tests cover the boundary. | <!-- symbols: try-completion, all-completions, test-completion, compare-strings, upcase, downcase -->

## Explicit OClosure compatibility

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-OCLOSURE1 | Closure metadata and GNU generic dispatch | GNU OClosure inspects closure documentation and captured environment slots. | Ordinary closures retain a private traced documentation value. The explicit `oclosure--fix-type` marker emitted by GNU's macro retains named capture cells and exposes the supported interpreter metadata. `romacs-cl-generic` loads the owned `romacs-oclosure` type-query bridge before unchanged GNU `cl-generic`. | Compatibility callers must load the named entry after GNU core prerequisites; this is not an automatic GUI startup addition. Ordinary closure array access remains rejected. Explicit OClosure capture retention may include bindings GNU optimizes away. Existing code-image restrictions on mutable captured cells remain. Code images use VM2CODE8 to serialize private documentation. `tests/oclosure.rs` covers accessors, copying, inheritance, identity, GC, dispatch, replacement and method combination. | <!-- symbols: oclosure-type, romacs--oclosure-type, make-interpreted-closure, cl-type-of, purify-flag -->


## GNU ERT batch execution

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-ERT-BATCH1 | Explicit batch adapter and compiled backtrace metadata | ERT isolates each test with a temporary buffer and a window excursion; GNU backtraces can describe interpreter/source frames and lexical locals. | The named `romacs-batch` adapter retains the temporary buffer, assertions, error handling, cleanup, expectedness and GNU batch runner, and replaces the precautionary window excursion. Condition objects use `prin1` plus a newline instead of editor indentation; their data and expectedness are unchanged. Automatic window-layout restoration and GUI fixtures are unsupported. Backtraces report real compiled calls, original evaluated argument snapshots and dynamic locals; lexical/source locals, pre-argument named-autoload frames and debugger mutation are unavailable. GNU apply optimizations can produce different frame/argument shapes. Ordinary closures print opaquely through an owned `cl-print-object` method. | Keep the batch regime explicit and preserve private closure representation. | <!-- symbols: ert--run-test-internal, mapbacktrace, backtrace--locals, cl-print-object -->
| VM2-PROPERTY-EDIT1 | Bulk text-property edits and forward boundary queries | GNU supports arbitrary property keys, modification callbacks, byte buffers and raw interval traversal with `next-property-change` LIMIT t. | Bulk edits support interned symbol keys, mutable strings and annotated text buffers, with atomic validation, read-only checks and a changed-result flag. Unibyte buffers are edited too, and change hooks run as VM2-EDIT-HOOKS1 describes. Other key types and immutable image-string writes refuse explicitly. The two forward queries support strings and annotated text buffers; unibyte-buffer queries and raw interval LIMIT t refuse explicitly. Effective property queries retain the existing general property-resolution limits. | Reuse the native property runs and overlay precedence for the ERT printer's actual calls. | <!-- symbols: add-text-properties, next-property-change, next-single-char-property-change -->

## Observable by a user: workspace file coding

| # | Scope | GNU | VM2 | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-WORKSPACE-CODING | File coding in the default workspace (no loadup) | GNU's image has all of mule-conf.el and the language environments; `set-locale-environment` for a UTF-8 locale on GNU/Linux puts `utf-8` first in `coding-system-priority-list` and makes the default `buffer-file-coding-system` and `default-file-name-coding-system` `utf-8-unix`. Visiting also consults `set-auto-coding-function` (`set-auto-coding`: `auto-coding-alist`, `auto-coding-regexp-alist`, `coding:` cookies, `auto-coding-functions`) and `file-coding-system-alist`; saving consults `select-safe-coding-system-function` (`select-safe-coding-system`, which asks through `select-safe-coding-system-interactively` when no default coding system encodes the text, and warns and asks before writing under a cookie that names an unknown coding system), and `basic-save-buffer-2` keeps `last-coding-system-used` as `buffer-file-coding-system`. | Startup (`initialize_coding`, `crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/startup.rs`) evaluates verbatim from the pinned mirror mule.el's `define-coding-system`, `coding-system-type`, `buffer-file-coding-system-explicit`, `after-insert-file-set-coding` and `find-new-buffer-file-coding-system`, and mule-conf.el's fundamental aliases, `prefer-utf-8`, `raw-text`, `no-conversion-multibyte`, `iso-latin-1` (`iso-8859-1`, `latin-1`), the `utf-8`, `utf-8-with-signature`, `utf-8-auto` and `utf-8-emacs` definitions with their aliases, and the `latin-extra-code-table` entries. It also evaluates mule.el's auto-coding machinery (`auto-coding-alist`, `auto-coding-regexp-alist`, `auto-coding-functions` with `sgml-xml-auto-coding-function` and `sgml-html-meta-auto-coding-function`, `find-auto-coding`, `set-auto-coding`, `coding-system-get`, `coding-system-category`, `coding-system-equal`) and its `(setq set-auto-coding-function ...)`, and mule-cmds.el's `select-safe-coding-system` with `find-coding-systems-region`, `sort-coding-systems`, `coding-system-change-eol-conversion`, `coding-system-change-text-conversion`, `get-language-info` (`language-info-alist` nil, `current-language-environment` "English") and its `(setq select-safe-coding-system-function ...)`, together with the helpers they call from files.el (`set-auto-mode-1`, `inhibit-local-variables-p`, `file-name-sans-versions`, `find-file-literally`), subr.el (`caar`, `ignore-errors`, `string-match-p`, `ensure-list`), byte-run.el (`defsubst` and its `eval-and-compile`, `function-put`, `byte-run--set-speed`) and mule-util.el (`with-coding-priority`). `rlisp/romacs-coding.el` then sets GNU's UTF-8-locale result restricted to those systems: priority `utf-8 iso-latin-1 raw-text utf-8-auto utf-8-with-signature undecided` and the `utf-8-unix` defaults; it assumes a UTF-8 locale. Its one adaptation replaces the question: the workspace cannot show GNU's *Warning* explanation (temp-buffer display, help buttons, fill), so `select-safe-coding-system-interactively` signals `romacs-unsafe-coding` with the unsafe defaults and the safe systems GNU would offer; `write-region` has not opened the file, which keeps its bytes, and the buffer stays modified. The workspace save (`romacs-gui-write-visited-buffer`, `rlisp/romacs-startup.el`) keeps `last-coding-system-used` as GNU's `basic-save-buffer-1` and `-2` do. No other coding system is defined (ISO-2022, UTF-16, CJK, other ISO-8859 sets), and `file-coding-system-alist` stays nil. | UTF-8 (LF and CRLF), ASCII and Latin-1 files (with or without a cookie, agreeing with the bytes or not) and a UTF-8 file with a BOM visit, edit and save as pinned GNU does: decoded text, `last-coding-system-used`, `buffer-file-coding-system` and bytes on disk, including λ typed into a Latin-1 file (saved as UTF-8) and text typed before a BOM (kept after the signature) (`crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/command.rs` `coding_journey_visits_edits_and_saves_as_pinned_gnu`); where pinned GNU asks (λ under a `coding: latin-1` cookie) C-x C-s signals `romacs-unsafe-coding` with GNU's unsafe defaults and its safe systems that the workspace defines, before writing (`coding_save_refuses_where_pinned_gnu_would_ask`). **User-observable**: (1) where GNU asks which coding system to save with, the workspace refuses the save; (2) a cookie or signature naming a coding system the workspace does not define (e.g. `coding: euc-jp`, a UTF-16 BOM) is ignored by the visit, which detection decides, and saving then signals `void-function display-warning` before writing where GNU warns and asks `Really proceed with writing?` (warnings.el is absent); (3) `detect-coding-region` is not implemented, so GNU's `sgml-xml-auto-coding-function` fails inside its `ignore-errors` for an XML declaration without `encoding=`, and detection decides (`undecided-unix` for ASCII XML where GNU names `utf-8-unix`); (4) bytes only an undefined coding detects (C1 bytes outside `latin-extra-code-table`, which GNU decodes as `japanese-shift-jis`; UTF-16; ISO-2022) refuse the visit (`raw bytes into a multibyte buffer` or a detector refusal), leaving the file untouched. | <!-- symbols: buffer-file-coding-system, last-coding-system-used, set-coding-system-priority, define-coding-system, after-insert-file-set-coding, default-file-name-coding-system, latin-extra-code-table, set-auto-coding, find-auto-coding, select-safe-coding-system, select-safe-coding-system-interactively, romacs-unsafe-coding -->

## World snapshots

| # | Mechanism | GNU | Romacs | Boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VM2-WORLD1 | `emacs-build-time` in a VM restored from a world | A dumped GNU reports the time its dump was made: `version.el:79-84` runs in loadup, and pdumper restores its value without evaluating it again. | A VM booted from source reports its own clock at `version.el`; one restored from a world reports the time the world was built, as a dumped GNU does. | **Package-observable** only through that one value: every other value a restored VM holds is a function of the binary, the recipe and its keyed host facts, or is rebuilt (`romacs-world-restart`). gnu-tests/tools/world-diff-allow.txt lists it. | <!-- symbols: emacs-build-time -->
| VM2-WORLD2 | Where a process booted from a world keeps its heap | GNU restores its dump at any address: pdumper relocates every pointer. | A world image is the heap of the process that built it, mapped back at its own addresses (`world::arena`): the arena's address is random per world, not per process. Everything else a world process maps keeps address randomization, the executable too. The image addresses only read-only data of the builder's executable (literals, constant tables, vtables); a restored process maps a read-only copy of those segments where the builder had them and leaves the rest of that range inaccessible, so an image holds no code address that could be followed. | Not package-observable. Someone who can read the world store learns where a world process keeps its heap; `ROMACS_WORLD=off` boots from source, with a heap at a per-process random address. The store defends against accidental change, not against a writer with the user's own permissions: a world is refused and rebuilt after a truncated or partly written image, an interrupted build, an upgraded binary or library (keyed by content and by each library's build-id and mapped constant bytes), and corruption that changes a file's identity or times. It deliberately does not detect a same-user writer, who can already edit the user's init file, the store's images and the binaries themselves (one can, say, record an old digest under a damaged image's metadata); bit rot that leaves size and times unchanged, since the image and executable digests are cached by file identity; or writes to a mapped image or library after it was checked. | <!-- symbols: -->
| VM2-WORLD3 | The libraries a user's init loads, in a VM restored from a world | GNU dumps only its own preloaded Lisp; every library the init requires loads from source or bytecode in each session, and its load-time `defcustom`, `defvar` and `defconst` forms evaluate in that process (`executable-find`, `locate-user-emacs-file`, `(getenv ...)`). | A live `--loadup` launch that loads the init restores the init's layer when the store holds one (`romacs_vm::world::layer`, `crates/romacs-vm-gui/src/world.rs`): the base world plus the libraries the init's recorded plan loads, replayed after the chain under each entry's recorded `load-path`. The plan is the `require` and autoload calls the init itself issued, recorded while it ran; a launch without a current layer boots from the base world, records the plan again and builds the layer ahead of the next launch. A layer's definition form that reads a per-process fact is kept and evaluated again at restore (`romacs--world-reseed-forms`: a `defcustom` through `custom-reevaluate-setting`, the others by their value form), as GNU's delayed init re-evaluates its dumped options; a definition a refused effect cuts short (a `make-directory`) is deferred whole. Any other form's such read, an effect, a signal or a timer left behind refuses its entry, which then loads from source as before. The startup function runs again in the builder after the libraries, as a restored process runs it after them, and must compute what it computed before them. | **Package-observable** in four ways. A library's load-time code sees the standard value of a variable the init sets before requiring it, since the layer loaded the library first (the init's own `setq` still applies after). A hook function a library adds when it loads precedes, in `add-hook`'s order, the ones the init adds, where a source session has the init's first (`kill-emacs-hook`: the init's savers run before org-persist's). A library's load-time code runs with the chain's initial terminal, not the session's display: `window-system` and `display-graphic-p` answer nil there, where a source session's library finds the pgtk frame (`noninteractive` is nil in both). A `require` the init issues only under a condition that differs between sessions is replayed from the previous session's plan: the layer may hold a library this session's init would not have loaded; the next launch plans afresh. A library edit, a package update, a changed `load-path`, an edited init, another build or another base world rebuilds or bypasses the layer: every file a layer build read is a keyed record of its manifest, the plan's digest and the base world's key are in the layer's key, and the init's content digest is checked at the launch. `ROMACS_WORLD=off` boots from source. | <!-- symbols: require, autoload, custom-reevaluate-setting, load-path -->
